# Lookout tower: TD note

Author: t-td (technical-director), 2026-10-10. Spec: planning-artifacts/poc-spec-washed-ashore-lookout-tower.md.
ADR: studio:engineering `adr/tower-1-climb-zones-interaction-pipeline`.

**Status: DESIGN GATE RULED 2026-10-10 (section 6; far view in section 7, numbers pending a measured slot).** Sections 2-4 are the early ruling, issued before tower-design-spec.md arrived, because the geometry leaves one viable
traversal family. Where they disagree with section 6, section 6 wins. Code-review findings and the final "as built" sections are added later.
Feature code may land in Assets/ once the spec revision meets the section 6 conditions; the far-view component waits for section 7 numbers.

## 1. Facts the ruling rests on (read or measured 2026-10-10)

| Fact | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Player capsule | radius 0.40, height 1.80, skinWidth 0.05, slopeLimit 45, stepOffset 0.4; eye 1.65; camera near 0.1, far 1000, FOV 70 | _bmad-output/poc/pipeline-scripts/setup_player.cs:23-31 |
| Walk / sprint / jump | 5 / 8 m/s, jump 1.2 m, gravity -20, grounded stick -2 m/s | Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/PlayerController.cs:21-31 |
| Stair width | 0.61 m between stringers. The capsule is 0.80 m wide (0.90 with skin) | mc39 digest; setup_player.cs |
| Upper landings | triangles with legs of about 0.94 m and 0.64 m: inscribed circle about 0.28 m, smaller than the capsule radius | mc39 digest (USFS-T1004) |
| Cab | 2.13 m square inside, pedestal in the centre | spec T1, C3 |
| Top flight | 8 in 12 (33.7 deg), not 52 deg; all other flights 12 in 9.25 | mc39 digest (USFS-T1006) |
| Fog | linear 120-520 m, colour measured from the sky at the horizon; guarded by SkyBuildTests | Assets/Editor/Level/BellsBendSky.cs:25 |
| Tree cull | 450 m | Assets/Editor/Art/BellsBendPerf.cs:7 |
| URP | LOD cross-fade on (dither), MSAA off, in both PC and Mobile assets | Assets/Settings/*_RPAsset.asset |
| Render queues | water Transparent, fish body +10, fish ring +20 | the three shaders |
| Gamepad South | already bound to Jump | PlayerController.cs:76 |
| Layers | 4 Water, 8 Birds, 9 WorldBounds ("every invisible player-only boundary collider"); SightMask leaves out 4 and 9 | Assets/Scripts/World/WorldLayers.cs |
| Blender 5.2.2 FBX | object ids come from Python `hash(key)`; `blender -b` runs with hash randomisation ON; the hash of one string differed between two runs. With `PYTHONHASHSEED=0` plus `--python-use-system-env` it repeated; the env var alone is ignored | io_scene_fbx/fbx_utils.py:834; probed |
| FBX time fields | only FBXHeaderExtension/CreationTimeStamp (7 int32) uses now(). Top-level CreationTime and FileId are constants in the Blender writer | export_fbx_bin.py:3235-3244; encode_bin.py:29-30 |

Consequences, all arithmetic:
- The capsule cannot pass between the real rails, stand on the upper landings, or walk a ring around a pedestal wider than 0.23 m. **The collision set must be a purpose-built walk envelope, not the visual mesh.** T1 measures render meshes only.
- An upright 1.8 m capsule riding a slope through a floor needs an opening of 0.9 + 1.8 / tan(pitch) along the run: 2.3 m at 52 deg, 3.6 m at 33.7 deg. The cab is 2.13 m. **The hatch passage has to be near vertical** (0.9 m clear at 90 deg, 1.2 m at 80 deg).
- At walk speed a 52 deg surface is climbed in under 15 s and descended airborne (5 m/s needs 6.4 m/s down against a 2 m/s stick). The 60-120 s in C1 needs about 0.35-0.7 m/s along the path. **A stair-specific movement rule is needed whatever the collider shape.**
- From 1 km the tower is past the fog end (invisible with scene fog) and on the far plane (1000 m), where the hill under it is clipped.

## 2. Ruling (a): stair traversal

**Technique: analytic climb zones.** A third movement mode beside walk and swim, built like the water: decided every frame from position, pure rules, a tuning asset, no stale state.

1. `ClimbZone` (data only): an oriented box plus a walk profile, a polyline of (distance, height) along one heading. Pitch 0-90 deg, so the same type serves flights, the hatch passage and later ladders.
2. While the feet point is inside a zone and the player is Dry, mode = Climb: no gravity; velocity = profile tangent x (input direction . zone heading) x climb speed, plus a small lateral term, plus a follow term that brings the feet to the profile. W while facing up the flight climbs; turn round and W descends; S backs down; stopping holds position. Look is never touched.
3. Movement still goes through `CharacterController.Move`, so walls, a closed hatch and landings stay physical.
4. **Nothing solid lies on the profile.** The capsule touches only side walls and end floors. An invisible catch proxy sits parallel below each inclined run as a fall arrest; the scripted climb must record zero contacts with it.
5. Each flight profile ends in a flat run-out at landing height, and the solid landing floor starts only after it (set back by radius + skin + play). Without that the side of the capsule hits the landing edge 0.58 m below its top, above stepOffset.
6. Jump is gated off in Climb (same pattern as `SwimRules.CanJump`). Containment must also hold by geometry with jump enabled on landings and in the cab.
7. CharacterController radius, height, slopeLimit, stepOffset and skinWidth are never written at runtime. With no zone in range `Move()` runs the existing branch unchanged; the out-of-range cost is one distance check.
8. Climb state clears on Respawn, on any snap, and when the zone is disabled. Statics reset in a SubsystemRegistration hook.
9. `ClimbTuning.Validate()` (pattern: SwimTuning) rejects numbers that break the rule; tests read the tuning asset and the targets file, not literals.

Rejected:
- Stepped tread colliders with stepOffset: frame-rate dependent, 0.23 m camera stutter, airborne on descent.
- Raising slopeLimit globally, or shrinking the capsule: changes cut banks, bluffs, bank exits and the water footprint (P4, W6, W8).
- Changing radius or slopeLimit inside a trigger: restoring them next to walls is a launch risk; trigger enter/exit goes stale on respawn.
- A spline rail with the controller disabled: collisions stop (hatch, landings) and entry snaps.
- Fallback that needs my sign-off first: real ramp colliders with a per-frame derived slopeLimit. Only if a prototype passes the same tests.

Acceptance tests I will look for in review:
- EditMode: the rules as pure functions (direction mapping, speed, profile height, validation).
- EditMode: sweep the capsule along every profile and around each landing and the cab ring with `Physics.CheckCapsule`: zero overlaps with solids; headroom and widths read from the parameter table.
- EditMode: the capsule numbers in the parameter table equal the CharacterController on the Player in World.unity.
- PlayMode: full climb and descent at `Time.captureDeltaTime` 1/30 and 1/120, both inside the time target.
- PlayMode: C2 stress with jump, sprint and strafe on every flight and landing: 0 escapes, 0 stuck, 0 catch contacts.
- PlayMode: controller properties equal before and after; a world walk with the zones present matches the baseline.

### Collision proxies the Blender script exports (t-artist)

All derived from the parameter table, which carries the capsule numbers (radius, height, skin) and `play` (0.05 m).

| Prefix | Becomes | Layer | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `COL_BOX_<Part>_<nn>` | BoxCollider fitted to the 8 vertices | Default | Visible solids: pads, legs and bottom-panel members up to 3.1 m above any walkable floor, landing floors, cab floor, cab lower walls, flat cab ceiling at eaves height, pedestal column |
| `COL_CVX_<Part>_<nn>` | Convex MeshCollider | Default | Triangular landing floors and other non-box solids |
| `CLIP_BOX_` / `CLIP_CVX_` | Same, no renderer | WorldBounds (9) | Invisible containment: corridor walls, landing and cab perimeter walls, catch proxies (`CLIP_BOX_Catch_F<nn>`) |
| `MARK_Front`, `MARK_Up` | Empty transforms | - | Axis test: +Z and +Y after import |
| `mc39_walk.json` (sidecar) | ClimbZones | - | One entry per flight and one for the hatch: profile points in tower-local metres (Unity axes), heading, half-width, box height |

Tolerances:
- BOX: exactly 8 vertices and 12 triangles, edges orthogonal within 1e-4 m, or the import fails. CVX: at most 32 vertices, convex within 1 mm. No concave MeshCollider and no collider on render meshes. At most 150 colliders in total.
- Corridor: CLIP wall faces at +/-(radius + skin + play) = 0.50 m from the flight centre line, from the profile up to the next solid above or 3.1 m (jump 1.2 + height 1.8 + 0.1).
- Profile: through the visual tread nosings within 0.02 m; ends level with the landing floor within 0.01 m; run-out at least radius + skin + play.
- Catch proxy: parallel to the incline, 0.20 m below it.
- Landings: collision floor and walls sized so a circle of radius + skin + play can turn from one corridor mouth to the next. It may overhang the visual planks.
- Hatch: clear opening at least 2 x (radius + skin + play) = 1.00 m both ways; passage pitch at least 80 deg.
- Cab: pedestal solid at most 0.23 m wide; perimeter CLIP walls full height whatever the sashes do.
- No solid inside the swept walk envelope; headroom at least 1.85 m everywhere on it. The build fails on a violation and prints the minimum clearance.

## 3. Ruling (b): interaction system

1. **Where.** Contract, `Interactor`, `InteractionTuning` and the prompt view: `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Interaction/`, namespace `WashedAshore.Gameplay.Interaction`, in the existing WashedAshore.Gameplay asmdef. Climb code: `Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Climb/` (`PlayerController.Climb.cs` partial, `ClimbRules`, `ClimbTuning`, `ClimbZone`). Tower behaviours (hatch, sash, firefinder): new `Assets/Scripts/Tower/WashedAshore.Tower.asmdef`, which references Gameplay; Gameplay never references Tower. Editor code: `Assets/Editor/Tower/WashedAshore.Tower.Editor.asmdef`. Tests: `Assets/Tests/EditMode/Tower/` and `Assets/Tests/PlayMode/Tower/`, own asmdefs.
2. **Contract.** `IInteractable`: a prompt text (verb plus noun; empty means not available now) and `Interact(Interactor)`. One modal seam for the firefinder: the Interactor holds at most one focus object, which gets a per-frame tick and an end call. No base class, no event bus.
3. **Input.** One code-built `Interact` action (keyboard E, gamepad buttonSouth), owned by the Interactor, in the existing PlayerController pattern. No move to the .inputactions asset this pod. South pressed while a prompt shows interacts and does not jump; Space always jumps. The order is explicit (DefaultExecutionOrder or a direct call), with a PlayMode test using a virtual Gamepad.
4. **Raycast.** One `Physics.Raycast` per frame from the camera pivot; range from `InteractionTuning` (Validate: at most the 2.5 m in the targets file); mask `WorldLayers.SightMask`; triggers ignored; first hit only, so solids occlude; the target is the IInteractable found with GetComponentInParent on the hit collider. No new layer, no TagManager edit, no proximity query, 0 B GC per frame.
5. **Prompt.** One screen-space uGUI Text with the built-in LegacyRuntime font, built by script, inactive unless a target or focus supplies text. Key label from the binding display string of the action for the device last used. A test counts enabled Graphics across all canvases: 1 while aiming, else 0.
6. **Player seam.** `PlayerController` gains a suspend/resume pair (move and look input off, horizontal velocity zeroed, gravity still on) and nothing else. The focus object drives the camera pivot pose and FOV and restores them exactly on exit (tested to 0.00001). Focus ends on Respawn or a snap. The feature never writes Cursor.lockState.
7. **State, for later multiplayer.** Each interactable has a stable string id from the build script, a small explicit state, and one apply method separate from the input path. Animation is a pure function of (state, time since change) from the tuning asset. No static state.
8. **Hinged parts.** Collision changes in discrete steps. The hatch leaf is solid only when fully closed. Open is refused while the capsule footprint overlaps the leaf; close is refused while the capsule is in the passage. The hatch zone is enabled only while the hatch is open. Sashes never change the CLIP walls of the cab.
9. **Out.** Highlight, outline, glow, icons, crosshair, inventory or pickup hooks, quests, save data, audio hooks, networking code.
10. **Spec conflict to settle before code (t-designer, with t-qa):** I3 has E both rotating the bearing and exiting. One control means one thing per mode, and no tap-or-hold timing.

## 4. Ruling (c): asset pipeline

1. **Export.** Static FBX; after import every node has identity rotation and scale 1, `MARK_Front` at +Z, `MARK_Up` at +Y, bounds equal to the parameter table. An EditMode test asserts it. Movers (hatch, four sashes, firefinder ring) are separate objects with origins on their hinge axes, in LOD0 only; LOD1 and LOD2 show them closed.
2. **Import.** A path-scoped `TowerImportSettings` AssetPostprocessor (pattern: FishImportSettings): no cameras, lights, animation or imported materials; builds colliders from the prefixes and strips their renderers. A rerunnable, batch-callable `TowerBuilder.Build()` makes the prefab: materials, LODGroup, zones from the sidecar, interactables, far card. No hand edits; grep the YAML for `fileID: 0` after wiring. The FBX .meta is kept across regenerations. `ModelFacingPostprocessor` must leave the tower alone.
3. **Budgets.** LOD0 at most 60k triangles including movers; LOD1 at most 25% and LOD2 at most 5% of LOD0; at most 4 materials across the whole prefab including the far card; one atlas of 2k or less, with the far-card frames in a reserved region of it (a separate texture needs agreement from t-qa on T4 counting first). Tower in view costs at most +0.5 ms median against a twin with the tower absent, at 1 km and in the cab.
4. **LODs.** Cross-fade through the LODGroup (already enabled). Lattice geometry is retired before its members fall under about half a pixel (a 0.127 m leg is one pixel at about 110 m at 1080p, FOV 70, MSAA off); thresholds come from tuning.
5. **Far card.** A separate yaw-facing, alpha-blended card with pre-filtered coverage, baked by script from the finished model. Hand-written URP HLSL, one pass, no depth or opaque texture (WebGL2-safe), ZWrite off, queue Transparent+5. It fades in by distance as the geometry fades out, and uses its own haze toward `unity_FogColor`, capped below 1 from tuning. It is the only thing in the scene exempt from the 120-520 m fog.
6. **Fog, trees, URP assets: unchanged.**
7. **Far plane.** Proposal: 1000 to 1200 m as one named constant applied by script, so the tower and its hill are inside the frustum at 1 km. The card is fully faded by 1150 m. Kept only if a measured slot shows: Windows shore-walk median within +0.3 ms of baseline and p99 under 16.6 ms; before/after stills at the three BellsBendSky poses; an OK from t-director on those stills. Otherwise revert and come back to me.
8. **Stated limit:** the tower is engineered to be legible at 1 km (T4, G4). Beyond about 1.15 km it is not drawn, because terrain past the far plane is not drawn either. "Visible across the map" in the T4 rationale is not claimed. Told to t-qa before any result exists.

### T3 determinism (with t-tools)

1. `build_mc39.ps1` sets PYTHONHASHSEED to 0, clears PYTHONPATH, PYTHONHOME and PYTHONSTARTUP, and runs `blender -b --factory-startup --python-use-system-env`. `build_mc39.py` fails at start unless `sys.flags.hash_randomization` is 0 and the Blender version equals the pinned one.
2. **The only mask:** the 7 int32 values under FBXHeaderExtension/CreationTimeStamp (Year, Month, Day, Hour, Minute, Second, Millisecond), 28 bytes, found by walking the binary node records, never by offset or pattern. The check fails unless exactly 28 bytes are masked. Creator, CreationTime, FileId, ids and geometry are never masked.
3. **Procedure:** two fresh processes into two different directories. Pass = every differing byte lies inside the 28. Report raw and masked SHA-256. Negative control: one parameter changed must differ outside the mask.
4. **Script hygiene:** start from an empty factory scene; one seeded `random.Random` with the seed in the parameter table; no clock, uuid, pid or environment reads; explicit sorted iteration; fixed creation order and names; path_mode STRIP, no embedded textures; no modifier whose result depends on thread order unless the two-run check proves it.
5. **Scope:** the byte bar is the FBX, as T3 says. `mc39_walk.json` must be byte-identical with no mask. Generated textures and the far-card bake get a hash manifest; if they are not bit-identical that is reported to t-qa, not hidden.

## 5. Open points raised with their owners

- T1 says height "to the eaves"; the digest says the standard heights are to the cab floor. T1 says pitch 52 +/- 2 deg; the top flight on the drawing is 33.7 deg. t-artist and t-qa settle both targets before results.
- The E conflict in I3 (section 3.10).
- P4 bird avoidance: prefer an analytic keep-out from the landmark over a collider layer. A new layer needs a ruling and one integration slot.

## 6. Design gate verdict (2026-10-10, on tower-design-spec.md r1.3 and the t-engineer view)

**(a) Stair traversal: APPROVED WITH CONDITIONS. Technique B: flight lanes, real landings, tower capsule.**
**(b) Interaction system: APPROVED WITH CONDITIONS.**
**Hatch and top flight: the t-director ruling stands (opening 1.50 x 0.66 m, 8 cm duck). No T1 number moves.**

B is the same family as section 2 (analytic placement on the nosing line, decided per frame). It goes further in two places, and I accept both. Where this section and sections 2-4 disagree, this section wins.

Superseded from the early ruling:
- 2.3 (move through CharacterController.Move on flights), 2.4 (catch proxy), 2.5 (run-out and floor set-back): replaced by "no physics on a lane".
- 2.7 (no controller property is ever written): replaced by condition 2 below.
- Proxy table: no corridor walls at 0.50 m, no catch proxies, no mc39_walk.json. Hatch tolerance (1.00 m, 80 deg) and pedestal limit (0.23 m) withdrawn.
- 3.1 (asmdef layout) and 3.10 (no tap-or-hold): replaced by conditions 11 and 15.
- 4.7 and 4.8 (far plane 1200 and the 1.15 km limit): replaced by section 7.

Traversal conditions:
1. **A lane has no physics.** Feet position is a pure function of (lane, distance along it, lateral offset). The rules are a static class with EditMode tests. Either the controller is disabled for the stay or its excludeLayers is set to everything; t-engineer picks one and says which.
2. **Controller writes allowed:** radius (authored value or towerCapsuleRadius), and enabled or excludeLayers while on a lane. Never slopeLimit, stepOffset, height, center or skinWidth. A test asserts every authored value 1 s after leaving the first foot and after a Respawn from the cab.
3. **Tower state heals itself.** Respawn, a WorldBoundsClamp snap, and being outside the climb area bounds each clear lane and tower state in the same frame. The radius returns only through the 0.4 overlap check, retried every frame. Statics reset in a SubsystemRegistration hook.
4. **Hand-over is proven clear.** EditMode: at every lane end (16, plus the top end with the hatch open) a CheckCapsule with the tower capsule finds no solid. Lanes are left only through their ends.
5. **Off the tower nothing changes.** The new code sits behind one early check in Move(); with no climb area in range the existing branch runs unchanged.
6. **Generic types, in Gameplay** (Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Climb/): StairLane, ClimbArea (owns the lanes; the small capsule, jump lock and walk speed apply inside it), ClimbRules, ClimbTuning, and a PlayerController.Climb.cs partial. No tower or lookout names in Gameplay. A later ladder is a lane at 90 deg.
7. **One camera seam.** The duck eye offset and the firefinder pose and FOV both go through one view-override API on PlayerController, which restores the authored pivot pose and FOV (tested to 0.00001). Nothing else writes the pivot.
8. **Lane data comes from the build.** Positions from FBX empties (LANE_Fnn_Foot, LANE_Fnn_Head, hatch opening corners); widths and other scalars from the parameter table file, read by the import script. EditMode: each lane lies on the visual nosing line within 0.02 m.
9. **Frame-rate proof.** The climb and duck tests run at Time.captureDeltaTime 1/30 and 1/240.
10. **Collision set.** Prefixes as in section 2 (COL_BOX_, COL_CVX_ on Default; CLIP_BOX_, CLIP_CVX_ on WorldBounds). Per flight: one solid wedge and CLIP side walls 2.2 m high at the visual rail planes. Landings: CLIP walls at the rail planes from deck to 2.2 m. Every proxy within 0.05 m of the surface it stands for. A clear circle 0.60 m across at every lane end. No concave MeshCollider, at most 150 colliders.

What the proxy is, for T1 and C2: T1 measures render meshes. On a flight the containment is the lane itself: the capsule centre stays within laneLateralSlack (0.05 m) of the centre line, and the rail proxies are never touched. Rail and wall proxies sit at the visual rail planes within 0.05 m and exist for players who are not on a lane. On the tower the capsule is 0.25 m radius (0.60 m wide with skin) instead of 0.40.

Interaction conditions:
11. **Assemblies.** WashedAshore.Interaction (Assets/Scripts/Interaction/): references Unity.InputSystem, WashedAshore.World and uGUI; never Gameplay. WashedAshore.Gameplay references Interaction, only for the South gate. WashedAshore.Lookout (Assets/Scripts/Lookout/): hatch, sash, firefinder; references both. Editor code in Assets/Editor/Lookout/ and tests in Assets/Tests/EditMode/Lookout and PlayMode/Lookout, each with its own asmdef.
12. **Three tuning assets, split by assembly:** InteractionSettings (range, prompt layout), ClimbTuning (stair speeds, lane numbers, towerCapsuleRadius, towerWalkSpeed), LookoutTuning (hatch, sash, firefinder). Each has Validate(). The retune levers and ranges in spec section 8 are unchanged.
13. **Prompt:** one uGUI Text with the LegacyRuntime font and a Shadow component, built by script. Not UI Toolkit.
14. **Contract:** Verb, Noun, CanInteract, Interact as in spec 3.1. Nearest hit only, WorldLayers.SightMask, triggers ignored, one ray per frame, 0 B GC per frame (prompt strings cached).
15. **Firefinder E tap or hold: accepted.** A pure rule over press and release times on game time, with EditMode tests either side of the 0.20 s threshold.
16. **South gate:** explicit order between Interactor and PlayerController, tested with a virtual Gamepad (T-I1d).
17. **State for later multiplayer:** stable string id per interactable, explicit state, one apply method separate from input; animation a pure function of (state, elapsed).
18. **Hatch:** leaf solid only when fully closed; the swing-volume box is a prebuilt CLIP collider that is switched on and off, never created at runtime.

## 7. Far view (replaces 4.7 and 4.8)

Targets that stand (team-lead): the tower in view from 1 km (T4, N15) and the north barrier visible from the cab at 2,218 m (P3, N10).

The far plane is not the main blocker. Fog reaches 100% at 520 m and trees are culled at 450 m, so from the cab everything past 520 m is flat fog colour whatever the far plane is.

**Technique: view distance by eye height.** One component and one tuning asset in WashedAshore.World. Far plane, fog start and end, and terrain tree distance are a smooth function of the eye height above the ground under the player.
1. At ground level fog and tree distance are exactly the values in the scene today, read at start, not copied. Existing ground-level looks and suites see no change from those.
2. Raised (the cab): far plane, fog and tree distance go out far enough to draw the barrier and the shore. The values come from the measurement below.
3. Ground-level far plane: 1000 to 1200 m, so the tower and its hill are inside the frustum at 1 km. This is the one global change.
4. Tree distance is written in steps, and only when the step changes.
5. The tower from the ground still uses the far card of 4.5.
6. Nothing here needs a second camera, a depth texture or a new pass.

Budgets: ground shore walk median within +0.3 ms of baseline and p99 under 16.6 ms; in the cab at least 60 FPS (spec) with median at most 12 ms and p99 under 16.6 ms; water and fish suites unchanged against the baseline.

Not known until measured: the cost of trees out to 2 km from the cab, and whether a 1 to 2 px barrier line reads at 2.2 km. Final numbers follow the measured slot. t-director rules on the look (fog thinning with height) at G5.

## 8. Clarifications after the verdict (2026-10-10)

- All eight flights are 52.4 deg (86 ft 3 in standard, cab floor 26.289 m). The 33.7 deg row in section 1 is superseded.
- No per-collider ignore list and no Physics.IgnoreCollision: a lane has no physics for the whole stay (condition 1).
- T3: any output file that differs fails, textures included (stricter reading, agreed with t-qa and t-tools). Results in TestResults/tower-tools/.
- Ownership: t-tools owns TowerBuilder.Build, BuildBatch and TowerImportSettings. Tower assets live in Assets/World/Lookout/.
- Generic gamepad prompt label is mapped to "South".
- Far view, added risk from the G4 criteria (studio:decisions gate/lookout-tower-g4-far-view-criteria): at ground level the ridge under the tower at 1 km is fully fog-coloured. If it does not read against the sky the tower hangs. A ground still with a terrain-hidden twin is in the t-tools measurement. A ground-level fog change, if needed, goes to t-director and t-producer with that still.

## 9. Gate closed; far-view conditions added (2026-10-10)

- Spec r2 (tower-design-spec.md) checked against section 6: conditions carried. **The design gate is closed; climb and interaction code may land in baton slots.**
- Prompt key text is the spec mapping (E, A, Cross, South) by last device used, not the raw binding display string.
- Far view must also meet the t-director conditions V1-V7 (studio:decisions ruling/lookout-tower-view-distance-by-height). The ones that bind the build: a function of eye height only, nothing eased over time (V1); most of the change happens below canopy height (V2); tree-distance steps must not show as pop, so they are taken below the canopy or the step rule is dropped (V3); cab values are the least that works (V7). The barrier is never thickened or given a distance-only material.
- P4 is graded against the pre-tower baseline at far 1000 m. The far-plane and eye-height change lands on its own, before the tower, so t-qa can attribute any difference to it. Suites that could move: fish F7 and wildlife or bird sighting counts (frustum-based), water readback and look tests, the FPS tests.
- Decisions from Noah via team-lead (2026-10-10): the ground-level draw limit of about 1.15 km is accepted, with "visible across the map" a disclosed limit and follow-up; ground-level fog and tree distances stay as today. So a ground fog change is not an option for the G4 ridge risk. If the 1 km still shows the ridge does not read, the options left are inside the far card (for example a hazed ground apron under the tower baked into the card) and go to t-producer and t-director with the still. The P3 barrier clause is met by the eye-height technique or reported as a FAIL with numbers.
- View-distance component, added condition (asked by t-qa): one read-only accessor returns what was applied this frame: far plane, fog start, fog end, tree distance, and the eye height above ground it used. Tests read that, never a recomputation. The Unity terrain does not expose its drawn-tree set, so tree pop is graded from the frames where the applied tree distance changes (with the eye height at each) plus frame diffs of the capture.
- Spec changes after r2, noted (no change to the technique or the section 6 conditions): T-C2c split in two (eye clearance 0.20 m everywhere; corridor clear except named, drawn guard rails on landings 5-7); ClimbTuning gains laneJoinAlong, laneMouthWidth, capsuleFitLift and capsuleRestoreSeconds; jumpInCab removed; T-C2d added. The hand-over check (T-C1g, condition 4) still has to pass with those rails back.
- Approved for the climb code: controller disabled for the lane stay; grow-back check on the full authored capsule with capsuleFitLift; a single error log if the radius stays small for capsuleRestoreSeconds outside every climb area, which makes every existing suite a guard without editing it.
- **Condition 18 amended (2026-10-10):** the hatch leaf collider is on in both rest states (closed = floor, open = slab against the lower wall) and off for the whole swing, because the leaf is its own aim target and must be closable. Nothing moving is ever solid. At the end of a swing the leaf collider goes on before the swing blocker goes off, in the same frame; the reverse at the start. The open-rest slab is included in the EditMode capsule sweeps.

## 10. Near plane inside the climb area (TD position, 2026-10-11)

Proposal (t-designer): camera near plane 0.05 m inside the climb area, eye-clearance rule restated from 0.20 to 0.12 m. Cause: the tower faces lean in 0.125 m over eye height, so on every landing a capsule 0.30 m from a face rail has its eye about 0.13 m from the bracing; the 0.20 m bar cannot be met as drawn. The spec changes only when t-qa and t-director also agree.

**Technical position: APPROVED WITH CONDITIONS** for the landing-face case. The flight 7 and 8 figures wait for the layout root-cause table.

1. Depth: on Windows D3D11 (reversed-Z) with a 32-bit float depth buffer the far precision barely depends on the near plane, so 0.05 m with a 3 km far plane is acceptable. The depth format is not serialized in PC_RPAsset, so it is the platform default; expected 32-bit float, not proven. Proof required before it is relied on: from the cab, the same frozen frame at near 0.10 and 0.05, changed pixels along the far shore at the noise floor. The far card does not write depth. WebGL2 has no reversed-Z float depth, so there the smaller near plane costs far precision: known limit, not tested.
2. Seam: the PlayerController view-override API is the only writer of nearClipPlane. The authored value is captured once and restored exactly on leaving, on Respawn and on any snap, tested like pose and FOV. The value is in ClimbTuning; Validate refuses less than 0.03 m. The view-distance component remains the only writer of the far plane.
3. Scope: one value everywhere inside the climb area, cab included, following the same derived state as the tower capsule. Not tied to eye height. The firefinder inherits it and sets none of its own.
4. The switch at the first tread changes nothing visible unless something lies between 0.05 and 0.10 m from the eye. Evidence: a twin still either side at the noise floor.
5. Tests compute the near-plane corner distance from the camera near plane, FOV and aspect at test time, not a typed number.
6. Aspect: every budget and still in this POC is at 1920x1080 (16:9). The 0.12 m rule is 1.4 x the corner distance at 16:9 and 1.17 x at 21:9; 21:9 is not blocked and not verified, and is recorded as such.
7. Aspect, decided with t-designer and t-qa: the eye rule is evaluated at 21:9. The project does not limit aspect (vertical FOV fixed at 70), so 32:9 and wider (corner 0.139 m at near 0.05) are not covered. No clamp or letterbox is added in this pod: it would change the camera and the prompt layout for the whole game. Disclosed limit and follow-up.

## 11. Rulings on the schema 3 manifest (2026-10-11, from t-engineer arithmetic, nothing run)

- **Head of flight 8: layout finding, no code seam.** 0.177 m of floor before the cab wall; the tower capsule needs 0.30 m (the previous manifest had 0.38 m). A sideways run-out does not fit (0.075 m between the opening and the firefinder ring) and an early level-off lifts the feet 0.18 m off the nosing line. Condition 4 fails there with the end named, until the layout gives 0.30 m. Flight 8 is reported at 56.3 deg, outside the T1 bar of 52 +/- 2: with t-artist's root-cause table and t-qa. Foot of flight 8 is 0.203 m from CLIP_BOX_Landing07_06: same.
- **Hand-over at the end of a level run-out: approved (amends condition 4).** The controller takes over 0.30 m (radius + skin) onto the landing, not on its lip. No early hand-over inside the run-out: a player who stops there is still on the lane; turning back descends. Run-out length per lane end comes from the manifest and never drops below radius + skin; an end with less room fails the check by name. The CheckCapsule point is the end of each run-out, with a sweep along the run-out itself.

## 12. Chained lanes (ruling, 2026-10-11; replaces the run-out item in section 11)

Context: the stair was rebuilt to sheet T-1004-A (manifest schema 3). The top flight is drawn at 56.3 deg with the hatch 9 in from the wall; the pitch ruling is with team-lead. Arithmetic by t-engineer, nothing run.

**APPROVED WITH CONDITIONS: chained lanes (t-designer), as the one mechanism for run-outs and the cab exit.**
1. A junction joins exactly two lane ends. No branches. The player passes through with no controller hand-over.
2. At a junction the player continues only if the input points along the next lane by at least laneJoinAlong; otherwise they stand still. Deterministic, EditMode-tested in ClimbRules.
3. Lane kinds are named in the manifest: flight, run-out, exit. Condition 8 is amended by kind: a flight lane lies on the visual nosing line within 0.02 m; a run-out lane is level on its deck within 0.01 m; an exit lane is a straight line between two named empties, and the manifest states its largest feet distance from a visible tread or floor, which may not exceed 0.10 m.
4. A level lane moves at towerWalkSpeed; an inclined lane at stair speed.
5. Condition 4 applies to free ends only: at every free end a CheckCapsule at radius + skin finds no solid, and the free end is at least radius + skin from any edge the capsule could fall over. The build names any end that fails; nothing shortens silently.
6. Run-out lanes on landings need not be collinear with their flight: they may angle away from a wall, which is how the foot of flight 8 gets its 0.30 m without moving anything.
7. A CLIP wall must stand for something: a drawn rail plane (within 0.05 m) or a named headroom or eye guard. t-artist states which CLIP_BOX_Landing07_06 is. A guard may be set back as far as the eye-clearance sweep allows.
8. The cab exit lane needs t-director's yes (the last 0.18 m of rise happens on a westward step, with a required turn of at least 30 deg below the floor).
Not approved: moving, widening or thinning any drawn member or platform; loosening a check; a per-end special case in code.

## 13. Far-view measurement (slot 2) and what it settles (2026-10-11)

Source: TestResults/tower-tools/farview/farview-table.json and stills (9 sessions, release player, RTX 4060 Ti, 1080p).

Settled:
- **Tree distance is dropped from the technique.** Terrain.treeDistance 450, 1200 and 2500 gave identical triangle counts, frame times and stills. Trees already draw to the far plane and are limited by their LOD prefabs. The component writes far plane and fog only. The tree-pop condition (V3) no longer has a mechanism to guard.
- **Ground far plane 1200 m:** median +0.12 to +0.15 ms against +0.3 allowed. p99 is not shown: the 1200 run and its control both read 33 ms while Blender renders used the same GPU. A clean rerun is required before it lands.

Not settled (cab values are NOT set):
1. **Frame time.** p99 is 25-33 ms in all six cab runs against 16.6 ms (median 7.0-7.5 ms passes). It is heading dependent: 4.5 M triangles median, 7 M at the worst headings. 101,592 trees as 4-triangle cards are 0.4 M, so the load is not far trees; the likely source is near forest at full LOD seen from above. No run at the authored settings exists, so the cost of the far view itself is not separated. Needed: one cab run at authored far plane and fog, and frame time and triangles per 10 deg of heading in every cab run.
2. **Barrier.** With NorthBarrier hidden, 1 pixel changes by more than 8/255 at far 2500 (fog 625-2500) and 5 pixels at far 3000 (fog 750-3000), at 2,187 m. The fence is 2.6 m tall; one pixel there is 2.5 m. The t-level viewshed has only 7 of 403 fence samples visible over a 20 m canopy (longest run 35 m, about 14 px). Rendering reaches it; it does not read.
3. **World edge.** With the fog thinned, the end of the water shows as a hard line with a band of sky-ground colour below the horizon (heading-090 still; the map ends 1,461 m east of the summit). One fog curve cannot both hide the east edge at 1.46 km and leave the barrier readable at 2.19 km.
4. **Ridge stills** did not answer the G4 question: both viewpoints stood in forest.

### 12a. Version built (settled 2026-10-11 with t-designer, t-engineer, t-artist's clearance map, t-qa, t-director). Overrides section 12 where they differ.

Fact set: TestResults/tower-art/cab-landing7-clearance-map.txt (schema 3). Correction: my "0.075 m between the opening and the firefinder ring" was measured near the cab centre, not at the head of flight 8; the map shows a 0.30 m circle fits west of the opening, nearest centre (0.03, -0.71), 0.648 m from the head.

1. **Landings:** the level run-out is part of the flight lane (as staged by t-engineer), not a separate lane. Length per lane end from the manifest (foot_run_room, head_run_room), never under radius + skin (0.30 m); an end with less fails by name. Hand-over only at the run's far end; no early hand-over. Run-outs are swept clear along their whole length.
2. **Linked lanes:** two lane ends that coincide within 1 mm are linked; no controller hand-over there. With the input within laneJoinAlong of the other lane the player continues; within laneJoinAlong back along the current lane they turn back; otherwise they stand. Exactly two ends per link. No link joins two inclined flights (graph assertion in EditMode).
3. **Cab step-off, flat form:** flight 8 stays on its nosing line to its head at floor level. Lane 9 is linked there and runs flat on the cab floor plane about 0.65 m west to a free end 0.30 m from the south wall proxy and 0.30 m onto the floor west of the opening. All rise is on the stair. The player turns about 80 deg themselves; holding W without turning leaves them standing; the same in reverse. The hatch gate also gates lane 9 while the hatch is not fully open. The earlier variant (leave the stair 0.12 m early and rise 0.18 m on the step) is withdrawn and not built.
4. **Named exception on lane 9 (t-qa):** the capsule overlaps the south wall proxy only, by at most 0.13 m, decreasing to zero at the free end, asserted every 2 cm. Overlap with anything else fails. The camera does not enter the wall.
5. Level stretches move at towerWalkSpeed, ramped by stairAcceleration.
6. Condition 8 by kind: flight on the nosing line within 0.02 m; level run and lane 9 level on their deck or floor within 0.01 m; lane 9's largest feet distance from a visible floor or tread stated in the manifest, limit 0.10 m.
7. Condition 4 at hand-over points only (run-out far ends, lane 9's free end): clear at radius + skin, and radius + skin from any fall edge.
8. **CLIP_BOX_Landing07_06** stands for no drawn rail. It is kept only as a named edge guard if the capsule sweep shows the inner edge of landing 7 is open without it; in that case it is trimmed to stay at least 0.30 m from flight 8's hand-over point and clear of its run-out sweep. If the sweep shows flight 8's wedge and side walls already close that edge, it is dropped from the export.
9. Pitch of the top flight (56.3 deg, drawn) and the T1 row for it: with team-lead.

### 12b. Further settled (2026-10-11)
- t-engineer costing: every run-out as its own lane is 6-7 h of rework on unrun code plus a third detector freeze. Built: option 2, a built-in level run at every lane end by default and a linked lane only where the manifest provides one (same as 12a).
- Approved: on a level stretch that meets an incline, speed is capped by sqrt(stairSpeed^2 + 2 x stairAcceleration x distance to the incline). Pure function of position, in ClimbRules, no new tuning field.

## 14. Cab water look: proposal, not a ruling (2026-10-11; all numbers are estimates, nothing built)

Asked for by t-producer after t-director's findings (water reads as a flat dark slab from the cab; a band and a hard water edge at the horizon; ruling/lookout-tower-water-skirt gives six conditions).
- One cause for both: the water shader's far haze (BellsBendWater.shader, the _WaterHaze term) is keyed to a shallow view angle tuned for a 1.65 m eye. From the cab the angle is steeper and the fade switches off.
- Proposed as one work item: (a) skirt quads at MapConfig.WaterLevelY outside the terrain extent on the east, south and west sides, same water material, own root (not the Water root), layer 4, no colliders, built by BellsBendWater.cs; (b) one extra input to the existing haze term, driven by the view-distance component, zero at ground level.
- Owners and estimate: skirt script and tests t-level, 2-3 h; shader and look t-artist, 2-3 h plus look rounds; component hook t-tools, under 1 h; two slots.
- Exposure: WaterBuildTests count quads under the Water root, so the skirt needs a separate root; a scene hash pin moves; the shader change needs the water look and readback suites rerun. Lake-mask guard, WaterMaps hash, WorldBounds, fish and wildlife have no mechanism to move. Both parts have an off switch; with both off everything must match the baseline exactly.
- Open risk: beyond the map the shader has no shore data; the cheapest way to make the skirt read as deep open water is unproven.
- Alternatives rejected: fog only (hides the far shore and barrier); matching the sky ground colour to the fog (changes ambient and reflections map-wide); a plain fog-coloured plane (visible join, not the same water).
- T4 in the cab is graded as written (t-qa). If the baseline run shows the p99 load is near forest at full LOD from height, the fix is on the tree LOD side; no bar is moved.
- t-qa accepted 12a for grading. "Run-out lane" in the spec rows means the level run-out stretch of each flight lane; the manifest marks each stretch (start, end, length per lane end). An invisible wall that stands for no drawn rail is allowed only as a named "edge guard" in the manifest, with drawn geometry within reach of the plane it blocks.
- t-director accepted the height-keyed water term in principle (studio:decisions ruling/lookout-tower-water-skirt-amend1), conditions W1-W5: a function of eye height and view angle or distance only, never time; no visible tone shift between stills one landing apart; near water keeps its approved look; it reflects the sky the scene has; ground-level stills unchanged. The skirt has conditions S1-S6 (ruling/lookout-tower-water-skirt). Both wait for the measurement runs and the barrier decision.

## 15. Lane-end rule: DRAFT, not issued (2026-10-11)

Prompted by new layout facts (deck straight ahead of five lane ends on landings 5-7 is 0.10-0.16 m; the faces cut the platforms). Second occurrence of the lane-end blocker. Issued only after t-qa has checked t-engineer's table of all 17 lane ends (both directions, current manifest, top flight at 56.3 deg) and t-artist's clear-region maps for landings 5-7.
- R1. Every lane end hands over at a point clear at radius + skin (0.30 m) and at least 0.30 m from any drop.
- R2. If the straight level run reaches such a point within laneMouthDepth: built-in run-out stretch.
- R3. Otherwise a linked level lane from the lane end to the nearest such point on the same deck, from the manifest: level within 0.01 m; swept clear at radius + skin every 2 cm across the lateral slack (t-qa's row of record); no hand-over at the junction.
- R4. Carry-through at a junction only within the angle set in ClimbTuning (t-director: about 30 deg; t-designer sets it); beyond it the player stops and turns. What stops them must be visible, and the releasing turn must be toward the next flight; a landing where it is not goes to t-director by name (studio:decisions ruling/lookout-tower-upper-landing-step-on).
- R5. Each landing keeps two hand-over points with at least 0.30 m of ordinary controller floor between them, 0.30 m from any drop along the whole crossing.
- R6. If no point on a deck satisfies R1, that end fails by name. No shortening, no exception.
- Step-off eye distance (0.140 m with slack at flight 8's head): expected about 0.17 m at 0.02 m slack; unconfirmed.
- Draft status after t-artist's clear maps (TestResults/tower-art/landing-clear-maps.txt) and t-qa's read: every upper landing has a clear region (case B areas 0.140, 0.070, 0.050, 0.014 m2 on landings 4-7; the shrinkage is the player body against the drawn platforms, not a model error). As mapped: landing 7's two points are 0.197 m apart (R5 fails; its strip's two ends are being tried); landing 6's straight lines hold only 0.293 m (R3 fails as mapped); margins of 1-5 mm were computed on a 5 mm grid and are not a demonstrated pass. Asked of t-artist: analytic distances, points chosen for margin not nearness, bearings in the script output. The 0.32 m build margin is withdrawn as a gate; 0.30 m rules and the margin is reported. Bearings by hand: landing 6 is 35 deg off straight ahead (stop and turn), the others within 30; every releasing turn is toward the next flight. Open with t-engineer: whether a player can be off a linked run-out's line at hand-over.

## 16. Slot 3, site placement (t-level, 2026-10-11): conditions met

Tiles listed: Terrain_1_1, 1_2, 2_1, 2_2 (the four expected); only TD_2_2 changed by hash, the other three byte-identical, which shows the art scripts still reproduce the tiles as painted. Trees 101,592 to 101,572, all 20 inside the ring; 0 splat texels changed outside ring or track; heights changed only on the pad (max 0.340 m); lake-mask guard, WaterMaps and both build suites clean. Evidence: TestResults/tower-level/.
Finding (pre-existing, not the patch): a headless BellsBendGround.Build re-saves 6 plant materials with legacy mirror properties reset and rewrites 7 terrain layers and 6 prefabs EOL-only (Tinted() in BellsBendVegetation). Visual properties unchanged. Owner t-artist, non-blocking follow-up; until fixed the revert is a scripted runbook step.
- Draft status after t-artist's HAND-OVER output (points chosen for margin, analytic distances; not yet checked by t-qa). Largest clear circle 0.381 m on landings 4-6, 0.3193 m on landing 7. Landing 5: linked run-outs 0.398 m, turn 32 deg both ways toward the next flight, crossing 0.438 m, thinnest line 0.3007 m. Landing 7: run-outs 0.371 and 0.351 m, turns 31 deg (up) and 24 deg (down), crossing 0.300 m exactly; coming down, the step is to the right while the next flight is to the left (sent to t-director by name). Landing 6: no single straight run-out holds 0.30 m (0.2925 m past the end of CLIP_BOX_Landing06_06). **R3 amended in the draft: one or two linked level lanes**, so landing 6 may use a straight-on segment then a turn; being computed. The 0.32 m build margin stays withdrawn; landing 7 would miss it by under 1 mm. Carry-through angle is a separate ClimbTuning field, laneJunctionAlong, default 0.87 (t-designer); at that value landings 5 and 7-up are stop-and-turn by 1-2 deg.
- Draft R4 settled in value: laneJunctionAlong default 0.82 (35 deg), t-designer. t-director's ceilings (studio:decisions ruling/lookout-tower-upper-landing-step-on-amend1): carry-through up to 35 deg toward the next flight, up to 30 deg away from it; nothing above 35 deg carries through. Landing 7 coming down (24 deg right, next flight 66 deg left) is accepted provisionally as a named exception, judged at G6; if it reads wrong the remedy is the interpreted start of flight 8, never the deck or a drawn member. The lane-end table flags any step away from the next flight over 30 deg. Lane 9 as built (manifest schema 4): 0.666 m, flat; overlap with COL_BOX_CabWall_03 0.1234 m at the foot, never increasing, zero at the free end; 0.053 m from the visible floor edge over the hatch.
- Draft status after t-engineer's 17-end table (scratchpad tower-eng/lane-end-table.txt; manifest schema 3 sha256 d5dec8b3a4e1322c; arithmetic, nothing run; top flight at 56.31 deg): 9 ends on the built-in run (ground, landings 1-4), 5 on a linked level lane (landing 5 both, landing 7 both, lane 9), 2 failing as tabled (landing 6, single straight lane). **Decided for R3: no lateral slack on any level stretch** (built-in runs, linked run-outs, lane 9); the offset settles to zero, so checks are on the line. Zero margins not to be issued on: drop distance exactly 0.300 m at the eight landing ends of landings 1-4 and along their crossings (run length = laneMouthDepth 0.30; room is 0.38-0.46 m; t-designer's value); lane 9's free end 0.300 m from the south wall (to be chosen for margin); landing 7's crossing 0.300 m. Missing from t-artist: eye distance per run and linked lane; drop distance along the landing 5 and 7 crossings; landing 6 two-segment geometry; clear maps for landings 1-3; body clearance at flight 1's ground hand-over point. Step-off eye distance: 0.140 m to Sash_S_Hinged_muntin at the junction with 0.05 m slack, 0.190 m with none.
- t-qa's check of the 17-end table: no arithmetic disputed; verdict "do not issue yet". Adopted order: margin-chosen points (landings 5, 6, 7, lane 9) and the missing numbers from t-artist; table reissued; t-qa checks; ruling issued naming the exact point set; export; t-qa checks the rebuilt manifest's own table before the first test.
- Landing 6: a two-segment run-out exists (first segment 0.030 m straight on, bend at least 37.4 deg toward the next flight, second 0.379 m, crossing 0.403 m). No exception needed; it is a stop-and-turn (over the 35 deg ceiling). The minimum-bend solution sits on the boundary (0.3000 m), so the point is to be chosen for margin at a larger bend.
- Selection rule given to t-artist for every linked run-out: hard limit 0.30 m on every clearance and on the crossing length; margin target 10 mm where the region allows; one segment if possible, else two; carried step at most 35 deg toward or 30 deg away from the next flight, larger means stop-and-turn the same way as the next flight; choose the pair with the largest smallest-margin.
- Zero margins (t-qa, adopted): 10 mm of real margin wherever there is room (landings 1-4 by a longer run-out, laneMouthDepth is t-designer's; landing 7 crossing 0.320 m at no cost; lane 9 end and landing 6 points chosen for margin). Where there is none the ruling declares the comparison as at least 0.30 m minus 0.5 mm, for float error only, naming the ends, before any result.
- Lateral slack on level stretches is built as: offset closes to the line at walking pace, never snapped; detector asserts 1 mm or less on linked level lanes.
- t-designer: run-out length is split from mouth depth. New ClimbTuning field laneRunOutLength, default 0.34 m (range 0.30-0.45, Validate at least radius + skin + 0.03), one value for all straight built-in runs; laneMouthDepth stays 0.30 m for the join mouth. The planned cut of laneLateralSlack to 0.02 m is withdrawn (flights keep 0.05 m; level stretches have none).
- t-qa agreed landing 6 by two linked level lanes, no exception. Open design point they raised: landing 6's first segment is 0.03 m, too short to close 0.05 m of flight slack. Proposed to t-engineer for costing: on a flight whose end is linked to a level lane, the lateral band tapers to zero over the last laneMouthDepth of the flight (pure function of position), so the player is on the line at the junction. Not decided until t-engineer answers.
- t-director accepted landing 6 as a stop-and-turn and the mix of carry and stop on the upper landings; ceiling stays 35 deg (studio:decisions ruling/lookout-tower-upper-landing-step-on-amend2). G6 capture includes landing 6 both ways and landing 7 coming down.
- **Lateral offset, adopted for the ruling (t-designer's two envelopes; replaces the taper proposal above; mechanics being costed by t-engineer):** (1) on a flight the allowed slack tapers to zero over the last 0.30 m before each end, a function of position, both directions, no snap; (2) joining a level stretch from free floor, allowed offset = half the mouth width x (1 - s / stretch length) from the free end, zero at the lip or junction, closed at walking pace; (3) where the full 0.61 m funnel does not fit when swept at radius + skin, that free end's mouth is narrowed to what fits, per end, by name in the manifest, computed by the build. The offset is guaranteed zero at every junction and at the lip end of every level stretch. A player standing still holds their offset.

## 17. Code review: TowerImportSettings.cs and TowerBuilder.cs (t-tools, staged, 2026-10-11; read in full, nothing run)

Confirmed: box proxy nodes are re-posed onto their box (centre and axes); the identity-rotation rule of 4.1 applies to render meshes, movers and empties, not to proxies.
Blocking before landing:
1. TowerImportSettings.cs:178 requires mesh.vertexCount == 8; Unity splits vertices by normal and UV, so a flat-shaded box has 24. Test the 8 distinct positions and 12 triangles, proven on a box from the real FBX.
2. TowerImportSettings.cs:136 checks localScale only; check lossyScale, and make a proxy node with children an error.
3. TowerBuilder.cs:144, the fileID 0 scan misses null entries inside lists.
4. No proof that a rebuild keeps the prefab's file IDs stable; the placed instance in World.unity depends on them. Build twice and compare the prefab byte for byte in the landing slot.
Non-blocking: null references fail the build against an allow-list from LookoutGameplaySetup instead of being listed; use WorldLayers.WorldBoundsName; cap texture import at 2048; check the player log for unreadable collision meshes; assert identity rotation and scale 1 on non-proxy nodes in the builder; log the material count against 4.
- Draft status after t-artist's missing numbers (schema 4 dev manifest; not yet checked by t-qa). Eye distance on built-in runs, on the line: all above 0.20 m, smallest 0.215 m (flight 8 foot); flight 8's head and lane 9 are 0.190 m to the south hinged sash muntin (belongs to the near-plane request; the 0.140 m step-off figure assumed slack across lane 9 and no longer applies). Drop distance along crossings: landing 5 0.3369 m, landing 7 0.3191 m. Ground hand-over point 0.509 m from the nearest member. Lane 9 free end moved for margin to (-0.0148, 26.289, -0.6648): 0.701 m long, +0.045 m over 0.30, wall overlap 0.1234 m at the foot and never increasing, feet at most 0.068 m from the floor edge. Landing 7 crossing 0.320 m at +0.019 m margin. Still open: landing 6 points chosen for margin (only the boundary solution exists); straight-run figures at laneRunOutLength 0.34 m for landings 1-4 (t-artist's landings 1-3 rows came from the linked-point search and do not describe the built-in run); eye distances on the linked run-outs of landings 5-7; t-engineer's costing of the lateral offset rule and confirmation of level-to-level links.
- t-engineer's reissued rows (manifest schema 4 sha256 d8d8a9bd785428eb; arithmetic, not run): at laneJunctionAlong 0.82 landings 5 and 7 carry through, landing 6 stops at its bend 3 cm past the lane end (on a lane, so a stop like the cab corner, not a hand-over on the lip), lane 9 stops (about 105 deg), no step away from the next flight over 30 deg. laneRunOutLength: smallest margin on landings 1-4 is 3.0 cm at 0.33 and 0.34 m and 2.3 cm at 0.35 m (the faces cut landings 3 and 4), so 0.34 m stands. Chains: level-to-level links are accepted by the staged graph; hand-over only at the final free end by construction. Go given on two fixes: braking distance measured along the whole chain of level lanes; run-outs read as an ordered list of points per flight end. Still owed by t-engineer: fit and cost of the two-envelope lateral offset rule (what is staged is the first version, closing along the stretch).
- **Point set chosen for the ruling (from t-artist's SELECT output; provisional until issued and until t-qa has checked the final table):** landings 1-4 built-in straight run at laneRunOutLength 0.34 m (smallest margin 3.0 cm); landing 5 flight 5 head to (0.873, 1.233) and flight 6 foot to (1.233, 0.873), one segment 0.362 m, 29.3 deg toward, carried, smallest margin +0.0101 m, crossing 0.509 m; landing 6 two segments, flight 6 head 0.080 m straight on to (-0.417, -1.028), bend 44.2 deg toward flight 7, 0.325 m to (-0.742, -1.032), flight 7 foot the mirror, smallest margin 10.0 mm (no option gives more: the roomiest point cannot be reached by a line keeping more than 10 mm from the short rail between the mouths), crossing 0.410 m, stop-and-turn; landing 7 flight 7 head to (0.509, 0.853) at 31 deg toward and flight 8 foot to (0.829, 0.853) at 24 deg away, both carried, crossing 0.320 m, margin +0.0187 m (pinned by t-td so the two run-outs do not cross; the script's own tie-break made them cross); lane 9 free end (-0.0148, 26.289, -0.6648), +0.045 m. Points are 5 mm grid picks with analytic distances at the points and 1 mm sampling along segments. Still to come: STRAIGHT rows at 0.34 m for landings 1-4 from the same script, eye distances along the selected run-outs, analytic segment minima for landings 5 and 6, t-engineer's answer on the two-envelope lateral offset rule, the final table, t-qa's check.
- **Landing 6 reopened (t-director reversal, studio:decisions ruling/lookout-tower-upper-landing-step-on-amend3, supersedes amend2):** carry-through ceiling toward the next flight is now 40 deg (away stays 30); no arrival may be a stop released by a turn of less than about 10 deg. The margin-chosen bend of 44.2 deg (10.0 mm margin) falls between: too sharp to carry, and released by about 4 deg. Smallest possible bend is 37.4 deg at zero margin. Options put to t-director: (a) carry at 40 deg or less with the margin that leaves; (b) 44.2 deg and stop, which they have ruled out; (c) a bend of 50 deg or more as a real corner. t-td accepts (a) if the margin is at least a few mm and t-qa agrees a declared tolerance, and (c) outright; not (b). t-artist is computing the best margin at bend <= 40 and at bend >= 50. The rest of the chosen point set stands. laneJunctionAlong will be reset by t-designer (cos 40 deg is about 0.766). A layout check is added: every junction that stops the player must exceed the carry-through angle by the release minimum.
- **Lateral offset rule: go given to t-engineer (about 1.5 h staged), mechanics settled.** Sideways offset is clamped to a band that is a pure function of position: on a flight, laneLateralSlack x clamp01(distance to the nearer lane end / 0.30); on a level stretch with a free end, half the mouth width x (1 - s / stretch length) from the free end; on inner segments of a chain, zero. Standing still holds the offset, reversing never widens it, the clamp never pushes outward. The join test uses the funnel's half-width at the player's position, so a join is never a snap. Mouth width per free end comes from the manifest where the full funnel does not fit. Lane-end checks read a margin target (0.010 m) and a float tolerance (0.0005 m) from the targets file, fail under 0.30 m minus tolerance, and list every end under the target. Chain items closed: level-to-level links accepted; braking distance measured along the whole chain.

- **Defect in the join funnel as ruled (found by t-artist's mouth-width export, dev manifest SHA-256 5a9b4726da89cfc515bedf782920091048ae4ea1666e168a307a898b5d4d0762; no run-out point moved; segment_turns_deg added).** With the funnel symmetric about the lane line and swept at 0.30 m, the widest mouth is 0.61 m at the ground only; 0.111 m on landings 1-2; 0.098 m on 3; 0.085 m on 4; 0.017 m on 5; effectively zero on landings 6 and 7 and on lane 9. Cause: each hand-over point is a few cm from a wall on one side and has open deck on the other, and a symmetric funnel applies the wall-side limit to both. **Fix in progress, not yet computed or costed: the mouth and the lateral band are per side** (toward the wall about the point's wall margin, which is as far as the controller can physically stand; toward open deck whatever the sweep at 0.30 m allows, capped at half the default). Rests on an assumption to be confirmed by t-engineer: a controller on ordinary floor cannot bring its centre closer to a wall than radius + skinWidth. - t-qa on the funnel fix: a per-side funnel is fine in principle, but the assumption (controller centre no closer to a wall than radius + skinWidth) must be measured, not read from the code: a CharacterController can end up closer for frames at a time when pressed in at an angle, at speed or into a corner. Asked of t-engineer: a PlayMode probe with the tower capsule (radius 0.25, skin 0.05) against a flat wall, an inside corner and a short wall end; head-on and at 30 and 60 deg; tower walk, walk and sprint; captureDeltaTime 1/30 and 1/240; 5 s hold; smallest centre-to-wall-plane distance per case. Needs one short Editor PlayMode slot (no tower import, build or launch). Requirement the per-side widths are checked against (t-qa's wording): a descending player can join from every position the controller can reach on that landing's free floor with W and yaw only; the detector asserts the join is inside the manifest's per-side widths. Put to t-qa for T-C1g part 2: on the wall side the funnel is swept at the measured physical minimum (never under the 0.25 m solid radius plus a stated margin), on the open side and along the line at radius + skin; not agreed yet. Blocker count: still occurrence 2, open; nothing has been issued and withdrawn.
- Per-side mouth half-widths, first result (dev manifest SHA-256 9954ab6c5d602c6035ab1b9efc1e554845e6a8729be13c37901accb40e1cea08; t-artist states lane ends, run-out points and lane 9 unchanged from a9062524, compared field by field; not yet re-verified by t-td): ground 0.305 / 0.305; landings 1-4 open side 0.216 m (limited by the short guard rail between the stair mouths), wall side 0.042-0.055 m, about the point's wall margin, as expected; **landings 5, 6, 7 near zero on both sides (0.000-0.041 m)**; lane 9 north 0.087 m (firefinder ring), south 0. Suspected cause on landings 5-7, to be confirmed by instrumenting which constraint binds: the funnel was also held 0.30 m from every stair-mouth edge. That clearance is a rule for hand-over points (where the controller takes over); a body on a lane approaching its own stair is not at risk from the mouth. Asked: recompute the funnel against walls only; hand-over points keep 0.30 m to every mouth edge.
- t-designer's acceptance criterion for join mouths, adopted: per free end report open-side half-width, wall-side half-width and coverage (fraction of the reachable cross-section at the free end that the mouth contains); an end passes at 100% coverage whatever its width, or at 0.15 m or more on the open side; an end with less than both goes to t-designer by name with what a player who misses meets. A lopsided mouth is acceptable on feel.
- **Blocking finding from t-engineer (arithmetic on dev manifest 9954ab6c, nothing run):** even per side, the band that fits when swept at 0.30 m is 0-18 mm on landings 5, 6, 7 and the cab and 42-55 mm on the wall side of landings 1-4; at walk speed one frame is 27 mm of travel, so the upper tower could not be joined from the floor by a person. Cause: the hand-over points clear the walls by only 11-18 mm over the bar. Geometry rows of the table are built from a9062524 and pass (all margins, no-twitch at +0.18 deg, away at 26.59 deg, 8 turns and 3 stops each way, hatch drop 0.3448 m); not sent, because the dev manifest moved three times in four minutes.
- **Ruled in principle (provisional): t-engineer's option A, catch separate from band.** Band = the per-side funnel the body is held to on the lane (unchanged). Catch = where a floor join may start: per side, computed by the build as the widest region from which the straight pull-in path to the band keeps the body no closer to any wall than the controller can physically stand (a table value, wall_reach, 0.30 m until the PlayMode probe measures it). On a join outside the band the offset glides into the band, never outward, at no more than walk speed, and holds when the player stops. An EditMode sweep verifies every catch region's pull-in paths on the built colliders. Provisional on: the probe measurement; t-artist's computed catch regions and coverage; t-qa's agreement (replaces "the join is inside the band by construction" with a named, logged glide; pull-in paths swept at the measured minimum); t-designer's agreement on the glide's feel (cap if needed). 0.0 in the manifest means "on the line", never "use default". t-artist asked to stop changing out/dev/ until band and catch are both in, then name that hash as final.
- **Join mouth, simplified form (replaces "catch separate from band" above, same day; adopts t-designer's three conditions):** one funnel per side per free end. Its half-width at the free end, W, is computed by the build as the largest value that is (1) at most half the lane's width, 0.305 m; (2) at most the stretch length x tan(40 deg), so the offset never closes faster than a 40 deg carry (0.285 m on a 0.34 m run); (3) such that the funnel region on that side, offset up to W x (1 - s / length), swept by the body at wall_reach, touches no wall. wall_reach is a table value: the measured minimum distance from the controller's centre to a wall (t-engineer's PlayMode probe), 0.30 m until measured. Stair-mouth edges constrain hand-over points only, not the funnel. The clamp is position-driven as already staged (zero at the lip or junction, holds when the player stops, never grows); no rate-based glide; a join is inside the funnel by construction. The manifest states per side W, the binding constraint by name, the reachable span and coverage; coverage under 100% with W under 0.15 m on an open side is named to t-designer and t-qa. Not adopted: sweeping at the 0.25 m capsule without skin (the radius is to be measured, not assumed). Reading of t-engineer's finding: a wall-side mouth equal to the point's wall margin is complete coverage, not a small target; what was broken is landings 5-7, where W fell far below the point's own margin. Awaiting: the probe slot, t-artist's recompute with binding constraints named, t-qa's agreement.
- **Join mouths with the binder instrumented (t-artist, dev manifest SHA-256 41ed64131dce4ce494a6d82504385c0df7df9795561745d19207376bb398db71; walls-only funnel swept at 0.30 m; sampled sweep, not a proof; points stated unchanged).** With mouth edges swept, the open side on landings 5, 6 and 7 was bound by a stair mouth; with them removed, a wall binds next on landings 5 and 6 (t-td's hypothesis half right). Per end, mouth against reachable span: landings 1-4 open 0.216 m of about 0.91 m (short rail between the stair mouths), wall side complete (0.042-0.055 m), coverage 27-28%, not flagged; landing 5 (F5 head, F6 foot) 0.025 / 0.013 m in 0.077 / 0.013 m, 42%; landing 6 (F6 head, F7 foot) 0.021 / 0.011 m in 0.085 / 0.011 m, 33%; landing 7 F7 head open side 0.305 m, 93%, F8 foot 0.037 / 0.041 m in 0.052 / 0.040 m, 84%; lane 9 north 0.087 m (firefinder ring), south 0 of 0.051 m, 63%; ground 0.305 both. **Flagged by t-designer's criterion: F5 head, F6 foot, F6 head, F7 foot, F8 foot, lane 9.** A miss meets a rail or wall in every case, never a stair mouth. On landings 5 and 6 the player is confined to a pocket about 9 cm wide and the mouth is its middle 3-4 cm.
- What-if asked of t-artist (printed only, not written to the manifest): the funnel alone swept at 0.26, 0.27, 0.28 m (solid radius plus margin; skin is solver tolerance and nothing collides on a lane), reachable span and all hand-over, line and crossing checks still at 0.30 m. If it gives near-full coverage it goes to t-qa as a named, bounded departure from T-C1g part 2; if not, landings 5 and 6 are fiddly by geometry and it is a finding for t-designer. t-designer agreed: one funnel per side; ground keeps the one run length (slope-limited at 0.285 m); closing tied to forward travel.
- **Join band: rule adopted (t-designer's proposal; replaces the single per-side funnel width and the reduced-radius what-if, which is withdrawn).** t-designer judged the six flagged ends unacceptable as computed: a straight funnel is widest at the free end, so a player outside it who presses W at the stair moves away from it; walking forward never rescues a miss. Rule: per free end and per side, band(s) = min( W0 x (1 - s / length), reach(s) ), made non-increasing along s and zero at the lip or junction; W0 = min(half the lane's width, length x tan 40 deg); reach(s) = how far to that side a body at wall_reach from every wall can stand on the deck at that cross-section (walls and deck edge only; stair-mouth edges do not constrain it). The mouth at the free end is the whole reachable pocket by construction (coverage 100%, asserted by the build), except where the cap or slope limit binds on a wide platform (landings 1-4, the ground), which the manifest names. The body is never anywhere the controller could not stand at wall_reach, so t-qa's envelope (radius + skin) holds as written with no departure. The 40 deg cap reads: the offset closes no faster than tan(40 deg) of forward travel except where a drawn rail or wall bounds the reachable set; the manifest marks those stretches and names the rail (landings 5 and 6; t-director to see at G6). The manifest carries a sampled band profile per side as flat arrays; the code takes the smaller neighbour between samples. wall_reach stays a measured table value (0.30 m until the probe). Nothing computed yet under this rule; the table waits.
- **Join envelope, final form (t-engineer's shape plus the reachable term; same rule as the entry above, stated as built):** per side on the final level stretch, allowed(s) = min( cap half-width, tan(laneGlideMaxDegrees) x distance from the lip or junction, reach(s) ), non-increasing from the free end, zero at the lip or junction; used by both the join test and the clamp; a pure function of position, no rate, no per-join state. cap half-width = half the lane's width x laneMouthWidthFactor (default 1.0); laneGlideMaxDegrees 40. reach(s) comes from the manifest as a flat list of samples every 10 mm per side (how far to that side a body at wall_reach can stand on the deck; the code takes the smaller neighbour between samples). No separate catch or swept band: t-artist computed a catch in a scratch folder and found it never binds anywhere; what limits a join is the reachable set itself (reading confirmed: sideways at the same distance along the stretch). Stated consequence: a player beside a stair mouth, further off the line than tan(40 deg) x their distance from the lip, is outside the envelope and steps back to join. Checks: EditMode sweep of the envelope on the built colliders at wall_reach; steepest narrowing and wall name recorded where reach binds; side read from the exported left_dir unit vector. Dev manifest out/dev/ held at 41ed6413 while t-artist iterates in scratch; next copy is named final. Probe staged by t-engineer (WallApproachProbe.cs; walk and sprint cases at the authored 0.40 radius since the game never moves a 0.25 capsule faster than 1.6 m/s); definition with t-qa; slot still needed.

Points stated unchanged from a9062524. Record of why the straight funnel was dropped, open-side coverage of the reachable span at wall_reach 0.30 m: landings 1-4 W 0.216 of about 0.91 m (not flagged); landing 5 W 0.025 of 0.077 m, 32%; landing 6 W 0.021 of 0.085 m, 25%; F8 foot W 0.036 of 0.052 m, 69%; lane 9 south 0 of 0.051 m; wall sides 100%; binder on landings 5 and 6 is the short rail between the stair mouths, passed as the funnel narrows. Agreed in advance if the probe measures wall_reach under 0.30 m: the body on a lane may be as close to a wall as the controller can physically stand on the floor there and no closer; T-C1g part 2 then sweeps the band at the measured wall_reach on the wall side, never under 0.25 m plus a stated margin, with hand-over points, lines and crossings still at 0.30 m; disclosed as a measured-controller fact. wall_reach is the smallest value over the whole probe matrix on the tower capsule, repeated by t-qa on the built tower. Detector: lateral bound inside a free end's funnel is the manifest's band profile at that position + 0.01 m, never growing; on stretches the manifest marks as wall-bound it logs each sideways move by the clamp with its speed. Blocker count (t-qa friction row 16, referred to t-producer and team-lead): the unjoinable mouths are a further finding inside the still-open lane-end blocker, found in design review before any ruling or build of record, not a third occurrence. The ruling is issued once, on the final manifest hash, after t-qa's check of the table.

- **What the landed code does at a lip today (t-engineer, by reading, not run on the tower): a known defect until the lip completion lands.** A player off the line at a stair head does not join (band zero at the lip), keeps walking on the controller down the flight's solid wedge at 1.6 m/s with no clamp, and nothing catches a grounded player mid-flight; going up, the first tread is a wall to them. The drop-in join takes the lane's full 0.61 m prism and then clamps the offset in one frame near a flight end. No tower PlayMode result from the current code is meaningful for C1 or C2. Go given to stage the lip completion (about half a day): envelope without the forced zero; flight band from each end's lip width easing to the slack; junction carry re-expressed in the new lane's frame so the feet do not move, with a build check that the carried offset is inside the next lane's band; inner segments min(reach, smaller end band); drop-in join accepted only inside the band at that position; drop distance taken over the whole free-end width per side; sweeps and detector on the one band function. What t-engineer's replay does show is a frame-rate-dependent arrival: reading the target before the frame's move, the player reaches the first tread at 0.647 m/s at 1/30, 0.580 at 1/60, 0.480 at 1/240 (stair speed 0.45). **Ruled (t-qa, agreed by t-td): read the target at the position this frame's move will reach**, arriving at exactly stair speed at all three rates with the drop within a x dt; one pass, no iteration; tested at 1/30, 1/60, 1/240 in both directions, including the frame that crosses onto the incline and a level-to-level link.

Not yet: detector and fixture on the new band; eye, corridor and free-end sweeps on the game's band function; drop distance over the free-end width; table regenerated on the next hash. The landed code still has the lip hole.
- **Open, with t-designer (and t-qa for the envelope): the hatch drop-in.** A player stepping into the open hatch falls through a 0.66 m slot and can arrive about -0.085 to +0.075 m off the lane's line; flight 8's mid-flight band is 0.05 m, so about 40% of what physically fits is outside it and, as staged ("inside the band or keep falling"), lands on flight 8's wedge or landing 7. t-td's technical view: the band under the opening should be what the slot allows, from the manifest, over the stretch of flight 8 under the opening only, easing to laneLateralSlack below it (the lip-width mechanism applied to a stretch); not a whole-flight slack unless the stretch form costs materially more; not a full-width catch for drops only; not accept-and-measure. Nothing changes until t-designer answers.

Today: flight 8 under the cab hatch, about 0.085 m a side. The drop-in join stays "inside the band at that position or keep falling"; with the profile every drop that fits is inside. Go given to t-engineer (optional per-flight profile, no flight-8 special case; TaperProblem extended; EditMode and PlayMode cases); record asked of t-artist in the same export (stretch under the opening, per-side width at wall_reach, what bounds it, eye and corridor sweeps on flight 8 across that band); put to t-qa for the envelope over that stretch.
