- Three findings from it. **F-1:** with the drop rules as t-td worded them, four reach-bound sides fail the 0.26 m floor (landing 5: band 0.146 m, edge 0.2433 m from a stair mouth; landing 6: 0.155 m, 0.2521 m), and the cap reduction cuts flight 7 head's open side from 0.305 to 0.021 m. t-td's position, put to t-qa for their ruling: withdraw the cap reduction and the floor; every position in the envelope is one the controller can already stand in, so a drop-based cap protects nobody and recreates dead spots; state the smallest mouth distance per side as a figure; assert instead that the centre is on the deck by a stated distance (0.24 m table value) and state the mouth distance over the narrower set an arriving player leaves from. Provisional until t-qa answers. **F-2:** eye rows confirmed by a real sweep (old then new, body at 0.30 then 0.25 m): landing 6 0.163 to 0.111 m; landing 6 run-outs 0.172 / 0.184 to 0.112 / 0.123; landing 7 0.189 to 0.126; landing 4 0.177 to 0.130; F8 foot run-out 0.232 to 0.133; landing 4 runs 0.206 to 0.136; F7 head run-out 0.207 to 0.149; cab 0.297 to 0.247; lowest flight 0.299 to 0.253; lane 9 0.190 unchanged. Earlier landing figures were up to 14 mm optimistic (25 mm grid missing the edge of the reachable region). This is the eye-clearance finding, for t-designer, t-qa, t-director. **F-3:** t-td's instruction on the ground end was wrong: the envelope there uses the tower capsule's 0.25 m like every other end; the 0.40 m body only bounds where a not-yet-joined player can stand, exported as a separate list shown to be inside the band.
- Next scratch run: deck datum; points re-selected; this candidate with the F-1 and F-3 changes; the band under the hatch; the frozen-behaviour comparison; the findings' figures again on the new decks before any copy.
- **F-1 ruled by t-qa (final): the drop condition is withdrawn as a limit and kept as stated figures.** The envelope is the reachable set, capped at half the lane's width and by the 40 deg slope; no drop-based cap reduction, no 0.26 m floor. Per side per free end the manifest and table state the smallest distance from the envelope to any stair-mouth edge and which term binds; R1 keeps two guarantees separately (the line's point at 0.30 m from walls and drops with its margin; the envelope's worst case as a figure); first shown is the smallest mouth distance over the set a player arriving up the stair leaves from (offsets up to the lip width). Two build assertions replace the floor: a regression guard, centre at least 0.24 m from any stair-mouth edge everywhere in the envelope (set just under today's smallest figure, 0.2433 m on landing 5, labelled as a guard); and a principled floor, centre at least 0.06 m inside the deck's edge everywhere in the envelope (more than one frame of travel at towerWalkSpeed at 1/30, 0.053 m; the derivation is asserted against the tuning value). PlayMode addition (t-designer, with t-qa's yes): on landings 5 and 6 and at flight 7's head, walk to the envelope's outer edge at the free end, join, leave and stand; zero falls, zero joins from above, supported for 5 s. Disclosed by t-qa as a pre-result change with the three named ends and figures (0.2433 m, 0.2521 m, flight 7 head's 0.021 m mouth under the withdrawn rule).
- Eye clearance: t-qa records the scratch sweep as confirming the finding (landing 6 at 0.111 m is under the derived floor of 0.118 m at a 0.05 m near plane); it needs t-td, t-designer and t-director together, after the raised decks' figures; t-qa will not agree a third number.
- Band under an opening staged by t-engineer (general optional record per flight, no flight-8 case; TaperProblem extended; built-tower test fails if the flight under the hatch has no record). Refinement asked of t-artist: the fit width per side as samples along the stretch, because flight 8's head lip is 0 on both sides and the band eases to it over the last 0.30 m; a sample where the body fits further out than the band reaches is a finding, not something to fix by widening the head's lip.
- **Scope of the two guards corrected (t-td's wording was too broad; put to t-qa for their yes):** both apply at the free end's cross-section, across the envelope's width there, because hand-over happens only at a free end and a lane runs up to its own lip by design. envelope_mouth_guard 0.24 m: the centre is at least that far from the nearest edge of any opening on that deck, no exclusions (every stair mouth including the lane's own; for lane 9 the hatch opening). envelope_deck_inset_min 0.06 m: deck under the centre and under points that far around it, with the derivation asserted against towerWalkSpeed / 30. Both live under checks in the manifest. For landing 6's first segment the eye figure of record is t-engineer's sweep at the game's band; t-artist's figure at the wider lip is an upper bound.
- t-qa agreed: both guards at the free end's cross-section, no exclusions. The statement rests on two things to be asserted, not assumed: (1) the game hands the player back to the controller only at a free end, never mid-lane or at a junction; Respawn, a clamp snap, the area being disabled, an external move and entering water are placements, not releases in motion. Asked of t-engineer: one function through which lane state ends, with a reason; a test walking every lane and chain in both directions asserting every exit is a free-end hand-over inside that end's envelope; cases for each placement reason with the player's state afterward; the failure named stateLost like the detector's. (2) Lane 9 over the hatch's south-west corner stays a named item with its own table rows (length over the open hatch, feet distance from a visible floor edge, the gate when the hatch is not fully open).

## 15b. Raised-deck export, built in scratch and HELD (t-artist candidate manifest SHA-256 e48af76a806ca45efcc89fb9c797adb76cd4fe7446dadad490ad337e1bda3d05, two fresh builds identical; out/dev/ still e5c77c45; nothing copied; not checked by t-qa)

Datum as built: steel on the drawn levels; every walking surface 0.0508 m above its steel (landings, cab floor, stair foot); every flight keeps its risers and plan position, both ends rising together, so lane ends do not slide; landing outlines shrink about 3.5 mm a side where the leaning faces cut them 5 cm higher; points re-selected by the module moved 2-3 mm. The stair-foot landing raised above the bed plates is a named interpretation (with t-qa and t-director).
Frozen table on the new decks (was, now): landings 1-4 straight on, wall margins +0.0556 / +0.0556 / +0.0349 / +0.0301 to +0.0556 / +0.0556 / +0.0311 / +0.0262; landing 5 carried 28.42 to 28.85 deg toward, margin +0.0127 to +0.0115, crossing 0.5205 m; landing 6 stop at a bend 45.09 to 45.00 deg, margin +0.0111 to +0.0093, crossing 0.3094 m; landing 7 carried 30.39 to 30.90 deg toward and 26.59 to 26.90 deg away, margin +0.0180 to +0.0171, crossing 0.3171 m; cab stop-and-turn, lane 9 unchanged. No behaviour changes.
- **N-1: landing 6 at 9.3 mm, under the 10 mm target, bend on the 45.0 deg floor.** Holds 0.30 m with real margin and the no-twitch floor (44.92 deg) by 0.08 deg. Reported to t-producer, t-director, t-qa as a named under-target end; t-td recommends accepting on t-qa's earlier conditions (named with reason, measured margin reported with a warning under 5 mm, the 45.0 and the release rule not moved).
- **N-2: the junction check fails at the four one-segment turned run-outs (F5 head, F6 foot, F7 head, F8 foot): a defect in t-td's layout rule.** Where a run-out turns at the lip, on one side the turned lane's perpendicular leaves the deck at the junction and its reach there is 0; a flight-lip offset carried across the turn (0.092 m on landing 5; 0.262 m on F7 head's open side) lands where the turned lane has no band (a sideways correction in one frame). Landing 6's bend, 8.5 cm onto the deck, holds both ways. **Rule change: no run-out turns at the lip; every linked run-out starts with a segment collinear with its flight and turns at a bend on the deck** (landings 5 and 7 become two-segment). The first segment has its own envelope (reach, converging at 40 deg toward what the bend accepts); the junction check compares the carried position with the other lane's band at the along-position it lands on. Risk: the bend sharpens when moved onto the deck; if landing 5 or 7 cannot stay carried (bend between 34.92 and 44.92 deg, or away over 30) it is a finding to t-producer and t-director. Next scratch run decides.
- N-3 and N-5: already settled (drop cap and 0.26 m floor withdrawn; ground end at 0.25 m).
- N-4, eye rows on the raised decks, body at 0.25 m (eye-clearance finding): landing sweeps 1-7 0.488, 0.488, 0.380, 0.121, 0.207, 0.111, 0.126 m, all minima against a wall; on the ordinary walking line landing 4 0.197, landing 6 0.170, landing 7 0.193 m (still under the 0.20 m bar of record), landing 5 0.299; lowest flight 0.230 m (flight 5 near its head); top flight under the hatch band 0.225 m; cab 0.247 m; lane 9 0.190 m.
- Band under the hatch as computed: fit from 0.219 to 2.022 m of flight 8's 2.473 m lane (0.45 m clear of the head's eased zone); east side 0.0752 m (opening's edge), west 0.0802 m (the flight's own rail-plane wall; kept, since a dropping body is on the controller).

## 15c. Run-out rule as sent in writing (2026-10-11, for one scratch run)

Sent as one text to t-artist and t-engineer at t-producer request. The lane-end ruling is still NOT issued; it waits for the export hash, the regenerated 17-end table and the t-qa check.

Linked run-outs (landings 5, 6, 7):

1. Shape: P0 (lane end of the flight, on the lip), P1 (bend), P2 (hand-over point). Segment 1 (P0 to P1) is collinear with the flight, length d0 at least 0.02 m. Segment 2 is P1 to P2. No turn at P0. This is the fix for N-2 (section 15b).
2. Hard limits unchanged: P2 at least 0.30 m from walls and stair-mouth edges; both segments 0.30 m from walls; the two hand-over points of a landing 0.30 m apart, with the line between them holding 0.30 m; no crossing.
3. Bands per side at wall_reach 0.25 m:
   - Segment 2: band2(s) = min(0.305, reach2(s), lip2 + tan(40 deg) x distance from s to P1), running minimum from the free end; lip2 = reach2 at P1.
   - At the bend, per side, accept1 = the largest w (no more than reach1 at P1) such that a body at P1 + w across segment 1, re-expressed in the frame of segment 2 (along t, across a), has |a| <= band2 at max(t, 0) on the side a falls on; solved both ways round.
   - Segment 1: band1(s) = min(0.305, reach1(s), accept1 + tan(40 deg) x distance from s to P1), running minimum from P0.
4. band1 at P0 must equal the lip width of the flight on each side (foot_lip / head_lip). A solution where it is less is rejected at build.
5. Bend angle b at P1 against the lever angle (acos 0.82 = 34.92 deg): carried if b <= 34.92 toward the next flight or b <= 30.00 away; stop if b >= 44.92; nothing between.
6. Frozen behaviour searched first: landing 5 carried at both ends; landing 6 a stop at both ends; landing 7 carried (flight 7 head toward, flight 8 foot away).
7. Selection: largest smallest-margin; then longest d0; then smaller b.
8. Fallback (t-producer): if landing 5 or 7 has no carried solution, search it as a stop, report both attempts, do not copy; it goes to t-producer and t-director as a finding with "stops" as the fallback.
9. Export per linked end: points, per-segment s / reach / reach_bound_by / band per side, accept1 per side, bend angle with toward or away and carried or stop, d0, every margin, and two check rows (band1 at P0 against the lip; the carry check at P1 both directions both sides). The "band at P0 against the lip" row is printed for all 17 ends.

Runtime side (t-engineer): bands and accept figures are read from the manifest, never recomputed. The carried position is re-expressed in the new segment frame, then clamped by that band, never outward. JunctionProblem runs on the carried position at every junction, both directions, both sides; the check is not to be loosened.

Rulings on the staged guard and exit work:
- EndLane(reason) accepted with reasons FreeEnd, Respawn, ClampSnap, AreaDisabled, MovedExternally, EnteredWater, LeftAreaBounds.
- Release-position check at the free-end envelope plus LaneLateralMargin accepted as is.
- `checks` in the manifest stays a list of named results; wall_reach, envelope_mouth_guard and envelope_deck_inset_min go in a separate object (key name to be agreed by t-artist and t-engineer). A missing object fails the build; no defaults.
- Known limit, to carry into the final section: ClampSnap and EnteredWater exits have no test in LookoutLaneExitTests.

Landing 6: 9.3 mm smallest hand-over margin against the 10 mm target on the raised decks (candidate e48af76a, held). Proposed as a named under-target end, target unchanged. t-producer accepts if t-qa agrees on record; t-qa answer pending at the time of writing.

### 15c addendum (2026-10-11, after t-qa replies)

- Landing 6 at 9.3 mm: t-qa YES on record. Named under-target end, target unchanged. Reason: the no-twitch rule against 0.10 m of drawn deck. Conditions: named in the ruling with both numbers; margin and bend angle measured on built colliders and lanes, warning under 5 mm; the 45.0 floor, the release rule and the target are not moved to recover millimetres without going back to t-qa. The bend clears the no-twitch floor (44.92 deg) by 0.08 deg; that figure is reported, because a lever retune of a tenth of a degree would turn the stop into a twitch stop. Disclosed item, not narrowed-after-results (no result of record exists).
- Several junctions in one frame (t-qa point; a first segment may be 0.02 m, one frame at towerWalkSpeed at 1/30 is 0.053 m). Rule: lane travel in a frame is a loop over the remaining distance; at each junction the point is re-expressed in the next segment frame, clamped by that band (never outward), the junction test applied and the carry-or-stop decision taken, then travel continues. A stop at a bend ends the travel of that frame at the bend and discards the rest. A carried junction moves the feet by the frame travel only. The loop is bounded by the lane graph; hitting the bound is a logged error. No minimum d0 above one frame of travel was set (it would cost margin and hold at one frame rate only); d0 is printed beside 0.053 m as information.
- Tests required before the first tower PlayMode run: the two-junction frame at 1/30 built from the shortest first segment in the manifest, on a carried landing and on landing 6 both directions; same stop count at 1/30, 1/60, 1/240; ClampSnap exit in PlayMode; EnteredWater exit in EditMode. My earlier "stated limit" answer on the two exits is withdrawn.
- Export additions: bend angle to two decimals with the lever angle, the stop floor and the difference; the usable lever range in the ruling is restated from the angles of this export.

### 15c amendment (2026-10-11, t-director caveat via t-producer, before the scratch run)

Source: ruling/lookout-tower-upper-landing-step-on-final-amend3 (studio:decisions). Replaces rule 8 and adds an exception to rule 7 of section 15c.

- 8a. Landing 5 (both ends) and landing 7 going up (flight 7 head): with no carried solution, a stop is searched only with the bend turning toward the next flight, b >= 44.92 deg. A stop turning away is not a solution. Both attempts reported, nothing copied; finding to t-producer and t-director.
- 8b. Landing 7 coming down (flight 8 foot; bend away from the next flight, 26.90 deg against the 30.00 limit): no stop fallback. Over 30.00 goes to t-director and t-producer by name with the numbers before any copy.
- Rule 7 exception, flight 8 foot only: shortest d0 that meets rules 1 to 5, not largest margin first. Report per d0 from 0.02 to 0.06 m in 0.005 m steps: bend angle, room under 30.00, smallest margin, rule 4 per side, pass or fail; both choices printed if they differ. A selected margin under the 10 mm target needs t-qa agreement before the copy.
- Cost accepted: this end may end with less clearance margin than the general order would give.

### 15c: rulings on the staged inner-segment envelope (2026-10-11)

- t-engineer staged form approved (design): inner segment envelope per side = min(cap, reach(s), w_next + tan(laneGlideMaxDegrees) x distance to the far junction); w_next found by halving to under 0.1 mm; JunctionProblem uses the real band function at each junction, both directions, both sides. A segment without reach lists throws.
- Simpler alternative (limit the flight lip to what the linked lane accepts) declined as the mechanism: it equals the staged form only where acceptance equals reach, and hides a hole elsewhere. Kept as a check: reach at the lip against what the first segment accepts, per side, on all 17 ends; shortfall over 0.5 mm fails the build.
- Cross-check: the w_next computed in the game build check is compared with accept1 exported in the manifest; over 0.5 mm apart fails with both figures.
- Consequence of rule 4, corrected from an earlier remark of mine: band1 at the lip is at most accept1 + tan(40 deg) x d0 (0.017 m at d0 0.02 m), so rule 4 sets a floor on d0 wherever the lip is wider than what the bend accepts. "Shortest d0" at flight 8 foot means shortest that also passes rule 4.
- Manifest shape for first-segment lists: `inner_mouths[k]` on the run-out record, mouth-record form, s from the flight-side end toward the bend (name provisional, t-artist to confirm). Sides told apart by left_dir alone.
- Table: a collinear first junction prints as its own row (0.00 deg, carried).

### 15c: rulings on the generator readings and manifest shape (2026-10-11, before the run)

- Reverse carry at a bend: a candidate that fails is rejected; the band of segment 2 is not shrunk to fit. Each rejection is recorded in attempts[] with reason and shortfall.
- Segment 2 minimum length 0.05 m kept, as a named table value (run_out_final_segment_min).
- Search: 5 mm grid then hill-climb to 0.01 mm on the margin; the refined pair is kept only if every rule (amendment included) still holds. The refinement must be deterministic (T3).
- Manifest shape, fixed: table values in `check_values`, a sibling of the `checks` results list; first segment in `<end>_run_out.first_segment`; final segment stays `<end>_mouth`; `<end>_junction` on all 17 ends. t-engineer adapts the reader (provisional inner_mouths name dropped).
- s on a first segment runs from the lane end (on the lip) toward the bend, with s_origin: "lane_end" in the record, asserted by the reader. Found as a mismatch between the generator and the staged reader before any build.

- Riding in the same export: 114 ANCHOR_ empties (t-qa measurement anchors; importer and tests must tell them apart by prefix) and the far card as one quad. The far card is a look change; asked t-artist whether t-director approved it, and to take it out of this export if not.

### 15c: manifest keys, final (2026-10-11; corrects the entry above)

- First segment key is `<end>_run_out.inner_mouths[0]` (mouth-record form, s from the lane end toward the bend), not first_segment. t-artist adopted the name the staged reader already used; my instruction to t-engineer to rename was withdrawn.
- `check_values` object for table values stands. Reshape needs a schema number bump; the reader accepts only the new number.

- t-tools: staged importer and builder need no change. ANCHOR_ empties must be exported with identity rotation, scale 1 and unique names.

### 15c: travel loop and lane exits as staged (2026-10-11; staged and offline-compiled, not run)

- Blocking item confirmed and fixed in stage: lane travel stepped once per frame and discarded the rest of the frame at a junction (no stop skipped, but a carried junction cost up to a frame). Now ClimbTravel.Advance (Scripts/Gameplay/Climb/ClimbTravel.cs) loops over the remaining travel; stop, gate or lane end ends the travel of the frame and zeroes speed; bound = number of lanes in the area, with a named error.
- Tests staged: EditMode ClimbTravelTests (two junctions in one 1/30 frame on a 33 deg bend; a 45.09 deg stop reached mid-frame both ways; same stop count and final lane at 1/30, 1/60, 1/240). The single-frame cases set entry speed to walk speed by hand, stated in the test: at the ramp speed a 1/30 frame beside a flight travels about 0.018 m, just under a 0.02 m segment. The built-tower PlayMode comparison across frame rates is the result of record, added when the manifest exists.
- Lane exits: ClampSnap PlayMode case through the real world-bounds clamp; EnteredWater in EditMode via one pure function for why climb state is dropped (moved externally, area off, in water, outside bounds, in that order).
- OPEN RISK: unverified whether the world-bounds clamp acts on a body whose CharacterController is disabled on a lane. If not, the design gate claim (section 6) that lane state self-heals on a clamp snap is wrong and becomes a finding.
- Build step derives bands and bend acceptance and stores the derived lists; it is compared with the manifest (accept_at_bend and band[] per sample) at the 0.5 mm float tolerance; over it fails the build. "Runtime does not recompute" means nothing per frame or at load.

### 15c: state before the scratch run (2026-10-11)

Confirmed by t-artist, all written and untested under the Blender stop: amendment in (stop_fallback_allowed = flight 5 head, flight 6 foot, flight 7 head; flight 8 foot no fallback, shortest d0 with curve and general_order_pick); rule 4 as the floor on d0; rule_3_rejections per attempt with nearest misses; run_out_final_segment_min 0.05 in the table; deterministic hill-climb; s_origin "lane_end" on inner mouths and "free_end" on the final mouth; schema 4 to 5; accept_at_bend in inner_mouths[0] and repeated in the junction record.

### 15c: band between samples, and one tolerance (2026-10-11, before the run)

- Found by comparing the two implementations before any build: the game takes the SMALLER neighbouring sample between two band samples (standing ruling; never wider than computed); the generator took segment 2 linearly when computing accept_at_bend. Difference up to 8 mm per 10 mm interval at the 40 deg slope.
- Ruling: the smaller-neighbour form stays the rule; the generator matches it everywhere it evaluates a band off-sample (accept_at_bend, reverse carry, bend and junction check rows, no-penetration sweep). check_values gains band_between_samples: "smaller_neighbour", asserted by the reader. Runtime unchanged.
- One tolerance for rule 4 and the accept comparison: the 0.5 mm in check_values, read by both sides (the staged junction check used 0.1 mm at the lip; the generator 0.5 mm). Margins still logged at full precision.
- Reader as staged: schema 5 only; check_values required with no fallback; s_origin asserted; lever check at 0.01 deg; empties found by exact manifest name with a duplicate check; setup file split into a second partial (was 499 lines).

### 15c: last alignments before the run (2026-10-11)

- Generator band off-sample = min(cap, smaller neighbouring reach sample after the running minimum, lip width + tan(40 deg) x exact distance). Sampled reach is stepped, slope term exact. Accepted provided the game does the same; t-engineer asked for a one-line confirmation (pending at the time of writing).
- No-penetration sweep stays on the linear band (wider than the game band), labelled in each record; wider is the safe direction.
- The one tolerance is check_values.band_tolerance 0.0005 (replaces envelope_penetration_slack): solid-radius allowance, rule 4, bend carry, and both build comparisons.
- band[] and reach[] exported at six decimals, not rounded to 1 mm (a 1 mm rounding alone could fail the 0.5 mm per-sample comparison). Rounding only in printed tables. A last-digit wobble between two builds is a T3 finding.
- Staged by t-engineer (not run): build step compares its own band at every sample with band[] and its accept width with accept_at_bend; detector accepted width asserted against accept_at_bend at 1 mm (t-qa tolerance); T-C1e asserts identical stop count and junction-crossing sequence at 1/30, 1/60, 1/240.
- Confirmed by t-engineer from the code (ClimbRules.Envelope, LookoutWatch.Envelope; read, not run): reach stepped, slope term exact from the continuous lane parameter, cap constant. Generator and game agree; neither changes. Pending item above closed.
- Reach precision (t-artist, disclosed before the run): reach was computed in whole millimetres; now stepped at 1 mm then bisected to 1e-7 within the last millimetre, six decimals exported. Every reach, lip width and reach-bound band grows by up to 1 mm against figures quoted earlier in this note. Accepted: reach is the physical fact. The old figure was conservative by up to 1 mm, so the no-penetration sweep at 0.2495 m is now the protecting check; a newly failing row is reported, not fixed by flooring. Comparison against e5c77c45 splits changes by cause: deck datum, two-segment run-outs, sub-millimetre reach, other (expected empty). Disclosed to t-qa as a pre-result change.
- Comparison run: build_mc39.py --whole-millimetre-reach (default off; manifest carries reach_bisection false; output out/compare-whole-mm/; never copied). It must run through build_mc39.ps1, not a typed Blender command line (wrapper environment: PYTHONHASHSEED=0, system-env, factory-startup; and the no-hand-made-steps rule). t-tools asked for a pass-through switch that refuses to write into out/dev. If the switch is not ready, part (c) of the comparison is reported as pending rather than run by hand. reachable_span_uncapped stays whole-millimetre, informational, read by nothing.
- DISCLOSURE (t-artist, 2026-10-11): scratch candidates d23b854f and e48af76a were started from a typed Blender command line (same seed and flags by their account), not through build_mc39.ps1. Never copied; not candidates. e5c77c45 unaffected. Figures in sections 15b and 15c that come from e48af76a (landing 6 at 9.3 mm and 45.00 deg, the N-2 failures, dev eye rows) are indications that led to rule changes, not figures of record. The landing 6 number in the ruling will be the wrapper-built one; a material difference, or under 5 mm, goes back to t-qa first. Reported to t-qa.
- All builds from here go through build_mc39.ps1 (t-tools added -WholeMillimetreReach; diff review pending). The pair for the byte-identical check and the copy will be -NoTextures dev builds, as e5c77c45 was, because the textured build stops on failing rows t-qa has not yet listed; to be named with the hash.
- t-qa on the disclosure: friction row 22 (process, self-disclosed, not an assist). The automatic-FAIL item applies only if something built outside the wrapper reaches the project; nothing has. Before the copy t-qa checks the receipt (wrapper build, input hashes, two-run identity, module-closure line). Landing 6 yes stands as a principle; back to t-qa if the wrapper-built margin differs materially from 9.3 mm, is under 5 mm, or the bend is below 44.92 deg. TO STATE IN THE LANE-END RULING: the export it names is a -NoTextures dev build, not the build of record for T3 or T4; the textured wrapper build must reproduce every lane-data value exactly (t-artist diff, checked by t-qa) before the import.
- Review, build_mc39.ps1 (t-tools) at SHA-256 908617e9e08f6be5ff6031b56057e96c220e49f053f071f6026e364be739c76a, 185 lines; read, not run: APPROVED. -WholeMillimetreReach passes the flag, defaults output to out/compare-whole-mm, is refused with -IntoAssets and when -Out is under out/build or out/dev (exit 3); receipt gains wholeMillimetreReach and buildOfRecord (false for -NoTextures or the comparison switch). ## 18. team-lead rulings of 2026-10-11 and the shutter ruling

team-lead, via t-producer:
- Top flight pitch: T1 row is 52 +/-2 deg on flights 1 to 7 and 56.3 +/-2 on the top flight (T-1006-A); stair width 2 ft 0 in; pre-result correction, not an assist. The lane-end ruling is issued on the 56.3 deg geometry with no pending mark; the cab exit lane (lane 9) stays.
- Blocker count: the join-mouth finding is part of the open occurrence; count stays 2 of 3. A red lane-end check on the build that follows the issued ruling is the third. Issue once: one hash, the regenerated table, the t-qa check.
- North barrier: no thickening, brightening or special drawing. If the cab view does not show it against the t-qa bar it is a recorded FAIL finding with numbers and twin stills. The cab fog curve is tuned for the look (t-director conditions), not bent to rescue the barrier.
- Five launches and the water skirt and haze scope are with Noah; no far-view slot until he answers.
- Roof: drawing roof final. t-artist adds top-hinged window shutters as static dressing in a second export after the lane-end export.
- Process: every slot measuring frame time or seeded counts gets a quiet order to each agent by name.

Shutter ruling (mine): shutters and stays carry NO collision proxies. They are outside the cab outer wall plane, every interactable is inside, and mesh-only dressing leaves the collider count and lane data untouched. Check rows required in the build:
1. every shutter and stay vertex outside the cab outer wall plane by a named table margin;
2. each hinged sash, through its full swing, clears shutter and stays by a named margin (if not, report; no thinning or cut swing without t-director and t-designer);
3. the second export changes no collider, lane, empty or lane-data value (field diff, checked by t-qa);
4. budgets hold (LOD0 at or under 60k tris, 4 materials or fewer, one atlas), cost stated, simplified at LOD1 and LOD2;
5. shutters are visual occluders physics cannot see: the manifest exports each open shutter outline, and any raycast view check (P3) treats rays crossing an outline as blocked; cab stills with twins are the proof. t-qa and t-level told; a pre-result correction to the test.
How far shutters are propped and what view they hide is for t-director at the look gate.

## 19. Proposed order for issuing the lane-end ruling (2026-10-11; my position, awaiting t-qa and team-lead)

Proposal from t-engineer via t-producer: the built-tower checks have never run, so their first run on the export is a shake-down; the ruling is called ISSUED only when the named lane-end checks are green. A red traced by t-qa to a defective check is not an occurrence; a red traced to the build before issue is inside open occurrence 2; a red on a lane-end check after issue is the third.

My position: agree, with four changes, all declared before any result.
1. The ruling text is frozen at step 1 (after the t-qa table check) and circulated as "final text, not issued"; nothing in it changes to suit a test result. A needed change is a defect inside occurrence 2 and restarts the sequence.
2. A check sorted "the check is wrong" must be shown red on a deliberately wrong input for its named defect before its rerun counts; each fix reviewed by me as a diff; loosening a tolerance or skipping a case is not a check fix.
3. The same check sorted "wrong" twice comes to t-qa and me before a third attempt.
4. The setup throwing at import, the two build comparisons at band_tolerance and the lever check are named lane-end checks.

Which build is imported: not the dev export (the wrapper refuses -IntoAssets with -NoTextures). Step 1 runs on the wrapper-built dev export. t-qa then lists the known failing rows in known_findings (between slots, t-producer told). t-artist runs the textured wrapper build twice; its lane data must equal the dev export exactly (t-artist diff, t-qa check, receipt with buildOfRecord true); the table script on it gives an empty diff. That textured build is imported once and is the build of record; no second import. A wrong check changes test code only; a wrong build means a new export and back to step 1.
- Section 18 follow-up: t-engineer confirms from the code that the aim ray is one cast against the sight layers stopping at the first collider, and the sash logic knows nothing of what is behind it; no interaction change for shutters. Shutter row 2 made precise: sash swing is checked mesh against mesh over the whole swing, smallest gap and its angle per side exported in the manifest (t-artist computes; the built-tower wiring test reads it; t-qa checks). Eye-clearance sweeps (C3, T-C2c) are redone with shutters present (t-qa). t-level probe models each shutter as a full-width analytic panel, values from the manifest once exported, failing if missing.

## 20. Far view after Noah answers (2026-10-11). Supersedes sections 7, 13 and 14 where they differ

Noah, via team-lead and t-producer: launches approved; "I dont' think we need to thin the fog. don't get to hung up on that instruction of having to see the water from 3 out of 4 directions." Effect: no fog thinning from the cab; no water skirt or height-keyed haze; P3 shoreline and north-barrier clauses are recorded, not gating (above-canopy clause stands); logged by t-producer as assist 2 of 3.

Re-ruling:

## 21. Shake-down and issue order as ruled by team-lead (2026-10-11). Supersedes section 19 where they differ

1. ONE shake-down run, declared before it starts, on the first import of the wrapper-built export, to debug the never-run checks.
2. t-qa sorts each red as "check is wrong" or "build is wrong" with written evidence per red (an independent measurement that contradicts the check). A red without such evidence is a build red.
3. Check-defect reds are not an occurrence: fixed and disclosed.
4. Build-defect reds on the shake-down are part of open occurrence 2 and get ONE fix round (generator and/or lane data). Then I ISSUE the lane-end ruling naming the hash. Issuing is the declared fix; it is not withheld waiting for green.
5. The first full run of the lane-end checks on the issued hash tests the fix. A lane-end red there that t-qa attributes to the build is the THIRD occurrence, automatic FAIL; same for any later hash.
6. Rebuilds for unrelated reasons (shutters, G2 fixes) must not change collider or lane data (t-qa diff); if lane data changes, the lane-end checks rerun under rule 5.

Withdrawn from section 19: "issued only when green" and "back to step 1 on a build red". Kept: text circulated as "final text, not issued" after the t-qa table check, nothing changed to suit a test result; a corrected check shown able to fail, each check fix reviewed by me as a diff; a check sorted wrong twice comes to t-qa and me; setup throws, band[] and accept_at_bend comparisons and the lever check are named lane-end checks; the import path (dev export for hash, table and check; known_findings listed by t-qa between slots; textured wrapper build twice identical with a lane-data diff; imported once as build of record). Open: t-artist to confirm that listing known_findings changes no lane-data field and leaves the FBX byte-identical.

### P4-QA-1 (wildlife seed 101: 113 twice with the code landing out, 114 three times with it in; stands against the landing; mechanism open)

Facts from the per-sample evidence (TestResults/tower-qa/landout and code1; my comparison, reading only):

- Seed 202 landing out, full1 against full2: 21 samples differ (count 180 both). Seed 303 landing in, full1 against full2: 2 samples differ. The full-order walk is not bit-repeatable between identical runs on those seeds.
- Filtered run with the landing in is exact (t-qa) and the fixture order was identical in every full run. My test-order hypothesis is WITHDRAWN.
Candidate mechanism (one piece of code evidence, no proof): Assets/Scripts/Wildlife/WildlifeHerd.cs lines 55-58 gate each herd leg on absolute Time.time (float) against lastLegTime; at timeScale 3 after the fish and performance fixtures the value is large, so the frame a herd sets off on depends on the game time elapsed before the walk. The landing would then be a trigger through frames elapsed in earlier fixtures, not through anything done to animals.
Tests proposed: (1) landing out, same full command with one existing short PlayMode fixture listed twice before the walk (no code or test change); (2) t-qa variant: runtime code in, Lookout test assemblies out (expires once the tower is wired in, so it goes first if only one slot is available). t-producer caps the investigation at two slots. I would accept "cause not found, recorded as a P4 finding" after two empty tests, with these facts recorded, no code changed to chase the number, and the follow-up naming W-QA-1 (absolute time in the herd code). No grading change asked.

### Lookout tower: lane-end ruling. TEXT FOR ISSUE (not issued)

Author: t-td, 2026-10-11. This is one folded text: every correction from t-engineer, t-artist and t-qa is inside the parts, not appended. Parts A to D and F are fixed from now and do not change to suit a test result. Part E is filled from the first wrapper-built export after the t-qa table check; then the text is circulated as "final text, not issued". It is issued by t-td naming the hash under the shake-down protocol ruled by team-lead (one shake-down, at most one build fix round, then issue).

[FILL] Export named: manifest SHA-256 ........ (wrapper-built -NoTextures dev export; not the build of record for T3 or T4). Lane-data digest ........ Interaction-data digest ........ Schema 5. Table TestResults/tower-eng/lane-end-table-HASH.txt, checked by t-qa on ........
[FILL] Build imported for the shake-down (textured, wrapper-built twice identical): manifest SHA-256 ........, same lane-data digest.

#### Part A. What every lane end guarantees

R1. (a) The hand-over point, where the player returns to the ordinary controller, is clear at 0.30 m (radius 0.25 plus skin 0.05) from every wall and at least 0.30 m from every fall edge. (b) Every position the band allows on a lane is one where a body at wall_reach 0.25 m intersects no wall (no intersection at wall_reach less band_tolerance, 0.2495 m). "Wall" means every COL_ and CLIP_ proxy a body on that deck can touch, found geometrically from the proxy list; the only exclusion is the named overlap of C6. This holds on flights, on final segments, on first segments and on lane 9 outside its named overlap (C6).
   A fall edge is a stair-mouth edge or any stretch of a deck outline not covered by a rail-plane wall.
R1c. Which bar applies depends on whether the body has physics, never on the kind of wall. Where the ordinary controller is live or becomes live by design (hand-over points, the crossing between the two hand-over points of a landing, the far end of a built-in run, the ground hand-over point, the free end of lane 9): 0.30 m from every wall and fall edge. On every lane line where the body is carried (flights, both run-out segments, built-in runs short of their far end, lane 9 outside its named overlap): wall_reach from every COL_ and CLIP_ proxy, no intersection at wall_reach less band_tolerance. Each segment and each flight line has a check row with its smallest clearance, the proxy it is to and the margin over wall_reach (warning under 5 mm). Agreed by t-qa before any result as a correction of a target the honest geometry could not meet (flight lane ends on landing 7 are 0.2928 m and 0.281 m from the wall end of the neighbouring flight).
R2. Landings 1 to 4 and the ground: the level run is part of the flight lane, collinear with it, length built_in_run (0.34 m, from the table); hand-over only at its far end. The ground hand-over point is the far end of that run.
R3. Landings 5, 6, 7: a linked level run-out from the manifest, shaped and selected by part B, level within 0.01 m on its deck. No hand-over at a junction.
R4. A junction joins exactly two lane ends (coincident within 1 mm); no junction joins two inclined flights. At a junction the carry test is evaluated first, before the on-lane rule: the player is carried onto the next lane if it is open and the input is within the lever of it (laneJunctionAlong 0.82, acos = 34.9152 deg). Otherwise the player stays on the current lane, where input along it in either direction moves them by the ordinary on-lane rule (laneAlongDeadband 0.2, laneFullSpeedAlong 0.5), and input along neither holds them at the junction. A stop must be a real corner: bend at least the lever angle + 10 deg. No bend between the carry limit and that floor (no twitch stops). t-director ceilings: carried at most 40 deg toward the next flight, 30 deg away.
R4a. A collinear junction (a flight and the first segment of its run-out, built collinear by construction and marked so in the manifest) is not a corner: there is no wish test at it; travel that reaches it carries on if the next lane is open, and whether the player moves at all is the ordinary on-lane rule (laneAlongDeadband) of the lane they stand on, in both directions, as if the two lanes were one. A player who releases the input just short of it coasts across while slowing, and it is never reported as a stop. The lever and the carry-first test apply to bends only. The generator marks each junction collinear or bend in the manifest. The game decides it from its own lanes (local frame, double precision) at collinear_tolerance_deg (0.1, a table value asserted equal to the game constant; far above float precision on a 0.02 m segment and far below any bend), and the setup throws if any junction disagrees with the manifest mark. Nothing extra is serialized.
R4b. At a junction the two carry cones must not overlap, or a player would be passed to and fro: turn less than 180 deg minus twice the lever angle (110.17 deg at lever 0.82; the cab junction is 104.31). Asserted from the manifest turns and the tuning asset in TheLever_IsInsideTheRangeThisLayoutAllows.
R5. Each landing keeps its two hand-over points at least 0.30 m apart, and the straight line between them holds 0.30 m from every wall and every fall edge along its whole length. Run-outs on one landing do not cross.
R6. An end with no point satisfying R1 fails by name. No shortening, no exception.
R7. Junction principle: at every junction, the band of each lane at its junction end lies inside what the other lane accepts there (carried position, both directions, both sides, within band_tolerance).
Margin target run_out_margin_target 10 mm on every hand-over clearance; warning under 5 mm; band_tolerance 0.5 mm. The target is a robustness target, not the bar; the bar is 0.30 m.

#### Part B. Linked run-outs (the rule the generator runs)

B1. Shape: P0 (the lane end of the flight, on the lip), P1 (the bend), P2 (the hand-over point). Segment 1, P0 to P1, is collinear with the flight, length d0 at least run_out_first_segment_min (0.02 m). Segment 2, P1 to P2, at least run_out_final_segment_min (0.05 m). No turn at P0.
B2. Hard limits: R1(a) at P2; every point of both segments at least wall_reach from every wall (R1c); R5 enforced in the pair test. The smallest margin of a pair counts the hand-over and crossing clearances against 0.30 m and each segment line clearance against wall_reach.
B3. Bands, per side, at wall_reach. reach(s) is how far across the segment a body at wall_reach can stand on the deck, from one reach routine, bisected to sub-millimetre, exported at six decimals; it is 0 where the line of the lane itself is nearer a wall than wall_reach.
  - Segment 2: band2(s) = min(cap_side 0.305, reach2(s), lip2 + tan(40 deg) x distance from s to P1), running minimum from the free end; lip2 = reach2 at P1.
  - Bend: accept1 per side (exported as accept_at_bend) is the largest w, no more than reach1 at P1, such that a body at P1 + w across segment 1, re-expressed in the frame of segment 2 (along t, across a), has abs(a) no more than band2 at max(t, 0) on the side a falls on. Reverse direction: band2 at P1 carried into segment 1 must lie within accept1 on the side it falls; if not, the candidate is rejected (band2 is not shrunk).
  - Segment 1: band1(s) = min(0.305, reach1(s), accept1 + tan(40 deg) x distance from s to P1), running minimum from P0.
  - Between samples a band takes the smaller neighbouring reach sample (after the running minimum); the slope term is exact at the position; the cap is constant. Same in generator, game and detector (band_between_samples = smaller_neighbour).
B4. band1 at P0 equals the lip width of the flight (foot_lip / head_lip) on each side, within band_tolerance; otherwise the candidate is rejected. This sets a floor on d0: band1 at P0 is at most accept1 + tan(40 deg) x d0.
B5. Bend angle b at P1. Carried toward the next flight only if b is at most carry_bend_max_toward (34.92) less bend_limit_guard_deg (0.01); carried away only if b is at most carry_bend_max_away (30.00); a fallback stop only if b is at least stop_bend_min_lever (44.92) plus the guard; frozen stops are searched at stop_bend_min (45.00) or more; nothing between. The guard pairs with the game check that acos(laneJunctionAlong) is within 0.01 deg of carry_bend_max_toward, so generator and game decide the same for every lever that passes it.
B6. Frozen behaviour, searched first: landing 5 carried at both ends; landing 6 a stop at both ends; landing 7 carried (flight 7 head toward, flight 8 foot away).
B7. Selection, two-level. First the pair of hand-over points of the landing, ordered by the smallest of the two points clearance margins (against 0.30 m) and the crossing margins. Then, for each end with its hand-over point fixed, the longest d0 on the grid for which the whole run-out meets B1 to B6. Segment line clearance is a requirement (R1c) with its row and warning, not part of the ordering. Exception, flight 8 foot only: the shortest d0 that yields a valid pair, searched ascending from 0.02 m in 0.005 m steps and stopped at the first that passes; the failing lengths before it are printed as the curve. (The general-order pick and the cost to flight 7 head, once asked for as information, are withdrawn: each was a whole extra search.)
B8. Fallback (t-director, ruling/lookout-tower-upper-landing-step-on-final-amend3). (a) Landing 5 both ends and flight 7 head: with no carried solution, a stop only with the bend turning toward the next flight, per B5; a finding to t-producer and t-director, nothing copied. (b) Flight 8 foot (landing 7 coming down, away): no stop fallback; over 30.00 goes to t-director and t-producer by name with the curve, nothing copied.
B9. Search and build. The search is a tool in the repo, not part of the build. Two claims, both stated in the block: the grid result is exact on the 5 mm grid (the first pair in the order of B7 for which both ends have a passing d0 and the run-outs do not touch); then that one pair has its hand-over points refined below the grid to 0.01 mm on the pair margin, d0 re-derived, the refined pair kept only if every rule still holds, otherwise the grid pair stands (one seed, not several). Its cost is bounded by work per lane end: pairs of hand-over points taken in the order of B7; for each end and hand-over point the result (the preferred passing d0, or none) computed once and remembered; the d0 range given in closed form by the bend class (the bend is monotonic in d0 for a fixed hand-over point), floored by rule B4 and capped by the deck; cheap necessary conditions before the full band test; the first pair for which both ends have a d0 and the run-outs do not touch is the answer; exhaustive when the pairs are exhausted. A coarser grid is not used as the grid of record (a thin feasible set could be missed). It reports per landing whether it was exhaustive. Its result is stored in the parameter table as the block run_out_layout: only the free choices (the run-out points, full precision), written only by the tool, with provenance (tool and module hashes, the hash of the inputs searched against, grid constants, behaviour found, exhaustive or not, counts, the curve for flight 8 foot). Every build verifies the block through the same rule functions the search calls and fails naming the rule if the stored layout breaks one; it fails if the block was not written by the tool, or if the hash of the inputs differs from the one recorded (searched against different geometry: rerun the tool). A build of record refuses a block from a non-exhaustive search. The reproducibility check reruns the tool once and requires a byte-identical block. No length or angle in the search or the build is typed where the table names it.

#### Part C. Bands on flights, free ends, special ends

C1. Envelope at a free end, per side: allowed(s) = min(cap_side 0.305, reach(s), width at the lip or junction + tan(laneGlideMaxDegrees 40) x distance from it), running minimum from the free end. Clamp is by position, never outward, holds when stopped. Join is inside the envelope by construction.
C2. Flight band: the lip width at each end easing to lane_lateral_slack (0.05) over lane_taper_length (0.30). Both are table values asserted equal to the tuning asset.
C3. Guards at the cross-section of the free end only: envelope_mouth_guard 0.24 m (regression guard) and envelope_deck_inset_min 0.06 m (more than one frame of towerWalkSpeed at 1/30). There is no drop cap and no 0.26 m floor.
C4. Ground end: envelope at wall_reach; the 0.40 m full-body reach is a separate list describing positions before the join (ground_full_body_joinable); the 0.40 m full-body clearance check applies at the ground hand-over point.
C5. Top flight (56.3 deg, the T1 row as ruled by team-lead) under the hatch: the band is at least the fit profile under_opening from the manifest, never under lane_lateral_slack, easing to the lip width at each end (hatch 1.50 x 0.66 m; west side bounded by the rail-plane wall of the flight).
C6. Lane 9 (cab exit): flat on the cab floor from the head of flight 8 to a free end 0.30 m clear. NAMED EXCEPTION (t-qa): the capsule overlaps the named south wall proxy only, by at most the wall_overlap_max stated on lane 9, which is itself at or under exit_lane_wall_overlap_max (0.13 m); asserted every 2 cm, never increasing toward the free end, zero there. Overlap with anything else fails. The hatch gate also gates lane 9.
C7. Lane 9 at the cab junction: its width at the junction end is accept_at_junction per side, the largest offset, no more than its reach there, whose carried position on flight 8 lies inside the band of flight 8 where it lands; its band is min(cap_side, reach(s), accept_at_junction + tan(40 deg) x distance from the junction), running minimum from the free end. Expected 0 on both sides (the reach there is already 0 because of the overlap of C6).
C8. Deck datum (t-director): girts on the drawn levels, every walking surface 0.0508 m above its steel; flights keep risers and plan position.
C9. The side walls of flight 8 stop at the underside of the cab floor. No proxy stands in the cab walkable volume other than the cab walls, the firefinder pedestal, the hatch leaf and the swing blocker. There is no guard, drawn or invisible, at the open hatch (t-director, ruling/lookout-tower-open-hatch-no-guard).

#### Part D. Runtime and checks

D1. Lane travel in a frame is a loop over the remaining distance. At each junction: re-express the point in the frame of the next lane (no feet jump), clamp by that band (never outward), junction test, carry or stop, continue. A stop, a gate or a lane end ends the travel of the frame. A carried junction moves the feet by the frame travel only. Evidence: stop count and junction sequence at the four proof steps (1/30, 1/60, 1/120, 1/240; one list, LookoutTargets.ProofDeltaTimes) asserted on the tower by the T-C1e climb; stop count and ending lane at the same steps on a synthetic graph in EditMode.
D2. Lane state ends only through EndLane(reason): FreeEnd, Respawn, ClampSnap, AreaDisabled, MovedExternally, EnteredWater, LeftAreaBounds.
D3. The game setup throws, naming the end, side and both figures or the missing key, on: any lane end, run end or band corner outside the area bounds less the capsule radius; check_values.body_height differing from the Player controller height; collinear_tolerance_deg differing from the game constant; a junction whose computed kind disagrees with the manifest mark; schema other than 5; a missing check_values object or a missing wall_reach, envelope_mouth_guard, envelope_deck_inset_min, band_tolerance, bend_limit_guard_deg, carry_bend_max_toward, lane_lateral_slack, lane_taper_length; band_between_samples other than smaller_neighbour; s_origin other than lane_end on inner_mouths or free_end on mouth records; acos(laneJunctionAlong) more than 0.01 deg from carry_bend_max_toward; lane_lateral_slack or lane_taper_length not equal to the tuning asset; its own derived band differing from band[] at any sample, or its own accept width differing from accept_at_bend or accept_at_junction, by more than band_tolerance; at a built-in run, the last reach sample differing from foot_lip / head_lip by more than band_tolerance; half_width_cap or slope_deg on a mouth record differing from the tuning asset; stretch_length differing from the built lane; R7 failing at any junction. Band, lip, accept and lever throws name the end, side and both figures; missing-key throws name the key. Nothing is computed per frame or at load.
D4. Named lane-end checks. A red on one of these, attributed by t-qa to the build, on the issued hash, is the third occurrence of the lane-end blocker:
  - the setup throwing at import (all of D3);
  - LaneLinks_AreSound
  - Junctions_AreCarriedOrRealCorners_AndStepsAwayAreSmall
  - TheLever_IsInsideTheRangeThisLayoutAllows
  - FreeLaneEnds_AndTheirRuns_AreClearForTheCapsule
  - HandOverPoints_ReportTheirMargins
  - OpenStretches_AreGapsBetweenTheBuiltLandingWalls_FlightSideWallsNotCounted
  - JoinEnvelopes_AreClearAtWallReach_AndCoverTheReachableSpan
  - ReachProfiles_AreTheBuiltWallsOnTheirOwnSide
  - RunOutEmpties_AreWhereTheManifestSaysWithinAMillimetre
  - UnderTheHatch_EveryPositionTheBodyFitsIsInsideTheBand
  - LevelRuns_LieOnTheirDecks
  - ExitLane_OverlapsOnlyTheNamedWall_LessAndLessTowardItsFreeEnd
  - InnerSegments_AreClearAtWallReachAcrossTheirBand
  - InnerSegments_ReachIsTheBuiltWallsOnItsOwnSide
  - LandingCrossings_HoldTheBarFromWallsAndFallEdges
  - LaneLines_AreClearAtWallReachAgainstEveryProxy (run-out segments, built-in runs short of their far end, lane 9 outside its named overlap)
  - ControllerGoingLiveMidLane_AtTheTightestLineClearance_StandsAndWalksAway (PlayMode)
  Not lane-end checks (agreed with t-qa): Lanes_LieOnTheVisualNosingLine (flights against treads; a build red there is its own blocker at occurrence 1); FlightLines_AreClearAtWallReachAgainstEveryProxyButTheirOwnFloor (flight lines and bands at wall_reach; the wall set excludes that flight own floor proxies, by the manifest list floor_proxies, and nothing else; its own blocker); Wiring_HasEveryPart_AndNoMissingReference; RingNumerals_StandAtTheirBearings; AimRay_RangeOcclusionAndLayers; MovingParts_RestWhereTheManifestSays_Unscaled (each mover rest position within 1 mm of its manifest pivot, and its rest rotation); the Negative_ controls; every eye-clearance row (a known finding).
D5. Generator check rows: run_room passes where the straight run meets run_room_min, or the end has a linked run-out or exit lane on which every run-out row passes (band sweep, segment minimums, margin at target, bend rule, lane 9 overlap, hand-over clearance and crossing, band at the lip against the flight lip, bend carry both ways, the two free-end guards, frozen behaviour); a run_room row that depends on a red row is itself red. The old step-off eye row is a note on the eye rows along lane 9 and through the cab junction.
D6. Where lane state ends. In play lane state ends with the player left in place only at the far point of a free end (a hand-over point, 0.30 m clear by R1a). Respawn and the world-bounds snap relocate the player; nothing in game code disables a ClimbArea in play; a shut gate holds the player on the lane; leaving lane 1 at its foot is a free end; water does not reach the tower. LeftAreaBounds cannot occur on the tower because the setup throws unless every lane end, run end and band corner lies inside the area bounds (area_min / area_max) by at least area_lane_margin (a table value equal to the tower capsule radius) in plan, with the feet at or above the bottom and feet + body_height at or below the top; the generator checks the same (row lanes_inside_area) so the box and the lanes cannot disagree at build time either.
D7. The controller going live beside a wall (t-qa measure, restated after a calibration probe result and before any tower result; in the probe a tower-radius body released from a lane inside its skin distance of a wall was put out to radius + skin on its first Move, while an authored-radius body placed at its radius from the wall was not moved at all: the mechanism is NOT established): (a) at most one displacement (none is also a pass) of at most skin width + band_tolerance, within 10 deg of the outward normal of the nearest proxy in plan, complete within 2 frames counted from the first Move of the controller after it is switched on (which can be the frame after it goes live); (b) thereafter drift under 0.01 m in plan for 5 s; (c) where it comes to rest it is not in solid (axis at least the radius less band_tolerance from every proxy), it is on its deck with the axis at least the radius inside every fall edge, and it is at rest so that (b) holds from there (a live body legitimately rests nearer than radius + skin after a slide, 0.2853 m in the probe); (d) no in-solid report, no launch; (e) walks at least 0.05 m along the lane within 1 s of W. A second displacement, a displacement toward any proxy or larger than (a) allows, oscillation, an in-solid frame, failing to walk away, or a resting place failing (c) is a C2 red and a layout finding. The test runs at every tight point of D8. Height change on release from an incline is reported beside it.
D8. Free width. The generator reports per carried line the smallest free width across the line between the nearest proxies on opposite sides (row line_free_width): it fails under twice wall_reach anywhere, and under tight_free_width (0.60 m, twice radius + skin) at any hand-over point; every other point under 0.60 m is listed under tight_points. A tight point is acceptable only because lane state cannot end in place there (D6).

#### Part E. As built on the named export. [FILL from the table after the t-qa check]

- Landings 1 to 4 and ground: built-in run, straight on. Smallest margins [FILL] mm. Ground hand-over clearance [FILL] m (was +0.209 at the typed 0.30 m point).
- Landing 5 (flight 5 head, flight 6 foot): d0 [FILL] m; bend [FILL] deg toward; carried [FILL]; smallest margin [FILL] mm; crossing [FILL] m, nearest fall edge [FILL] m.
- Landing 6 (flight 6 head, flight 7 foot): d0 [FILL] m; bend [FILL] deg; stop; smallest margin [FILL] mm; crossing [FILL] m, nearest fall edge [FILL] m.

- Landing 7, flight 8 foot: shortest d0 [FILL] m; bend [FILL] deg away, room under 30.00 [FILL] deg; carried [FILL]; smallest margin [FILL] mm; line clearance of the layout picked [FILL] m (the best line clearance passed over under the earlier ordering is not computed); crossing [FILL] m, nearest fall edge [FILL] m.

Named exceptions and under-target ends. Exactly two are agreed with t-qa; anything else that the table shows comes to t-qa by name with its numbers before this text is called final, and is not confirmed by appearing here:
1. Landing 6, NAMED UNDER-TARGET END: smallest hand-over margin [FILL] mm against the 10 mm target (indication from an earlier scratch run: 9.3 mm), bend [FILL] deg, [FILL] deg above the stop floor. Reason: the no-twitch rule against 0.10 m of drawn deck. Conditions (t-qa): margin and bend measured on the built colliders and lanes, warning under 5 mm; nobody moves the 45.0, the release rule or the target to recover millimetres without t-qa. Back to t-qa before issue if the margin differs materially from 9.3 mm, is under 5 mm, or the bend is under the floor.

[FILL] Any fallback under B8, by name, with the finding reference; or "none".

Dependencies: wall_reach 0.25 m (measured by probe: a sliding capsule rests at its radius); the two guards of C3; the deck datum of C8.

#### Part F. What this ruling does not cover

- Eye clearance and the near plane in the climb area (separate decision; known finding against the 0.20 m bar).
- T3 and T4: the named export is a dev build. The build of record is the final visual export, which must print the same lane-data digest (team-lead rule 6).
- Shutters: visual only, no collision proxies; they must leave both digests unchanged.
- Lanes against treads (Lanes_LieOnTheVisualNosingLine).

## 27. After ALL CLEAR (2026-10-11, 18:05Z onward)

### Cab junction: the carry test comes first (pre-result behaviour change, by t-director requirement)

- Question from t-director (ruling/lookout-tower-junction-behaviour-as-built): where the going-on and going-back cones overlap, which wins. Turn at the cab junction 104.31 deg. Going-on cone (within the lever of lane 9): 69.4 to 139.2 deg from the heading of flight 8. Going-back zone on flight 8 (along at or under minus laneAlongDeadband 0.2): from 101.5 deg. Overlap 101.5 to 139.2; the direction of lane 9 itself is inside it (cos 104.31 = -0.247).
- Landed code, my reading (Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/Climb/PlayerController.Climb.cs lines 155 to 165): the step along the current lane runs first; with the wish along lane 9 the target is negative, laneU drops below length, and the junction test (laneU at or over length, then PassOnto) is false. Going back wins in the overlap. A sweep with W held is carried at 69 deg and never meets it (what T-C1i exercises); a player who stops at the head, faces the cab floor and presses W is walked back down the stair. Mirror on lane 9 at the junction, steering straight down the stair (75.7 deg from lane 9 reversed, cos 0.247).
- RULING: at a linked lane end the carry test is evaluated BEFORE the step along the current lane. Within the lever of the next lane and open, the player is passed on; otherwise the ordinary on-lane rule. On this tower zones overlap only where a bend exceeds about 66.5 deg, that is the cab junction. R4 in section 26 is to read "the carry test comes first"; this is a change to R4 made before any result and disclosed to t-qa as a pre-result behaviour change, not a wording fix.
- t-engineer to confirm what the staged ClimbTravel.Advance does; if it steps first, fix and tests (stationary at the head of flight 8 steering along lane 9; stationary on lane 9 at the junction steering down the stair; hatch not fully open: not passed on) join the compile pass. t-designer adds a row and one spec sentence; the G6 capture includes stop, look, press forward.
- t-designer: T-C1 rows otherwise match the behaviour as built. t-director: accepted as built apart from this point.

### Two defects in the accepted dev state e5c77c45, found in the generator debugging pass (2026-10-11, before any candidate)

1. Reach ignored the side walls of the flights. Reach, lips and bands on a landing were computed against the landing rail-plane walls only, not CLIP_CVX_FlightNN_01 / _02 (0.3302 m either side of each flight line, ending at the lip). A body on the lip line is wall_reach clear of the wall end only to 0.3302 - 0.25 = 0.0802 m; the manifest said 0.1056, and 0.305 / about 0.287 on the two open sides (flight 7 head left, flight 8 foot right). The false wide lip was driving the landing 7 result through rule B4 and would have failed the built-wall sweeps at import. RULING: the wall set for reach, lips, bands, accept figures and the no-penetration sweep is every COL_ and CLIP_ proxy a body on that deck can touch, found geometrically from the proxy list (height range of the body), not a list of families; the named overlap of lane 9 is the only exclusion; reach_bound_by names the proxy. Figures in this note that rest on the old lips are superseded. The comparison lists old against new lips per end. One wall function for built-in runs, flight bands and run-outs.
2. Side walls of flight 8 rise above the cab floor (2.2 m above the nosing line to the head): two invisible walls out of the hatch opening along its east and west edges. A DEFECT IN MY PROXY SCHEME (section 6), which never considered that the head of flight 8 is inside the cab. It would block the cab walk (C3), cross the closed leaf and make lane 9 overlap a second proxy. RULING: they stop at the underside of the cab floor; below it they stay (they guide a body dropping through the hatch). Named collider change. t-artist also checks for any other proxy in the cab walkable volume and any flight wall above its upper deck outside the stair mouth. Nothing now stands round the open hatch except the designed drop-in; a drawn guard would be for t-director.
3. Also: the no-penetration sweep moves to the stepped band the game uses (with reach on the limit the linear band went a fraction of a millimetre negative on lane 9); a stronger deterministic search for landing 6.
4. t-director accepted the going-on-first ruling at the cab corner (ruling/lookout-tower-junction-behaviour-as-built-amend1) and withdrew "accepted as built" for the cab corner only. t-engineer has not yet confirmed what the staged ClimbTravel does.

### Folded into section 26 in place (all before any result)

- R4: the carry test is evaluated first, before the on-lane rule (t-director requirement; t-qa samples the stationary cases across the whole overlap every 2 deg at three frame rates, with a negative control and the overlap threshold asserted from the manifest over every junction; the back-out input is defined per junction as inside the go-back zone and outside the carry cone).

Asserted in TheLever_IsInsideTheRangeThisLayoutAllows from manifest and tuning. Bounds the lever from below on this layout: laneJunctionAlong above about 0.79 (derived in the test, not typed).
- R4a (new, folded into section 26), from a finding by t-engineer: collinear junctions (flight to first run-out segment, 0.00 deg) took the lever. Going up with input 35 to 78 deg off the line would have stopped the player at the head of the flight on landings 5 and 7 (ends the table calls carried); backing out of the landing 6 stop would have walked the player 2 to 9 cm to the lip and held them. RULING: a junction whose turn is within bend_limit_guard_deg of zero is not a corner; it is passed by the ordinary on-lane rule in both directions as if the two lanes were one; the lever applies to bends only; speed continuous. I had specified the collinear first segment without saying the junction test must not apply to it. Tests for both cases at three frame rates. Table and T-C1a: a collinear junction is its own row, never a turn or a stop.

### Clearance along lane lines (2026-10-11; ruled, PENDING t-qa agreement; not yet folded into section 26)

- Fact from t-artist (arithmetic from lane ends and rail planes; harness only, no figure of record): with the flight side walls counted, the lane end of flight 7 on landing 7 is 0.2928 m from the end of the west side wall of flight 8, and the lane end of flight 8 is 0.281 m from the end of the east side wall of flight 7; on landing 6 the second segment passes about 0.29 m from the end of the inner side wall of flight 6. B2 as written (every point of both segments 0.30 m from every wall) cannot be met by any lane leaving those lips; it was met in e5c77c45 only because those walls were not counted.
- RULING (subject to t-qa): the bar depends on whether the body has physics, not on the kind of wall. Where the ordinary controller is live, 0.30 m (radius + skin) from every wall and fall edge: hand-over points, the crossing, the far end of a built-in run, the ground hand-over point, the free end of lane 9. On a lane line (flight, both run-out segments, built-in run before its far end, lane 9 outside its named overlap), wall_reach 0.25 m from every wall: no intersection at wall_reach less band_tolerance. The old 0.30 m along the line was a width margin, not a safety bar; the band already allows the body at wall_reach from a wall. The margin is kept in the selection (segment line clearance minus wall_reach joins the smallest-margin figure) and exported per segment and per flight line with a check row (segment_line_clear).
- Rejected: 0.30 m from landing walls only; and 0.25 m from flight walls with 0.30 m from landing walls. Both split walls by family.
- Asked of t-engineer: which tests sweep a lane line at 0.30 m against the built colliders (FreeLaneEnds_AndTheirRuns_AreClearForTheCapsule suspected); they would go red on landings 6 and 7.
- Early warning, no ruling: with honest reach landing 7 probably cannot keep "carried"; B8 would be invoked by name from a wrapper build. Two free-end mouth-guard rows on landing 7 look set to fail; reported with numbers, not capped. Run-out lines closer than band_tolerance count as crossing; smallest separation exported; no minimum named now.
- Generator runtime: about six minutes per build for the landing 7 search; candidate builds, seven controls and the comparison build all run in full (no skip option): over an hour of Blender.

Their reading: memory, since with carried segments allowed down to 0.25 m many more candidates pass the hard limits and the search keeps them all. NOT evidence about whether landings 6 and 7 have a layout. Plan proposed to t-producer: a 30-second diagnostic with no search (candidate counts per end; best hand-over clearance per deck), then one landing at a time, landing 7 first.
- RULING on capping the candidate lists: a cap by each candidate own margin is a heuristic (the pair margin also depends on the crossing and on the lines not touching). It may bound the search but never be the source of a "no layout" verdict. Preferred: an exact bound (a pair smallest margin cannot exceed either candidate own margin: sort descending, evaluate best-first, discard every candidate whose own margin is at or under the best valid pair found; keep only the numbers needed, compute bands for tested pairs), which keeps B7 true on the grid and stays deterministic. If a cap is still needed: a named build constant (candidate_cap), per end whether it was reached, the selection labelled "not proven optimal on the grid"; a landing where the cap was reached and no pair found is "search inconclusive" and is rerun exactly (coarser grid if needed) until the answer is a proven yes or no on a stated grid; each attempt states whether it was exhaustive. The two diagnostic numbers become permanent manifest rows. A time limit in a wrapper build fails the build; it never returns a partial answer.

### Decisions after the probe frames were read again (2026-10-13)

- CORRECTION OF FACT (t-engineer, from the frames at 1/240): the hand-over body is NOT left at 0.2500 m. It is 0.2500 m from the wall only while on the lane and for the one live frame in which no Move is called; on its first Move it goes to 0.3000 m and walks the wall at 0.3000 m. My statements to t-qa and t-producer that "the hand-over case shows no push" were wrong and are withdrawn with both. Mechanism, as a statement: the first Move of the engine controller after being switched on inside its skin distance of a wall puts it at radius + skin; game code writes no position on that path (ending lane state switches the controller on; FitCapsule only sets the radius and logged "capsule radius is still 0.25 ... blocked" a second later); a live frame without a Move does nothing. Both probe cases had a downward component in that first Move; a level-lane released case (wanted, not in slot 12) would separate the two.
- t-qa DECISION, as their own change to their own condition: the T-C1j drift measure is restated; R1c stands. Fixed measure (D7 in section 26): (a) one displacement of at most skin width + band_tolerance within 10 deg of the outward normal of the nearest proxy in plan, complete within the first 2 live frames; (b) then drift under 0.01 m in plan for 5 s; (c) at rest at least radius + skin less band_tolerance from every proxy, on its deck, radius + skin inside every fall edge; (d) no in-solid, no launch; (e) at least 0.05 m along the lane within 1 s of W. My objection to (c) is WITHDRAWN: it rested on the misread hand-over figure and on a body being pressed along a wall, not one at rest. Disclosed by t-qa as restated after a calibration probe result and before any tower result, old and new side by side.

GAP found by t-engineer and ruled in: a setup throw unless every lane end, run end and band corner lies inside the area bounds by at least the capsule radius (D3), with a negative control. A shut gate holds the player on the lane; leaving lane 1 at its foot is a free end; the open-hatch drop-in is a join.

The one thin margin: the floor is 0.5 m below the base, so a ground hand-over more than 0.25 m below the foot of lane 1 would trip it; today it is level. On the last export a lane leaving the box could not have happened; nothing guaranteed that before the throw.

Gating for the restatement: H30, H30D, H31, L30, P30; a step at H30 or H30D voids it. Placed and two-wall cases reported, not gating.
