### team-lead bound on the landing 7 search, and the design question (2026-10-13)

- State: three failures of the landing 7 search so far (one crash, two five-minute stops). team-lead bound: (1) first the count of PAIRS of hand-over points on landing 7 that pass the crossing rule alone; zero means no layout with two hand-over points there and goes straight to team-lead with the geometry; (2) ONE more attempt with the exact best-first prune, crossing test once per pair, band test last, under the five-minute limit; (3) if inconclusive, t-artist, t-director and I bring team-lead through t-producer one message: what is known for certain and two or three costed options (landing 7 a deliberate stop both ways with a t-director ruling on the descending case; a simpler fixed-form run-out for landings 6 and 7 with hand-authored table parameters; one named rule relaxed on that landing, with numbers).
- The exact prune, written out for t-artist: candidate = a run-out passing the cheap single-end rules (B1, R1a, R1c, B5 class with the guard), own margin m = min(hand-over clearance - 0.30, each segment line clearance - wall_reach); pair valid if the crossing rule holds and the run-outs neither cross nor come within band_tolerance; pair margin M = min(m(a), m(b), crossing margins), so M never exceeds m(a) or m(b). Lists sorted by m descending with the B7 tie keys; stop the outer loop when m(a) is strictly less than the best margin; leave the inner loop when min(m(a), m(b)) is strictly less; crossing test once per pair; bands, lip equality and carry figures last, memoised, a failing candidate marked dead; exhaustive when the loops end without the time limit, with counts. Flight 8 foot: the procedure per d0 ascending, first d0 that yields a pair.
- RULING on the design question (may the search result be table values that the build verifies): YES; B9 in section 26 rewritten in place. Conditions: only free choices stored; written only by the tool with provenance and its own hash; verified every build through the same rule functions as the search (B1 to B5, R1a, R1c, R5 and no-touch, R7, frozen behaviour class, free-end guards, free width, lanes_inside_area, check rows); staleness caught by a hash of the inputs searched against (build fails: rerun the tool); a build of record refuses a non-exhaustive block; the T3 check reruns the tool once and requires a byte-identical block; the parameter table stays the only input to the build. "No better layout silently skipped" matters (B7 is part of the rule; a B8 fallback is legitimate only if the frozen behaviour truly has no solution) and is covered by exactness plus the rerun, not by the build. With this design a hand-authored layout would be verified by the same rules; it would lose only the claim of being best on the grid.
- Sent to t-artist, t-tools (wrapper invocation for the tool, a RUN_OUT_LAYOUT line and receipt fields, the rerun step in verify_repro.ps1), t-qa (how T3 words the tool; whether the block and its input hash join the lane digest; what a geometry-changing negative control is expected to do) and t-producer.
- t-qa on the layout tool (accepted; passed to t-artist and t-tools as requirements): T3 graded in three parts. T3a (the spec test, unchanged): two clean wrapper builds on the committed table are byte-identical apart from the masked FBX timestamp. T3b (added): from the table with the run_out_layout block REMOVED, the sequence through build_mc39.ps1 as the only entry point (tool, then build) reproduces the committed block and then the FBX byte-identically; run by t-qa once on the build of record; a stored layout nobody can regenerate is a hand-made input. T3c: the same at height_standard 79-6 in a scratch table completes or reports "no layout" for a named landing (a finding, not a pass). T3 text states that the table now also holds derived data written only by a script and that the build alone does not regenerate the tower after a geometry change without the tool step. Conditions: (1) one entry point, the wrapper switch, with a receipt; a tool run from a typed command line is refused; README states the two-step sequence; (2) the block is written into mc39_params.json by the tool only, under the between-slots rule; a one-character hand edit of a point fails the build; (3) exhaustive or nothing, with the time limit a table value; a time-out writes no block; if the exact search cannot finish on this geometry it is the layout finding; (4) lane digest IN: stored points, layout-input hash, block hash; OUT: provenance prose; (5) each negative control states whether it reruns the tool; test_overrides offsets are applied after the stored layout is loaded and verified.
- Added by me: the tool output must be a pure function of the other table values and the generator code (T3b cannot pass otherwise); the staleness hash is taken before test_overrides. Flagged to t-qa: a hand-authored layout for landings 6 and 7 (one of the options if the search stays inconclusive) would pass every rule check but could not meet T3b as written; it would need a t-qa ruling on T3 first; not proposed.
- Layout tool interface, ruled from six points of t-tools: (A) the layout-input hash is over a canonical form of the table WITHOUT the run_out_layout block and its provenance, plus the hashes of the rule modules; staleness compares that, never the file hash; (B) the tool writes a scratch copy by default; the real mc39_params.json only by an explicit mode run by t-artist through the wrapper switch, under the between-slots rule, refused with non-zero test overrides; (C) the block alone is also written as a canonical file with its hash, and --print-block extracts the stored block in the same form; (D) the time limit is a table value in seconds and reaching it writes no block (a slow machine can fail the tool, never change its answer); single-threaded, fixed order; (E) nothing in the block varies between runs, and the block does NOT name the tool receipt (the receipt records the block hash, one way only, or T3b could never be byte-identical); (F) test_overrides are applied after the layout is verified and after the staleness hash; a control with an alternate table states whether it expects stale or reruns the tool. stale=true fails every kind of build. Distinct exit lines for "no layout for landing N" and for a time-out. Tool receipt at a fixed committed path beside the table (proposed tools/blender/lookout/run_out_layout.receipt.json). Wrapper switch working name -RunOutLayout; build line BUILD_MC39 RUN_OUT_LAYOUT block= inputs= exhaustive= stale=; verify_repro gains the T3b and T3c steps and a control mode with a stated expectation.
- WITHDRAWN by me: the whole-millimetre-reach comparison build (part (c) of the four-part comparison against e5c77c45). The wall set, proxies, wall thickness, the 0.25 m bar and the layout mechanism have all changed since; the old routine no longer measures against the same walls and would verify a stored layout against bands it was not chosen with. The comparison against e5c77c45 is now by named headings in the findings of t-artist (deck datum; proxy defects in the accepted state; reach wall set with old and new lips; rule changes since; export axis; other, stated). -WholeMillimetreReach stays in the verified wrapper, unused. t-qa told that "other expected empty" can no longer be proven by intermediate manifests.
- t-engineer: the staged release path is the same as the landed one on the points I read. The area box passes on the ruled wording too (cab walking level 26.34 + 1.80 = 28.14 m against a top of 29.68 m). Slot 13 prediction filed before any code (TestResults/tower-eng/slot13/predictions.txt): the step is the engine recovering from the capsule surface being a hair inside the wall at the start of a Move, a knife edge decided by float rounding at exactly the radius; predicts no step at H27. Held loosely.
- t-qa on the fallback options (nothing ruled now; needs a t-qa ruling before either is chosen): points typed into the table by a person are a hand-made step under the automatic-FAIL rule of the spec unless a script in the repo derives them from the other parameters, and could not meet T3b. A FIXED-FORM layout computed by a script from the parameters (a closed-form construction instead of a search) is a different thing and could meet T3b, since the script regenerates it. If the costed options go to team-lead, they must say which of the two is meant.
- t-qa accepted the withdrawal of the comparison build. In place of "other expected empty": (a) the list of t-artist by named headings with every lane end old and new figures (lip each side, hand-over point, margin, bend, crossing) attributed to exactly one heading, "other" stated with each entry explained or marked unexplained (an unexplained entry is a question to me before the table check passes); (b) the independent reach and lip recomputation of t-qa at every lane end from the manifest colliders and decks at 1 mm; (c) per typed copy replaced by a table value, the old typed value and the table value, equal, or listed as drift. Passed to t-artist. T3b will be run by t-qa on a copy of the tools tree and table in an empty folder, since nothing on this branch is committed by the pod.

- team-lead conditions for the band-rule correction (my option b; a rule change after a result: allowed only if the rule as worded was WRONG and t-qa agrees before any run): (i) text from before the 03:25Z run showing narrowing is the general form; (ii) what the reject form protected against and why narrowing protects equally, as a safety argument; (iii) applied to every landing, with what it changes on landings 1 to 6 from figures; (iv) t-qa written yes with the test row; (v) bars unmoved; (vi) disclosed. My answers as sent: (i) yes and it cuts both ways: C8 and R7 (written 2026-10-11) state narrowing as the general form, but section 15c and the R7 statement itself record that I kept rejection for final segments knowingly, twice; a deliberate exception, not a slip of wording; (ii) the reject form protected nothing that narrowing does not (chosen for simplicity); the one thing narrowing can worsen is join coverage at the free end, which has its own check; (iii) landings 1 to 4 nothing by construction; on 5 and 6 valid layouts stay valid with identical bands but the SELECTED layout could change; cannot be said from figures without runs; (iv) not answered. CONCLUSION: (iii) and (iv) not met and (i) shows a knowing choice; OPTION b IS NOT OFFERED. My question to t-qa on it is withdrawn.
- SINGLE RECOMMENDATION sent to team-lead through t-producer: landing 7 a stop going up (54.11 deg corner toward the next stair, north guard rail about 0.36 m ahead, release about 19 deg), carried coming down (32.92 deg toward the next stair, 2.0 deg inside the carry limit). The accepted form of t-director; no descending stop; no rule change; no further search. - REVISED RECOMMENDATION (my first final text crossed with the choice of t-director and the lean of team-lead): LAYOUT 4, stops both ways on landing 7. t-director (ruling/lookout-tower-landing-7-choice and -amend1): layout 4 first, "and it stays my choice even if carried both ways returns"; both corners turn TOWARD the next stair, so none of the last-resort conditions is needed; chosen knowing the 90 deg turn coming down; carried both ways second; stop up with carried down third; no longer asks for the top flight to move; the provisional exception for landing 7 coming down is withdrawn as moot. team-lead lean: layout 4 provided the 1 mm separation is ruled acceptable or fixed by rule, t-qa accepts 9.2 mm on its five conditions, and the tool produces the layout by rule. My earlier objection (the 55 deg release outside the 10 to 45 deg condition) was a misreading of whose condition applied: that condition was for a release turning away; I have no technical objection to either corner (both over the 44.93 deg stop floor and under the 110.17 deg cone limit).
- RULING on the two run-outs passing about 1 mm apart in the found layout 4: NOT acceptable in a layout of record; fixed by a rule. Functionally nothing breaks today (lanes join only at free ends and link only at end points), but 1 mm is the distance at which the game treats two lane end points as one place, the touching tolerance is 0.5 mm, and the pair looks like an artefact of the old twelve-seed refinement. New named table value run_out_min_separation = 0.005 m (the level below which every clearance in the ruling is flagged as fragile), in check_values and the lane digest: the two run-outs of a landing stay at least that far apart everywhere; the pair test rejects pairs that do not; smallest separation exported per landing with a check row. It replaces "closer than band_tolerance counts as crossing". Chosen after a harness figure and before any result of record; disclosed. The best stop-both-ways pair under it and its margin are not known without a run (expected near 9 mm: the class had no band-rule rejections and 671 pairs pass the crossing).
- Swapped hand-over sides, stated to team-lead and t-director: in layout 4 the hand-over point of flight 7 is EAST of the line of flight 8 (0.7984, 0.8426) and that of flight 8 is WEST (0.4897, 0.8591), 0.309 m apart. Going up: stop on arriving (52.19 deg, release about 17 deg), carried to the east point, walk about 0.31 m west across the deck, carried 0.175 m back EAST to the 89.78 deg corner, turn about 55 deg to the stair (or a diagonal approach 55 to 78 deg off the second piece is carried through; facing straight down the first piece is inside the on-lane deadband and does not move along the second piece at all). Coming down: carried 0.325 m, stop, release about 55 deg, carried 0.175 m west, cross 0.31 m east, carried straight on if facing the head of flight 7. Asked that the bundle also report the best pair with the sides NOT swapped.

Down: cab; landing 7 arriving (90, always, release about 55); landing 7 leaving (52, only if walked in along the line); landing 6 arriving (about 45, always); landing 6 leaving (about 45, only if walked in along the line). Three places (landings 6 and 7, the cab); five junctions per direction. Climb time (t-designer arithmetic): 0.3 to 0.6 s a stop, under 2 per cent in all; C1 60 to 120 s not at risk.
- How the tool reaches layout 4 by rule: frozen_behaviour for landing 7 = stop at both ends in the table, the class searched exhaustively; stop_fallback_allowed and shortest_first_segment lose their landing 7 entries (the shortest-d0 pin existed only for a carried, away bend); T3b regenerates the block.

The combined message to team-lead carries the BUNDLE figures, not the harness ones: my revised text is held as a draft and written once after the bundle. Option b accepted as withdrawn; it goes in the friction log as considered and not taken, with my reasons.
- SEPARATION DEFINED IN WRITING BEFORE THE RUN: for the two run-outs of a landing, each the plan polyline P0 (lane end), P1 (bend), P2 (hand-over point): the smallest plan distance between any point of one polyline and any point of the other, the minimum over the four segment pairs of the exact segment-to-segment distance, analytic, not sampled; intersecting segments are 0. Rule: at least run_out_min_separation 0.005 m; replaces the 0.5 mm touching tolerance. Printed per stored pair to 0.1 mm with the two segments and closest points; the harness layout 4 pair evaluated by the same function for the record.

- t-director (…-choice-amend2): the stop-both-ways layout is already accepted by name; the 10 to 45 deg condition was written only for a descending release turning away and does not apply; both layouts acceptable on feel, preference stop both ways; if the other were built and failed its tests, stop both ways is the fallback without coming back to them.
- t-designer CONFIRMED my stop list for layout 4 (theirs described the withdrawn path): three places; five junctions per direction; three always stop the player each way and, going up, a fourth (leaving landing 7 for flight 8) stops them in practice: facing the stair is 89.78 deg off the second piece, so the player can neither join (needs within 60 deg) nor move along it (deadband 78.5); the only no-stop pass is input 54.9 to 60 deg off the line of the second piece. Their view on the swapped hand-over points: the weakest spot in the layout (west past the wanted stair, 17 cm carried back east, then stopped facing away until a 55 deg turn: two things in a row that read as the game steering, on the smallest deck); to go to G6 as "accepted to be seen" and the first place to look for a different hand-over point if Noah calls the landings wrong at I5. Passed to t-producer for team-lead.
- STATE CHANGED AGAIN: t-director SUSPENDED the preference for layout 4 (…-choice-amend4) on learning that going up only about a 5 deg heading window avoids a second stop at the run-out corner of flight 8; requirement for any landing 7 layout: for a player steering at the stair they want (about 20 deg either side), at most one stop on that landing per direction, and no walk past the stair followed by a carry back toward it; a second stop met only by walking squarely along the path line is tolerable. Conditional order: a same-side pair at 5 mm or more meeting the requirement; layout 2 if its join to flight 8 is clean; layout 4 only if the second stop is avoidable by natural steering or layout 2 is no better. The lean of team-lead is OPEN. Nothing is chosen; my revised text recommending layout 4 is a held draft.
- team-lead rulings: (A) run_out_min_separation 0.005 m ACCEPTED IN PRINCIPLE ("it tightens, it does not rescue anything, and it is set before any result of record") on terms: written agreement of t-qa BEFORE the bundle with the built-tower row; the value fixed now with a one-line reason and NOT retuned after the bundle; applies to every landing and class, the bundle saying if it removes or changes any pair on 5 or 6; disclosed as a rule added after a harness result. My reason as sent: 5 mm is the level below which every clearance in the ruling is flagged as fragile; equally five times the 1 mm lane-end coincidence distance (the reading of team-lead) and one grid step. (B) bundle additions approved: the not-swapped report for BOTH surviving classes; the step-by-step walk across landing 7 for each reported pair in each direction. (C) a per-landing behaviour class in the table is accepted as a design input provided the points, segments and bends are all found by the tool, the table records WHY and a regeneration shows it, and the tool fails if a typed class has no valid pair; t-qa confirms in writing before the export of record.
- My ruling for (C): for each linked landing the tool also searches the classes preferred to the chosen one and writes "none on the 5 mm grid" with rejection counts and nearest misses into the block (about a minute per landing), so the evidence is regenerated and compared in T3b. Advised against deriving the class by a fixed preference order (a creative choice per landing; a derived class could flip silently when geometry changes).
- Join cone read by me in the asset: ClimbTuning.asset laneJoinAlong 0.5 (acos = 60 deg); laneJunctionAlong 0.82; laneAlongDeadband 0.2; laneFullSpeedAlong 0.5. Heading window to be printed by the bundle: a player who joins a run-out at its free end and holds one heading is carried through a bend b without stopping iff the heading is between max(0, b - 34.92) and 60 deg off the second piece, toward the first. t-engineer asked to confirm the staged code does exactly that with one wish vector and that a free-end mouth is the only way onto a run-out.
- MY ARITHMETIC on the harness points (to be confirmed by the bundle; sent to t-producer and t-director as arithmetic, not as the walk): the only 0.30-clear floor on landing 7 is a thin east-west strip about 0.32 m long (448 grid points); R5 puts the two hand-over points at its ends; the line of flight 8 (x = 0.6648) passes between them, about 0.19 m from the west end and 0.13 m from the east; flight 7 arrives at the west end (its bend at about x = 0.30). So in EVERY two-hand-over layout the player walks the strip and is carried part of the way back: forced by the deck and R5 together; which stair gets which end is the selection choice. Layout 4 going up: carried east 0.48 m to the east point, walk west 0.31 m, the run-out of flight 8 runs due east 0.175 m then turns 89.78 deg: a player steering at the stair cannot join (90 deg off; needs 60); window 54.86 to 60 deg: about 5 deg; two stops. Layout 2 going up: same carry east and walk west; the run-out joined runs diagonally back toward the stair (32.92 deg off the stair line, 0.347 m; bend point about (0.6648, 0.5734)); steering straight at the stair is inside the join cone and carried through; one stop (on arriving). Coming down both end alike at flight 7 (cross east, carried back west about 0.48 m; no stop on a natural heading); layout 4 stops once on arriving off flight 8, layout 2 does not. A not-swapped pair would remove the 0.48 m carry east of flight 7 and shorten the back-carry of flight 8 to about 0.13 m. Expectation: the recommendation moves back toward layout 2 (price: 3.6 mm in the harness) or to a not-swapped pair.
- Asked of t-engineer: a name for the built-tower separation row (smallest distance between the two chains of level lanes of a landing at least the table value less band_tolerance; negative control); whether anything cares about two lanes within a millimetre mid-segment.
- THE ONE LINE for 5 mm, sent to t-qa and t-producer: "run_out_min_separation is 0.005 m because 5 mm is the level below which every clearance in this ruling is already flagged as fragile; it is also five times the 1 mm at which the game treats two lane end points as one place, as team-lead reads it, and one step of the search grid; it is fixed now and will not be retuned after the bundle."
- HEADING TEST defined in writing before any result (for the requirement of t-director), sent to t-artist: per reported landing 7 pair and direction; H_a = hand-over point of the arriving run-out; H_n, B_n, P0_n = hand-over point, bend point and lane end of the run-out of the wanted stair; steering at the stair = plan heading h0 from H_a to P0_n; test headings h0 + d for d from -20 to +20 deg in 1 deg steps, one heading held, W only. A heading is CLEAN if (i) joins: angle between h and the travel direction of the second piece (H_n toward B_n) at most 60.00 deg (acos laneJoinAlong 0.5); (ii) moves along it: that angle under 78.46 deg (acos laneAlongDeadband 0.2); (iii) carried through the bend: angle between h and the travel direction of the first piece (B_n toward P0_n) at most 34.92 deg. Printed: h0; the clean sub-range of d and its width; whether d = 0 is clean and which test fails if not; stops on the landing for a player steering dead-on = arrival stop (1 if the arriving bend is a stop class) + joining stop (0 if d = 0 is clean); the bar of t-director is a sum of at most 1. Walked past and carried back: whether the straight walk H_a to H_n crosses the lane line of the wanted stair, the overshoot of H_n beyond it (perpendicular), the back-carry of the legs H_n to B_n to P0_n, and how far the ARRIVING run-out carries the player past that line before the hand-over. Angles and one line intersection; no physics, no search.
- t-director advance line (…-choice-amend5), to be confirmed by the bundle: layout 2 if the walk confirms my arithmetic, and a not-swapped pair ahead of it at 5 mm or more keeping one stop per direction; layout 4 only if neither is available, as a named exception that comes back first. "One stop per direction" stands; "no walk past the stair with a carry back" is relaxed for landing 7 to "as short as the geometry allows and never ending in a second stop", because the deck forces it; it goes in the record as the weakest arrival on the tower, shown first at G6 and to Noah at I5. The earlier choice of layout 4 is withdrawn.
- t-engineer by reading: separation row named LandingRunOuts_KeepTheirSeparation with Negative_LandingRunOuts_FailWhenAHandOverPointGrazesTheOtherChain (to enter D4 once t-qa agrees the rule). The game does not care about two lanes within a millimetre mid-segment (links only between END points within 1 mm; joins only on the final piece of a free end; the travel loop looks only at the lane the player is on and its links); it would care about an end or bend point of one chain within 1 mm of an end or bend point of the other (linked as a junction); 5 mm covers both. Windows: one wish vector (facing times input, capped at 1, recomputed each frame) for the join test (at least laneJoinAlong 0.5) and the junction test (at least laneJunctionAlong); no heading memory, auto-turn or widening; a held heading passes a bend exactly when inside both cones. A run-out is joined anywhere along its FINAL piece at the envelope width there (the mouth is the whole final level stretch, feet within laneMouthHeight); a first segment cannot be joined from the floor; a player who has just left a lane cannot rejoin THAT lane until they release the input or move laneRejoinDistance away.
- PARTIAL STICK TILT (team-lead question; found by t-engineer reading): the join and bend tests use the PROJECTION of the wish, so with a partly tilted stick the cones shrink and at half tilt nothing passes the lever; C1 is graded on WASD so no criterion is touched, but a gamepad player at half tilt would be stopped at every carried bend. RULING (subject to the reading of the spec by t-designer, asked by t-producer): the two DECISIONS (joining; being carried through a bend) use the DIRECTION of the wish, normalised when its length is at least laneAlongDeadband (0.2), no input below it; SPEED along a lane keeps the projection. No new tuning value. A pre-result behaviour change found by reading, for the staged set before Slot A, with EditMode cases (half tilt dead-on joins and is carried; half tilt outside the cone is not; under the deadband nothing; speed at half tilt; keyboard cases untouched). My estimate 10 to 20 lines; t-engineer to cost. Alternative if team-lead leaves the code alone: t-qa records a known limitation (gamepad, partial tilt). Printed heading windows are labelled "full input".
- PARTIAL TILT, FINAL RULING. t-designer: the spec intends the DIRECTION of the input (spec 2.3 points 2 to 4 state the conditions as angles; the projection came from one formula written with W in mind; 3.3 and I1 put the gamepad on the same footing; N9 forbids unexplained stops). t-engineer, by reading the staged set: wish = facing x input capped at 1 (PlayerController.Climb.cs line 138); Along = dot(wish, lane direction) (ClimbRules.cs 83-86); the junction test Continues (24-28) needs Along at least 0.82, so nothing passes under 82 per cent tilt and at 90 per cent the cone is 24 deg; the join test JoinsAtEnd (158, 162, 168) needs at least 0.5, so nothing joins under half tilt; on-lane speed (69, 79, 97, 102) uses the projection as an analogue speed.
  RULED NOW (staged set, before Slot A; pre-result behaviour change found by reading; outside what C1 grades): Continues and JoinsAtEnd pass when the cosine of the angle between the input DIRECTION and the lane reaches the tuning value AND the projection reaches laneAlongDeadband (so joined or carried always implies the player will move; no new dead-zone value). Carry-first follows. Full input unchanged in every figure. Tests: both cone tests at input lengths 0.19, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1 across the cone edges; a carried junction always leaves a non-zero target speed; one PlayMode gamepad case at half tilt (the first stick-driven case in the fixture). Cost 2 to 3 hours (t-engineer); ClimbRules.cs at 492 lines is split in the same pass.
  DEFERRED: the full form of t-designer (direction also for the on-lane deadband and full-speed cone; tilt multiplies lane speed as on the ground). Cleaner, about one more hour, but it edits the speed functions (ClimbRules 64-80, 92-103) that the ramp, look-ahead and four-step arrival cases run through, in unrun code, for a response no criterion grades. Recorded as a KNOWN DIFFERENCE from ground walking (half tilt straight along a lane gives full lane speed; proportional between the deadband and half tilt), a named follow-up for the final note and the t-qa report. Comes back if team-lead wants it in this pod.
- BUNDLE SETTINGS FIXED before the run (t-artist script syntax-checked only, never run): no objection to their seven points and three notes. Stop classes at stop_bend_min 45.00 (the 03:25Z run used the 44.93 fallback floor; said in the output); flight 8 foot longest passing d0 when it stops, shortest when carried; new code = two-level order with one seed, separation_detail (exact, four segment pairs), the rule as a scratch table value, the not-swapped filter on grid pairs (refined pair kept only if it still passes), the walk printout and heading test, plus: refuses unless pinned to one core; the 03:25Z pairs separation by the same function; each class rerun with the rule OFF beside it with "rule changes this class". Nearest misses only for rule 3, counts for the rest; fall edges by index. Heading test printed with count, contiguity and both angles at d = 0; walked-past figures with signed distances; band-entry line SAMPLED at 1 mm against the smaller-neighbour band (information only). Top-flight check: as is, east 0.01, 0.02, 0.03 m and (my addition) west 0.03 m; hatch moved with it; landing 7 only; 90 s cap per position. Result file scratchpad\t-artist\h\bundle.json. Waits on the all-clear of slot 14 and the written agreement of t-qa to the separation rule.
- Partial tilt as staged by t-engineer (text only, uncompiled): 3 changed lines in ClimbRules.cs, one new 24-line file ClimbRules.Input.cs with Heading(wish, lane, tuning); 8 EditMode tests in ClimbInputDirectionTests.cs (188 lines). They staged the weaker condition (input LENGTH at least the deadband), leaving a sliver where an input just over the deadband is carried or joins and then stands (tilt 0.20 to about 0.244 for bends). CORRECTED: stage it as ruled, with the PROJECTION on that lane at least laneAlongDeadband; price accepted: just above the deadband the usable cone is acos(0.2 / tilt) (the full lever from tilt about 0.244, the full join cone from 0.40); "carried" strictly implies "moving". Tests state the edge as the smaller of the cone angle and acos(deadband / tilt) at tilts 0.19, 0.21, 0.25, 0.4, 0.5, 0.75, 1, plus a case that every passing junction leaves a non-zero target speed.

## 32. After the third ALL CLEAR (04:08Z, 2026-10-13)

- SLOT 14 (P4-QA-1 test 2, t-qa): with the runtime code of the landing in and its two Lookout test folders out, wildlife seed 101 reads 112 twice (in gate); whole landing out 113; landing in 114. The 114 needs the TEST ASSEMBLIES of the tower to be present, none of whose fixtures run. The two identical tests-out runs differed in one row of 360. Investigation closed under the cap; grade unchanged; P4-QA-1 stands; t-qa will not accept moving tower tests out for the final run.
- Direction of Noah via team-lead: "we have to make this succeed, so failure is not an option". Means: the route least likely to trip an automatic FAIL or a failed criterion, ahead of elegance, schedule or a nicer margin. Does not mean: any assist, blocker, deviation or red unreported or reworded; no bar or target moves after a result; the verdict of t-qa stays theirs.
- NEW RULE of team-lead, SUPERSEDING section 21 rule 4 ("one fix round, then issue, not withheld for green"): nothing is issued or run for the record on lane-end work until the declared shake-down is green on the exact hash; a red shake-down means STOP and team-lead before any fix round is started or counted. Sequence as I now hold it: candidate dev export, table, t-qa check, "final text, not issued"; textured build; import; the one declared shake-down; check-defect reds (sorted by t-qa with independent evidence) fixed and that check rerun; any build-attributed red: STOP, to team-lead; issue only when the named lane-end checks are green on the exact hash.
- My proposed list of what would be the THIRD lane-end occurrence (sent to t-qa, whose count it is): (a) after issue, any named lane-end check of section 26 D4 red on the issued hash, attributed by t-qa to the build, the layout or a rule; (b) after issue, any change needed to parts A to D of the issued text or to the lane data (a different lane-data digest) in order to pass a lane-end check or criterion; (c) after issue, a C1 or C2 PlayMode red traced by t-qa to a lane end, run-out, hand-over or junction; (d) a later export whose lane-data digest differs from the issued one without the prior word of team-lead. Not the third: a red traced to a defective check; anything on the shake-down before issue; the nosing-line and flight-line checks (their own blocker); the eye-clearance finding; an I5 "not right" (but a retune that changes lane data falls under (b)).
- P4, a REAL FIX of the cause, my answer to team-lead (t-engineer costing by reading): the only explicit dependence on absolute time in the wildlife runtime is WildlifeHerd.cs lines 55-58; re-read: WildlifeAgent uses Time.deltaTime and a per-agent System.Random; WildlifePopulation has one WaitForSeconds(5f); movement is NavMeshAgent. No shared random stream, no frame-count scheduling found. I cannot show it is the only path (Unity path-finding and crowd steering are not vouched for as repeatable; identical runs differ by a row). Confidence that fixing those lines brings the full run into the 111-113 gate: no better than even. Size about 15 to 25 lines plus an EditMode case of 40 to 60. Risk: it shifts herd timing, so probably every wildlife reading moves by a sample or two, including the filtered readings that match the baseline exactly today (112, 180, 182); t-qa would have to rule what "no change against the baseline" means on changed wildlife code; several graded runs to re-establish. RECOMMENDATION: do not change the wildlife system in this pod; record P4-QA-1 as it stands (one sample at one seed, following the presence of assemblies whose code does not run, in a walk not repeatable between identical runs; W-QA-1 class); fix as a follow-up. Whether that is a P4 FAIL or a recorded finding is the verdict of t-qa; the gate was not met.
- Landing 7, chance of a build-attributed red on the tower, my judgement with no figure of record: stops both ways (about 9 mm) unlikely, roughly one in ten; stop up with carried down (3.6 mm in the harness, three limits binding, plus the extra on-tower tests of t-qa for a 2 to 5 mm end and the explicit acceptance of team-lead) roughly one in four; a not-swapped pair at 5 mm or more in between. On the shake-down before issue a red is STOP and team-lead; after issue it is the third occurrence for any layout. Automatic-FAIL risk orders the layouts one way and feel the other; an I5 retune of landing 7 would be new lane data on an issued hash.

## 33. P4-QA-1 reading ordered by team-lead, and other items settled after the all-clear (2026-10-13)

### P4-QA-1: reading and arithmetic only (I lead, t-engineer reads; no code, no run)

- Order of team-lead: the two-test cap closed the diagnosis, not a fix; the position of t-qa (final P4 run graded as it reads, tests in, gate not widened) stands; fixing the cause of a pre-existing wildlife defect is in scope; if the cause cannot be found or a fix risks the baseline, say so plainly and it goes to Noah (an assist; the count is at 2).
- What the census depends on, by reading: (a) WildlifeHerd.cs 55-58, Time.time minus the last leg time against idle times of 3, 4 and 5 s (WildlifeTuning.asset idleSeconds.x), the only absolute time in the wildlife runtime; (b) WildlifeSightingTests.cs line 133, WaitForSeconds(startDelayAfterGrounded = 1 s) before the walk while the animals have simulated since scene load; (c) WildlifePopulation.cs line 75, WaitForSeconds(5 s) that only logs. Animal timers and the walk sampling clock are sums of frame steps from zero; per-animal System.Random seeded from seed and position; no shared random stream, frame-count scheduling or real-time clock; movement by NavMeshAgent (internals not readable). Common to (a) and (b): absolute time compared with a round number that the frame step divides exactly (4 s = 80 frames at 0.05 s; 1 s = 60 frames at the pinned 60 a second), so on the equal frame rounding of absolute session time decides.
- MY OWN ARITHMETIC WEAKENS (a): modelled in single precision at session times of 10 s to 20,000 s, the equal-frame case of the herd comparison resolves "not greater" in over 99 per cent of cases at every session time (exceptions under 1 per cent, at small session times). The herd line can matter only when an animal asks on exactly the equal frame and rounding falls the rare way. WITHDRAWN as my best candidate. (b) depends on engine internals I cannot read; if it resolves 60 or 61 frames with session time, the walker starts one frame off from the animals, which fits the shape of the data (91 identical samples, then in-range differences, one seen flag); it is in the TEST kit, and unproven.
- The table (filtered 112 in every state; full: baseline 112, placement 113, landing in 114, landing out 113, runtime in with tests out 112 twice) is not contradicted by a start-up-time explanation but cannot be derived from it: each project state gives a different game time before the walk and resolves equal frames its own way; the readings are repeatable draws from a small set. The one-row difference between identical runs fits game time before the walk varying by a frame, or an unrepeatable engine internal; not distinguishable by reading.
- Fix cost: remove both comparisons against absolute time: WildlifeHerd.cs, a herd-owned time since the last leg summed from zero with a fixed update order, 10 to 15 lines, one PlayMode case of 60 to 80 lines; the walk test start delay as a fixed number of frames, about 3 lines in a baseline fixture kit (the call of t-qa). Risk that the fixed count lands outside 111-113 for a reason unrelated to the tower: about one in three for seed 101 alone; every wildlife reading on all seeds, filtered and full, may move by a sample or two. Verification: one filtered and two full runs, about an hour of quiet baton.
- Cheaper first step proposed: a NEW separate diagnostic fixture (no wildlife code, no baseline fixture; about a minute alone) measuring at several session times the frames taken by WaitForSeconds(1 s) at the pinned rate and the herd comparison on the equal frame.
- WHERE IT LEANS, as sent to team-lead and t-qa: cause not established by reading; the fix is not sure and risks the readings that are exact today; the residue may be in the engine. Choices for team-lead and Noah: (i) accept P4-QA-1 as a recorded finding of the W-QA-1 class with the two controlled swaps as evidence; (ii) authorise the diagnostic, then decide; (iii) authorise the fix and its runs, accepting the readings may land out of gate and the baseline may need re-ruling. My recommendation: (ii), then (i) unless the diagnostic shows a single clean cause in the test start delay.

Why 0.03 and not 0.04: the absolute floor of t-director is an eye 0.08 m from a visible member; with the 1.15 factor of t-qa on the corner distance of the near plane at 21:9 (corner about 2.04 times the near distance at FOV 70), 0.04 m reaches 0.094 m and 0.03 m reaches 0.070 m. 32:9 not covered. Regression side measurable before the tower: asked t-tools for a -fvNear switch and one arm (cab position N, E, S, W and 60 deg down; ground at the tower foot; 0.10, 0.04, 0.03 as same-frame twins with changed pixels); verdict rule stated in advance: within the same-frame noise floor at every pose. t-director yes in principle with conditions (ruling/lookout-tower-near-plane-eye-rule-amend3): climb area only; invisible switch (twins at the first tread); eye never under 0.08 m from a visible member, 0.15 m on the ordinary walking line; no new artefact; steel holds up close. Grading is for t-qa.

### C10 and the band cap: text as issued to t-artist, t-qa, t-producer (t-td, 2026-10-13; not yet in the note: quiet order)

C10. FLIGHT 8 NAMED OVERLAP AT THE HATCH EDGE (top flight frozen at stringer_centre, 3.62 degrees). Agreed by t-qa before any figure of the tool.
1. Excepted set: the cab floor strip COL_BOX_CabFloor_04 (name confirmed by the first run's colliders block) and, with the hatch open, the hatch leaf and its swing volume standing on that same edge. Nothing else. The cab wall proxy is not excepted and is printed clear.
2. Stretch: along lane 8, the run positions over which a body of body_height on the lane meets those proxies' height range and the band's east edge, as the band rule forms it, is under wall_reach from the opening's east edge. Computed by the generator and written to the manifest.
3. Depth: wall_reach less that distance, largest over the stretch; printed at the band's east edge and on the lane's line.
4. Bound: depth at the band's edge at most checks.exit_lane_wall_overlap_max (0.13 m). A depth on the line above zero fails. A printed band-edge depth over 0.044 m goes to t-qa before the row is called green.
5. Row flight8_floor_overlap: passes when 4 holds and the stretch lies wholly under the opening. The flight's line-and-band check at wall_reach excludes exactly the set in 1 over exactly the stretch in 2, prints that it did, and fails on any other proxy, on these outside the stretch, or deeper than stated.
6. Negative control: with the exclusion switched off, the line-and-band check fails there naming COL_BOX_CabFloor_04.
7. Eye: eye rows apply in full and are not excluded. The eye row at the band's east edge over the stretch must meet today's 0.20 m on the printed figure in its own right; if it does not, that is an eye-row red outside the exception. The acceptance does not carry to the near-plane rule without t-qa saying so again.
8. No release there: lane state does not end on the stretch in play; T-C1j lists the stretch as "named overlap: not released here".
9. Tower case, hatch open, up and down, four frame steps, body on the line and held at the band's east edge: no frame at zero speed along the lane while W is held; no position change off the band between frames; lateral position logged; the camera's near-plane rectangle never crossing the floor strip or the leaf, by the eye rule's corner distance each frame; one twin still pair at the closest run position (floor strip hidden against shown: changed pixels only where the floor's edge is seen from outside, none inside the near rectangle's footprint). And with the hatch closed, from below: the body stops at the hatch as designed, no overlap case arises.
10. Reported as an exception agreed before any figure of the tool, made so the drawn stair is not altered to fit a rule of the pod's own; with this statement: the bound was changed after arithmetic and before any tool figure (first proposed as wall_reach less eye_clear on the line; corrected to the existing 0.13 m at the band's edge when the band's slack was counted).
Arithmetic so far, not figures of record: line 0.277 m from the edge at its closest (1.23 m of run from the head); band's east edge about 0.022 m inside wall_reach over about 0.88 to 1.23 m of run, assuming an east fit of 0.050 m at the drop-in stretch's start; eye about 0.228 m at the band's edge; cab wall proxy 0.264 m from the band's edge.

BAND CAP AT A FREE END (correction of the band rule; agreed by t-qa and t-engineer).
For each side of a free end's final stretch, cap_side = the smaller of half_width_cap and W, where W is the largest sideways offset from the free end, square to the stretch on that side, such that every point of the cross-section from the lane out to W is at least envelope_deck_inset_min from every edge of the deck's outline (found by the same 1 mm walk the inset row uses). band(s) = min(cap_side, reach(s), lip + tan(slope) x (length - s)), a running minimum from the free end, as now. reach[] is recorded as measured. One value per side for the whole stretch, written to cap_side as the game reads it.
t-qa's six conditions: (i) every free end on every landing and lane 9; the tool prints per end and side whether it binds and the width removed; (ii) bands unchanged to the last digit at every end and side where cap_side equals half_width_cap, shown on landings 1 to 6; (iii) the 0.06 m inset row and the 0.24 m mouth guard both stay as written and tested; (iv) the coverage half of JoinEnvelopes restated side by side ("the reachable span except the strip within 0.06 m of an open deck edge"), the game deriving the same band and the setup comparing it, t-engineer agreeing in writing (given); (v) a negative control: cap off in a test table, the inset row fails where the cap binds; (vi) on the tower, at every free end where it binds, four frame steps: the player carried to the free end's cross-section at the band's outer edge on the capped side and released by leaving the lane: for 5 s grounded on its deck, centre at least 0.06 m less tolerance inside the deck's edge, no fall, no join from above into a stair mouth, drift under 0.01 m after the first move, then W toward the lane rejoins; and a free-walking player placed in the 0.06 m strip between the band's edge and the deck's edge walks toward the lane and joins from the side with no fall and no join from above; a fall there is a C2 red and a layout finding, not answered by the cap.
Interplay with the mouth guard: "can be met" for the 0.24 m guard is evaluated on candidates as the band rule forms them; an end where only capped candidates exist is the exception case of the mouth-guard text.

## 41. Landing 7: the list of ways out sent to team-lead (13:56Z 2026-10-13; not final)

### Landing 7: the one list of ways out (t-td to team-lead, 13:56Z 2026-10-13; copies t-producer, t-director, t-qa). Margins pending t-artist. Nothing adopted on a figure.

CODE: standing region = R1(a) as ruled (5 mm grid; inside the deck; at least 0.30 m from every wall-set proxy and every fall edge = every unrailed outline stretch). Rule working, not a fault; control 448. Cause: the top flight plan angle alone (448 to 108; leg hulls give 14 back). Strip 26.5 cm by 1.5 cm between the north guard proxy and flight 8 start line.

BASES: R1(a) 0.30 m = live body radius plus skin (physical; not on offer for walls and drops). The 0.30 m between the two hand-over points = the pod design figure, not physical. The north deck edge 0.05 m inside the face = typed, no sheet draws a deck outline or guard on landing 7.

WAY 1. A rising stair start line is not a fall edge. Beyond it is the stair solid wedge, already in the wall set at 0.30 m. A correction of the rule SCOPE, general to every flight foot; descending mouths stay fall edges. Departs from R1(a) and R5 as written and applied. Possibly a target that was wrong: needs t-engineer physical reading and t-qa yes. Join test unchanged (t-director steering condition holds). Cost: t-artist about 1 h; t-engineer pending. Margin pending (figure b).

WAY 2. One joined path across landing 7, no free floor (t-director accepts as a named exception). Lane 7 head linked to lane 8 foot by one or two level segments with real corners (about 139 degrees in all: two corners of about 70, two stops each way); no hand-over points, no live controller on landing 7. Existing junction and line-clearance rules; no bar moves. Lines need 0.25 m a side (9 to 14 cm of freedom in the strip). Cost: generator 4 to 8 h (my guess); t-engineer pending; I5 gets a landing with no free floor.

WAY 3. North deck edge to the face (typed 0.05 inset to 0). An unexamined value with no sheet either way; not a correction toward the drawing; after a result it is moving a model value to get a layout. Guard would stand in the plane of the face bracing; landings 1 to 6 carry a different typed figure. Cost: a table value. Margin pending (figure a).

WAY 4. The stair pinned at its foot along the run (start line at the junction, as the table sheet note says) in place of at the head corner (my wording, no reading behind it). Same angle, stair and bearing. Needs both readers on both ends of the run; t-director refuses a shifted stair unless the sheet draws it. Not a candidate until read.

REFUSED OR NOT WAYS OUT: flight parallel; foot bearing or angle changed; 0.30 m from walls and drops shortened; the 0.30 m between hand-over points shortened alone (margin of millimetres); carried-through joins, widened deck, moved member, invisible wall.

TARGET THAT WAS WRONG: only Way 1, if confirmed. Way 3 moves a value to get a layout. Way 2 changes no target. Way 4 undetermined.
FROZEN FOOT BEARING: nothing in the figure bears on it as a reading.
MY ORDER: Way 1 if confirmed; then Way 2; Way 3 last and named as what it is.

### Addendum to section 41

ADDENDUM to the landing 7 list (t-td to team-lead, 13:57Z 2026-10-13; t-qa position file scratchpad tqa msg-landing7-position.txt governs its own conditions).

WHY THE START-LINE RULE EXISTS: no failure in any run stands behind it. Design-gate R1 and R5 (note lines 284, 288) say 0.30 m from ANY DROP. In the folded text (section 26, R1) I wrote: a fall edge is a stair-mouth edge or any stretch of a deck outline not covered by a rail-plane wall; the generator implemented every unrailed outline stretch. Line 624 only made the search enforce the crossing against stair-mouth edges. What was in mind was the other stair mouth, the head of the descending flight, a real drop. Nobody distinguished a mouth that goes down from one that goes up; nothing records a step-off or fall at a rising stair foot. The widening from drop to mouth was my drafting, not evidence.

E2, REPLACEMENT TEXT (written before any figure): A FALL EDGE is the head of a descending flight (the stair-mouth edge beyond which the stair goes down) and any stretch of a deck outline not covered by a rail-plane wall, other than the start line of a flight that rises from that deck. At the start line of a rising flight: the hand-over point and the crossing keep 0.30 m from every proxy as everywhere (the stair wedge and the ends of the flight side walls included), and the body centre stays on the deck side of that line by at least envelope_deck_inset_min. The mouth guard is unchanged and still measures to every stair-mouth edge, rising ones included.
t-qa accepts it as a correction of a definition only with E1 (build print for every rising flight foot on all landings and the ground: what a live capsule centre meets from the start line to 0.30 m beyond: floor-capable collider throughout, largest gap, largest drop; a gap or drop over the controller step means it is a fall edge there and the way is refused) and E3 (staging rehearsal at every such foot: T-C1j, the two-lane no-alternation case of T-C1i, a live body walked across the line with the lane held off). Applies to every landing; search rerun whole; disclosed as made after a harness none and before any export, old and new sentence side by side.

THE 0.30 m BETWEEN HAND-OVER POINTS (R5): not derived. Design-gate wording: at least 0.30 m of ordinary controller floor between them. The body figure reused to make the two release places distinct and the crossing a real stretch of free floor: feel more than physics. If changed it is t-director step 2; if argued as behaviour, an off-tower probe with predictions first (t-qa). No number proposed; none read off the 0.265 m strip.

NORTH INSET: not a bar; setting it to zero for room is a fitted value; changes only if two blind readings give a construction, then on every landing whichever way; else 0.05 stays unless team-lead re-chooses the interpretation by name.
PIN END ALONG THE RUN: pending the blind reading; t-qa writes the test first; no figure before it. FOOT BEARING: refused as a way out; not an entry.
ORDER (t-qa and mine): Way 1 with E1 first; then the inset reading; then the separation through t-director; the bearing not at all; then team-lead and, C1 being in question, Noah.

## 42. Fall edge: replacement rule text as sent (13:58Z 2026-10-13; not in force until print D is read as floor throughout and team-lead gives the word)

### FALL EDGE: replacement rule text (t-td, 13:58Z 2026-10-13; written before any figure under it; for every deck and every ascending foot on the tower; NOT in force until t-qa has read print D as floor throughout and team-lead gives the word)

OLD (section 26, R1): A fall edge is a stair-mouth edge or any stretch of a deck outline not covered by a rail-plane wall.

NEW:
F1. A FALL EDGE is (a) the mouth of a DESCENDING flight: the stretch of a deck outline at the head of a flight that goes down from that deck; (b) an opening in a deck that a body can pass down through (the hatch opening in the cab floor when open); (c) any other stretch of a deck outline not covered by a rail-plane wall, EXCEPT (d).
F2. (d) THE START LINE OF A RISING FLIGHT is not a fall edge: the stretch of a deck outline at the foot of a flight that goes UP from that deck (the ground pad included), over the flight width out to out of its stringers and no further. An unrailed stub of outline beside it stays a fall edge under (c).
F3. At the start line of a rising flight, in place of the fall-edge clause: (i) the hand-over point, the crossing between two hand-over points (R5) and every other place where the ordinary controller is live or becomes live keep capsule_reach (0.30 m) from every proxy, as everywhere; the stair wedge and the ends of the flight side walls are proxies like any other; (ii) the body centre stays on the deck side of the start line by at least envelope_deck_inset_min (0.06 m).
F4. Nothing else changes: capsule_reach, wall_reach, the mouth guard (still measured to every stair-mouth edge, rising ones included), the deck inset guard, the join test, the band rules, R5 separation.
F5. CONDITION OF USE (t-qa E1): the build prints, for every rising flight foot on every deck, what a body of the area radius meets when its centre goes from the start line to capsule_reach beyond it at deck height: floor-capable collider throughout, the largest gap and the largest drop. Where a centre can pass a gap or a drop larger than the controller step offset, that start line IS a fall edge under F1(c) and F2 does not apply to it.
F6. ON THE TOWER (t-qa E3; staging rehearsal; owed before issue), at every rising foot where a hand-over point or crossing stands under 0.30 m from the start line: T-C1j (a) to (e); the two-lane case of T-C1i (headings every 5 degrees, W: joins one lane, the same each time, no alternation over 5 s, no join from above); a live body walked from the hand-over point straight across the start line with the lane held off (test-only): no fall, no stick; four frame steps. A fall or stick there makes it a fall edge after all.
F7. SCOPE AND RECORD: every landing and the ground; the layout search is rerun whole under it; landings 1 to 6 may come out differently. Disclosed as a correction of a definition made after a harness none on landing 7 and before any export: the design-gate rule says 0.30 m from any drop (note lines 284, 288); the folded text widened drop to stair-mouth edge by drafting, with no run behind it.

WHAT THE GENERATOR CHANGES: fall edges = the outline less the rail runs, less the mouth stretch of each rising flight; the centre-on-deck test of F3(ii) at those stretches; print D (F5). WHAT THE GAME SIDE CHANGES: whatever reads fall edges from the manifest (hand-over point and crossing checks) reads the new list; the rehearsal cases of F6.

## 44. Fall edge text, amendment 1 (14:03Z, 2026-10-13): t-qa clauses added before any figure; other readings in

AMENDED TEXT (replaces F2 and F5 of section 42; F1, F3, F4, F6, F7 as written; still NOT in force):
- F2. The start line of a rising flight is not a fall edge over the width where the build prints floor-capable collider under a live body for 0.30 m beyond the line (row rising_foot_floor), and never wider than the flight out to out of its stringers. Any part of that line where the print does not show it is a fall edge. An unrailed stub of outline beside the line stays a fall edge (t-qa agreed; changes only on what print D shows).
- F5. The print is sampled across the whole width of F2 at a spacing no coarser than the search grid, for a body of the area radius: largest gap and largest drop over ALL samples. Where part of the width fails, F2 applies only to the part that passes.
- F6 gains one case (t-engineer unmeasured point, t-qa numbers set now): a live body walked into the toe of the wedge with the lane held off, three headings, four frame steps: feet height change between consecutive grounded frames at most 1 mm, no ungrounded frame, no sideways displacement beyond skin width, drift under 0.01 m in 5 s after input stops. A bob there is a red on Way 1 at that foot.
- Print D also states, per rising foot: where the toe of the wedge is relative to the start line, and how clearance to a sloped proxy is measured. FACT READ BY ME (mc39_runouts.py line 144, wall_d; line 124 named_walls): clearance to every proxy is PLAN distance to the proxy footprint (W.dist). That measure is the one in use for every proxy and is not changed for the wedge. Where the wedge footprint begins to count is for print D; the landing 7 diagnostic put COL_BOX_Stair_08 at 0.66 m from the region centroid with the mouth edge about 0.3 m away. If the toe counts at the line, F2 gains nothing and that is the answer.

READINGS IN:

- t-engineer, by reading: no drop beyond a rising start line; a body meets the wedge (about 52 degrees, over the 45 degree slope limit), stopped as by a wall; joined only if grounded, in the mouth and steering up inside the 60 degree cone. Not measured: a bob at the toe (now the F6 case above).
- t-director: Way 1 no feel cost; Way 2 accepted as a named exception but judged most likely to draw "not right" from Noah (three stops in the last metres); holds no minimum walk between hand-over points.
- t-qa blind readings (scratchpad tqa msg-pin-and-inset-outcomes.txt): pin test UNDECIDED, the frozen head-pinned construction stands, WAY 4 IS OUT. Deck inset NOTHING DRAWN, 0.05 m stays unless team-lead re-chooses it by name, WAY 3 has no drawing behind it.

A THIRD BLIND READER decides, before the search of record and before the staging export, asked two questions with no figure given: the digit, and what the arrowheads end on (stringers or the two 3 x 3 x 1/4 bars). ## 52. WAY 3 rule text, worded by me on the order of team-lead for ONE harness figure (14:26Z, 2026-10-13). Not a ruling that it is adopted. Written before any figure under it.

FACTS READ in the copied modules (mc39_layout.py): Layout.half(z) is the half width of the tower to the OUTER FACE OF THE LEGS at height z. Every deck is cut by the tower faces 0.05 m inside that line, typed in three places: corner outlines (a = half(z) - 0.05, landings 3 to 7), and the platform strip (half_width_at inset=0.05 and the tc line, landings 1 and 2). The leg corner cut is 0.12 m (typed). landings.guard_face_inset is 0.0: the guard rail plane, which is the face of its wall proxy toward the deck, stands on the deck edge, proxy thickness outward. t-qa blind readings: no sheet draws a deck inset at the faces.

W3-1. THE FACE LINE of a tower face at height z is the line through the outer faces of its two legs at that height: Layout.half(z) from the axis. It is the line every typed 0.05 is already measured from.
W3-2. THE RULE, one value, no sweep: on every landing, each deck edge that the model cuts 0.05 m inside a tower face lies ON the face line instead (inset 0.00). The three typed 0.05 become one table key, landings.face_edge_inset = 0.0; the old value is run by the same key at 0.05 as the comparison.
W3-3. THE GUARD: unchanged rule, the rail stands on the edge of the deck (T-1006-A typical landing), so its rail plane and the face of its wall proxy toward the deck are on the face line too; guard_face_inset stays 0.0; proxy thickness outward as on every edge today.
W3-4. EDGES IT MOVES, each outward by 0.05 m, nothing else: landing 7 (NE corner): the north and east face edges; landings 5 and 3 (NE): north and east; landings 6 and 4 (SW): south and west; landings 1 (NE) and 2 (SW): the two stretches where the faces cut the written 5 ft 2 in strip. 
W3-5. WHAT DOES NOT MOVE: every written or drawn thing: the inner edges of all landings (2 ft 1 3/8 in, 3 ft 1 in and the rest by riser count), the written 2 ft 6 in depths of landings 3, 2, 1, the 5 ft 2 in width, the start line of the top flight, the hatch, every stair, the legs, girts, braces and the landing 7 inner member. The leg corner cut stays 0.12 m. No steel is moved. The planks lie on top of the landing steel (deck level is above the steel level), so a deck edge on the face line overhangs no drawn member but lies over the girt.
W3-6. WHAT THE ONE RUN PRINTS, for EVERY landing, before anything is chosen (t-qa conditions, fixed before the figure): the standing region and every margin at 0.00 against 0.05; the eye rows against the steel of the face (body 0.05 m nearer girts, braces, legs); mesh against mesh interference of deck, guard posts and rails against girt, brace and leg; whether the visible guard stands on its proxy; whether anything visible or any proxy stands OUTSIDE the face line.
W3-7. CONDITIONS under which team-lead takes it over Way 2 (theirs; mine added and marked): every margin on landing 7 at least 20 mm (I do not raise this floor; t-qa may); nothing made worse on any other landing; t-director approves a still of the guard at the face; nothing drawn moves (W3-5: I confirm on reading; t-artist confirms from the build); t-qa has no objection beyond the disclosure. MINE: if W3-6 prints the visible guard or its proxy interfering with the bracing of the face, or anything outside the face line, Way 3 FAILS as worded; the guard is NOT then re-placed inside the bracing by a second number chosen after the figure.
W3-8. MY EXPECTATION, written before the figure so it cannot be fitted afterwards: the face bracing stands in the face plane just inside this line (frame inset 0.012 m, 2 to 3 in sections), and the wall proxy has thickness outward; I expect interference and something outside the face line, i.e. I expect Way 3 to fail W3-7 on t-director and t-qa conditions. I may be wrong; the print decides.
W3-9. WHAT IT IS, for the report if adopted: a model value with no sheet behind either figure, changed after a harness "none" to obtain a layout, by the choice of team-lead by name; not a correction toward the drawing.

## 53. WAY 2: landing 7 as one joined path. Named exception and rule text (14:28Z, 2026-10-13), written before any code or figure. Rule numbers are those of section 26.

OLD (section 26): R3 "Landings 5, 6, 7: a linked level run-out from the manifest ... No hand-over at a junction", with a hand-over point at the free end of each run-out (R1a), the two points 0.30 m apart and the crossing clear (R5). B6 "landing 7 carried". B7 exception for flight 8 foot. B8 fallback.
NEW, landing 7 only; landings 1 to 6 keep every rule as it stands:
J1. SHAPE. The head of flight 7 and the foot of flight 8 are joined by one level path on the deck of landing 7: a first segment collinear with flight 7 (no turn at the lane end of a flight, as B1), a middle segment, and a last segment collinear with flight 8. Two corners, A and B. Lengths: each collinear segment at least run_out_first_segment_min (0.02 m), the middle at least run_out_final_segment_min (0.05 m). Level within 0.01 m. If the proof of t-engineer or the generator of t-artist needs another shape (for example a corner directly at a flight end), they say so BEFORE code and it comes back to me and t-qa; it is not adopted silently.
J2. NO FREE END, NO HAND-OVER on landing 7. Lane state never ends there by FreeEnd (D2); asserted from the manifest and at run time. The ordinary controller is never live on landing 7 in play.
J3. BOTH CORNERS ARE STOPS: the player is held at the corner until their own input points along the next segment (R4), may stay, may reverse. Bend at each corner at least stop_bend_min (45.00 deg) and under the R4b limit (180 minus twice the lever angle, 110.17 deg at 0.82) less bend_limit_guard_deg. No carried corner on landing 7; nothing between.
J4. WHICH RULES APPLY on landing 7. R1(a): not applicable (no hand-over point). R1(b) and R1c carried bar: APPLY on the whole path: every band position clear at wall_reach, every line at wall_reach from every proxy; NO named overlap on landing 7. R3: applies as amended by J1 and J2. R4, R4a (at the two flight ends), R4b (at A and B): APPLY. R5: not applicable (no hand-over points); in its place the path does not cross or touch itself or either flight band. R6: APPLIES: a path that cannot meet J1 to J6 fails by name, no shortening, no exception. R7: APPLIES at all four junctions, both directions, both sides. B3 bands and B4 (band at each flight end equals the lip of that flight): APPLY, with accept figures at A and B both ways. B5: replaced by J3. B6, B7 exception, B8: REMOVED for landing 7. C3 guards (mouth guard, deck inset at a free end): nothing to guard, there is no free end; stated, not used as an exception. The band is never over a fall edge as section 26 defines one (unchanged definition): its outer edge keeps envelope_deck_inset_min inside every deck edge. D8 free width: never under twice wall_reach (0.50 m) anywhere on the path; points under 0.60 m listed. Eye rows along the whole path and through 360 degrees at A and at B.
J5. SELECTION, fixed now: candidates are the positions of A along the line of flight 7 and of B along the line of flight 8 on the 5 mm grid. Among pairs that pass J1 to J4, the pair with the LARGEST smallest margin wins, the margin being the smaller of (line clearance less wall_reach) and (half the free width less wall_reach) over the whole path; ties to the longer middle segment, then to the shorter first segment. "None" only when every pair was tried, with the counts printed.
J6. CONTROLS: negative control, the middle segment moved 0.05 m toward its nearest proxy must fail the clearance row; an EditMode case on made-up lanes (a chain with a flight at both ends and two stop corners) passes BEFORE any generator or game change lands; a built-tower path check.
J7. MANIFEST: checks.landing_behaviour for landing 7 is "joined_path" (a value no other landing has); the path record names the lane end it finishes on (the other flight end), each junction class and its accept and lip widths. New keys, schema 6. A game or test that meets "joined_path" without the reader for it fails by name.
J8. GRADING (t-qa, way2-grading-landing7.txt governs): the hand-over rows on landing 7 (T-C1j release, R1a, R5 crossing) are "not applicable: no hand-over on landing 7, named exception". C1 and C2 there are graded on the carried path; C2 is a lane test and is reported as weaker than a live-body test.
J9. THE STRIP CASES at the foot of flight 8 and the head of flight 7 (t-qa, live body, lane held off): on a joined path no live body exists there in play. They are still RUN, as a FORCED state (controller made live at that foot by the test), and reported as that: a defence check of the built colliders, not a state the player can reach. D6 lists how lane state can end without a free end (respawn and the world-bounds snap relocate the player); t-engineer confirms by reading that none leaves a live body in place on landing 7, or names the one that does.
J10. NAMED EXCEPTION, sentence for the report: "On landing 7 the player is not handed back to free walking. The last diagonal flight and the top flight are joined by one level path with two corners at which the player stops and turns by their own input. This was decided after the layout search found no pair of hand-over points on the corrected geometry (standing region 116 points, furthest two 0.265 m apart, against 0.30 m). No bar was changed; the hand-over rows have nothing to act on there. The game director accepted it and judged it the arrangement most likely to feel wrong; it is exposed at I5."

## 55. Stair HEADS: statement of facts and reading (14:32Z, 2026-10-13). No remedy built or printed. Arithmetic is mine and unmeasured unless a print is named.

FACTS (my second-hand print D control and print E of t-artist agree): at every descending head on landings 1 to 7 the deck collider ends on the head line. The solid wedge of the flight is set back 0.32 m (plan) from the head lane end: its upper end face runs from a corner 0.177 m beyond the line and 0.525 m below the deck up to a corner 0.320 m beyond and 0.415 m below; from there down its top face is the nosing plane at 52.37 deg (flight 8: 56.31). The end face itself is tilted 37.6 deg from level, sloping down TOWARD the deck. Under a centre from the line to 0.177 m there is nothing; the flight side walls close both sides from deck level to 1.785 m (print E: largest opening 0.015 m). Riser 9 in (0.2286 m, table); controller step offset 0.40 and slope limit 45 (pod rules); tower body radius 0.25. Tuning as staged: laneJoinAlong 0.5 (cone 60 deg), laneMouthHeight 0.20, laneCatchBand 0.30.

(1) WHAT A LIVE BODY MEETS stepping off a head with its input OUTSIDE the cone. While its centre is less than 0.25 m past the line some of its disc is on the deck: it stays grounded on the deck edge and is not joined (the floor join asks the cone). Past that it falls. Its feet reach the nosing line about 0.32 m down; there the catch from above joins it to the stair IF it is within the lateral band of the lane at that point (narrow: the head lip easing to 0.05 m). Outside that band the code lets it fall on: it meets the wedge upper end face 0.43 to 0.52 m below the deck. That face is at 37.6 deg, inside the 45 deg slope limit, so by reading it is WALKABLE and slopes back under the landing, into a notch 0.177 m wide between the deck edge and the wedge. The way back up is 0.43 m or more, over the 0.40 step offset. By reading: a body standing half a metre below the deck in the head of the stair, off the lane, unable to step back up; whether it can then get onto the 52 deg top face and slide down the flight I cannot say. That is a C2 escape or a stuck state if it happens. UNMEASURED.
(2) THE JOIN BAND AT A HEAD (t-engineer J1, ClimbRules.JoinsAtEnd 115 to 136): a grounded body at or before the line, inside the join envelope of that end (the lip widths), feet within laneMouthHeight, input within 60 deg of the direction down the stair. Past the line it never applies. So a body walking at the mouth and steering down the stair within 60 deg is taken before it leaves the deck; one walking across the mouth at more than 60 deg from the stair direction is not, and can cross the line.
(3) WHAT ALREADY KEEPS A WALKING BODY OFF THE LINE: nothing. R1(a) and R5 place the hand-over POINTS and the crossing 0.30 m from the line; they say where the body is released, not where it may walk afterwards. The landing guards cover every deck edge except the mouths; a mouth is open by definition and has always been a fall edge. A body that deliberately walks at a head mouth off-axis reaches the line.
(4) THE CATCH RULE: I SUSPEND my ruling of 14:28Z (scope the catch to the hatch by construction). At a head the catch is the one piece of code that takes a body that has left the deck, where its feet meet the nosing line, without a snap. Scoping it to the hatch would remove that at every head and leave the fall of (1) with no join at all. It is also an unasked join, which t-director refuses, and a drop through a mouth unsteered, which t-director also refuses: the two refusals meet at a head and only a measurement can say which the code does. NOTHING CHANGES in the catch until the rig has run the head cases BOTH ways (catch as staged; catch scoped to the hatch) and the rising-foot cases both ways.
(5) IS IT C2 EXPOSURE ON EVERY LANDING: by reading, YES at every descending head on landings 1 to 7, for a body walked off the head line outside the cone. Unmeasured. Not found by any test today: nothing written checks what a live body does past a head line, because the design assumed the body would be on the lane there. Same root as the strip at the feet: the wedge was approved by me as a stop against walking through a flight, never examined as what a live body lands on. MY MISS.

OUTLINE OF A CLOSURE, on paper only, ready if the rig shows an escape, an unasked join or a stuck state at a head (C2 grounds; heads were always fall edges, so no layout rule is touched): what stands at a real head is the top tread, one riser (0.2286 m) below the deck, between the two stringers. A solid for it: from the head line to the upper end of the wedge, the width of the flight between the side walls, top at one riser below the deck, the next at two risers where the wedge end face is now (so no face tilted back under the deck remains). Visible wood and steel behind it: the top treads and stringers already modelled (t-artist to confirm from the mesh). What a body then meets: a step down of one riser, under the step offset both ways, so it can stand there and step back up, and it is joined to the stair when it steers down it. Whether the catch then still fires there, and what the join does on a body standing one riser down, is for t-engineer by reading before anything is built. The same form mirrored at feet is NOT proposed (the floor route is closed).

## 64. The joined path re-worded for ANY landing (fallback of team-lead for a landing with no layout at or above the floor) (14:43Z, 2026-10-13). Paper; nothing built. Written before the guard ruling and before any figure on the re-fitted geometry. Section 53 (J1 to J10, landing 7) is its first instance and stands.

WHEN IT APPLIES: only to a landing k (1 to 7; never the ground) for which the ONE whole-tower search finds no pair of hand-over points at or above the one floor team-lead names. Each such landing is its own named exception with its own disclosure. A landing that has a layout keeps every rule of section 26 unchanged. It is never chosen for a landing that has a layout.
K1. SHAPE (was J1): on landing k the head of flight k and the foot of flight k+1 are joined by one level path on that deck: a first segment collinear with flight k, a middle segment, a last segment collinear with flight k+1; corners A and B on the two flight lines. Collinear segments at least run_out_first_segment_min, the middle at least run_out_final_segment_min, level within 0.01 m. On landings 1 to 4 this REPLACES the built-in run of R2 at both ends; on 5, 6, 7 it replaces the linked run-outs of R3.
K2 (J2): no free end and no hand-over on that landing; lane state never ends there by FreeEnd; asserted from the manifest and every frame.
K3 (J3): both corners are STOPS (held until the input of the player points along the next segment; may stay; may reverse); each bend at least stop_bend_min and under the R4b limit less bend_limit_guard_deg. The frozen behaviours of B6 for that landing (5 carried, 6 stop) are removed with its hand-over points.
K4 (J4): unchanged in every line: R1(b) and the carried bar of R1c on the whole path with no named overlap; R4, R4a at the two flight ends, R4b at A and B, R6, R7 at all four junctions; B3, B4; D8 free width never under twice wall_reach; eye rows along the path and through 360 degrees at each stop; no part of the band over a fall edge except where the path enters or leaves a flight along the lane of that flight; elsewhere centre at least envelope_deck_inset_min inside every fall edge. R1(a) and R5 not applicable there. The free-end guards have nothing to guard there.
K5 (J5): selection unchanged: corners on the 5 mm grid along the two flight lines; largest smallest margin; ties to the longer middle segment, then the shorter first segment; none only with counts.
K6 (J6): the negative control per joined landing; the EditMode case on made-up lanes once, before any change lands; a built-tower path check per joined landing.
K7 (J7): the manifest value joined_path on each such landing and only those; one path record per landing.
K8 (J8): the hand-over rows of that landing are not applicable, named exception; C1 and C2 there graded on the carried path as a lane test, reported as weaker than a live-body test (the grading file of t-qa governs).
K9 (J9): the strip cases at the foot of flight k+1 and the head-line cases at the head of flight k have no live body in play on a joined landing; run as a forced state, reported as a defence check.
K10 (J10): one report sentence per joined landing, with the figures of that landing from the one search (standing region, best pair, margin against the floor), and the count of joined landings stated up front.

WHAT DIFFERS PER LANDING: (i) which flights meet (k and k+1) and the deck outline (platform on 1 and 2; corner with a written 2 ft 6 in depth on 3; corner on 4, 5, 6; the drawn frame on 7); (ii) landing 7 ONLY: the last segment is collinear with the SKEWED top flight (3.62 degrees), the deck stops on the start line of the top flight, and the hatch-edge items (C10, lane 9, the bare top-flight rail stretch) lie beyond the path and are untouched by it; (iii) the wall set of each landing after the guard ruling (bracing as guard, or a rail set in); (iv) on landings 1 to 4 a joined path removes free walking from a deck that has plenty of room, which is why it must never be reached there except by a real "none".
WHAT MAY NOT GENERALISE, to be printed before it is relied on: (a) K3 needs the total turn between the two flight lines to be split into two bends each between stop_bend_min and the R4b limit: possible only when that total is between 90 and about 220 degrees. On landing 7 it is about 139. t-artist prints it for every landing (the same print as the join directions). (b) K1 needs the two flight lines not to be one line; true on a tower whose flights change corner. (c) FEEL: t-director accepted the exception for landing 7 only and rated it the most likely "not right". Every further joined landing adds two stops each way to the climb; N9 asks a real turn on every landing and a walk of 60 to 120 s. This is NOT a technical limit and it is not mine: each further landing needs the acceptance of t-director by name, and the climb time with n joined landings is to be stated before I5. (d) C2: every joined landing turns a live-body stress into a lane test; with several, C2 rests mostly on lane tests, to be said plainly in the report.
