## 69. WIDENED RULING on the first run of the interference row: every pair in one of three classes (14:54Z, 2026-10-13). By order of team-lead. No pair is waved through; t-qa reads the table and may refuse an entry.

THE THREE CLASSES (team-lead): DEFECT (fixed in the generator before the staging export). MADE FIXING (two members joined or bearing on each other on the real tower; entered in the table by name with the sheet or reading it rests on). MODELLING CONTACT (under a tolerance named with its reason). Class three is NEW after a result: my definition of 14:36Z had no allowed overlap; team-lead orders it; disclosed as a definition widened after the first run, with the reason below.
H1. GUARDS into face steel, landings 3 to 7 (40 pairs, worst 0.049 m): DEFECT. Remedy: the guard ruling, section 65 with G4 as amended in section 68. Required result: zero.
H2. HAND RAILS AND POSTS of flights 7 and 8 into diagE7_1, diagE7_2, midE7_1, plan7_2 (14 pairs, worst 0.044 m): DEFECT. A stair rail cannot be set inward like a guard (its plane is the rail plane of the flight, ruled before the search). So: a hand rail, mid rail or post ENDS, with a made end, where it would come within landings.guard_face_clear (0.0381 m, the SAME clearance as the guards, as team-lead asks) of any frame or plan member; this applies on every flight. This REPLACES the 0.03 m and the key stairs.rail_steel_gap I issued at 14:50Z; top_flight_rail_floor_gap (0.03, the cab floor underside and opening edges, agreed before the search) keeps its name and meaning. Flight side-wall proxies are not shortened. Each stretch left without a rail is an OMISSION listed by flight, side and length and goes to t-director by name; the conditions agreed for the bare stretch of the top flight apply to each. Flight 7 against plan7_2: first t-artist says whether that plan member should be there at all (the plan-frame note leaves out sides that cross a stair); if it should not, removing it is the fix.
H3. STRINGERS into landing bearers at every head (0.063 m; a 2 1/2 in bearer is 0.0635, so nearly through) and Stringer_F07_L into landinner7_1 (0.082): DEFECT as drawn. T-1006-A shows the head of the stringer fixed to the member with a 1/4 in bent plate and rivets: the stringer ENDS at the member, it does not pass into it. The model: each stringer ends at the near face of the member it bears on (t-director asks the same, a joint trim); the resulting contact is then a MADE FIXING, entered by name, source T-1006-A bent plate at the head (the plate itself is not modelled: a named omission). No notch is drawn.
H4. Stringer_F08_L into girtE7_1 (0.002 m): MADE FIXING by the frozen top-flight construction (the head of the east stringer stands on the toe of the top girt inward leg); the 2 mm entry falls under H5 only if t-artist confirms it comes from the plan angle of the flight and not from a wrong length; otherwise a defect.
H5. DECK AND BEARER solids into girts and joists (46 pairs, 0.001 to 0.004 m, at the underside of the deck): MODELLING CONTACT, for members that LIE ON one another on the real tower (plank on joist or bearer; bearer or joist on girt). Tolerance checks.bedding_overlap_max = 0.005 m (MY CHOICE, tagged). Reason: a member modelled exactly on another gives coplanar faces that flicker; a few millimetres of bedding avoids it and is hidden inside the joint; t-director: not a look fault provided no faces are coplanar. It applies ONLY to pairs listed in the table with kind "bearing", never as a blanket: an unlisted pair over 0.001 m stays red. Each of the 46 is listed by name by t-artist2 and accepted or refused line by line by me and then t-qa.
H6. THE 67 TOUCHING rows: proposed by name by t-artist2 as made fixings (kind "butt"); accepted line by line by me, then t-qa, BEFORE the next run; none is accepted by default.
H7. PROXIES against steel: not yet run; classified when they are; no class is promised in advance. For the guard draft, G4 gains one clause found while listing exceptions: the deck-side face of a landing wall proxy lies on the INNERMOST surface of its guard members (as on the stairs, where the post inner face and the side-wall face coincide), not on the guard axis, so no rendered guard member stands inside the wall where a body pressed to it would overlap it. Today the posts stand half a section (0.0254 m) inside the proxy face.
H8. WHAT MOVES: only H1 (guards and their wall proxies: standing regions of landings 3 to 7; pre-search). H2 to H5 are visible steel only: no collider, lane or standing region moves; obstacle records of trimmed stringers and shortened rails follow their meshes, so eye rows may move by centimetres on the flights concerned, to be printed. Hours added to the 6 to 9 of t-artist: theirs to state.
H9. EXCEPTIONS BY DESIGN for the body-against-rendered-mesh row (asked by team-lead; the table of t-qa stays empty except for entries we both sign): (1) the deck or floor the body stands on; (2) on each flight lane, the treads and stringers of THAT flight under a carried body, by lane; (3) C10, the named overlap of flight 8 at the hatch edge, as agreed with t-qa; (4) C6, lane 9 against the cab south wall, at most exit_lane_wall_overlap_max; (5) the camera at the firefinder sight against the alidade and ring (T-C3b by twin governs there). Nothing else is by design; in particular no guard, rail, post, leg, girt, diagonal, trimmer or hatch leaf.

## 72. Guard ruling, additions G11 to G15: the mouths near a face, the general wall rule, stair rails by name, the lists (15:00Z, 2026-10-13). Geometry and collision grounds only; no region or layout figure.

FACTS (t-artist, repo_b draft, harness): final figures with the wall face on the inner surface of the guard (posts are 0.04 m square: half 0.020): guard axis 0.206 to 0.228 m and wall deck-side face 0.226 to 0.248 m inside the face line at the deck (0.176 to 0.198 inside the deck edge); wall tops 2.005 m or more. On landings 5, 6, 7 the stair mouths reach nearer the face than the moved wall: the wall line lies inside the mouth by 0.032 and 0.041 (L5), 0.084 and 0.084 (L6), 0.034 and 0.087 (L7); remaining half width of the mouth on its face side 0.269 to 0.324 of 0.3556. t-artist2: all 24 face-edge landing walls as built have face steel on the deck side of their face below 1.80 m; flight side walls 05_01, 06_01, 07_01, 08_01 and the cab window-band walls have frame steel in their volume (classification pending the body-side print).
G11. THE MOUTHS (the question of t-artist): form (b). The WALL runs straight on its computed line to the inner edge; the walkable outline stays convex. Reason, collision only: the wall stands there because the face steel leans in over that strip; a body standing in the outer 0.03 to 0.09 m of the mouth would have its head in the brace; ending the wall at the side of the stair (form a) would leave a pocket whose face-side stop is again inside the lean. The VISIBLE guard ends at the side of the stair: no post or rail is drawn inside the width of a stair. The stair, its centre line, its lane line and its visible width are NOT moved. What changes is the usable width of the mouth on its face side on landings 5, 6, 7: the band of the lane end and its lip there are whatever the wall leaves, read from the wall outline as for any wall. This touches a thing agreed before the search (stair mouths unmoved): the drawn mouth is unmoved; its usable part is narrowed by the lean of the face. Notified to team-lead and t-producer as pre-search; t-director to accept by name an invisible wall inside the outer 3 to 9 cm of a visible stair foot or head with the brace overhead, or say what visible thing stands there.
G12. GENERAL RULE, of which G4 is the landing case: no proxy that stops a body may leave rendered steel on the body side of its body-side face from the surface the body is on (deck; nosing line on a flight; cab floor) up to landings.wall_clear_height. It applies to every wall proxy: landing walls, flight side walls, cab walls. Where the print of t-artist2 shows steel on the body side of a FLIGHT side wall or a cab wall, that wall (the proxy, not the visible member) is set toward the body locally until the rule holds, computed by the generator, and the band of the lane reads the moved wall. NOT ruled for any particular flight until that print exists; if it touches the top flight east side, the frozen construction of the top flight is not moved, only the wall and the band, and it comes to team-lead by name first.
G13. STAIR RAILS BY NAME (t-artist2: 12 of the 14 pairs are mid-run; t-director amend8): FLIGHT 7 WEST against plan7_2: the PLAN-FRAME member gives, not the rail. The position of the plan frame is an interpretation with no sheet figure and its own rule already leaves out sides that cross a stair; that rule is widened: a plan-frame side is trimmed or left out where it would come within landings.guard_face_clear of a rail, post or stringer of a stair. No break in that rail. If a reading shows the member drawn there, it comes back. FLIGHT 8 EAST against the east face (diagE7_1, diagE7_2, midE7_1), the form approved by t-director by name and no other: the top rail broken once round the diagonal, each side ending 0.0381 m clear with a made end and carried by a post; the mid rail left out between its first and last crossing; posts moved along the rail to stand clear, never cut. WHY THE RAIL PLANE DOES NOT MOVE LOCALLY: it was ruled before the search at the outer face of the stringer; a rail jogged inward would stand over the treads inside the written 2 ft width and take the side-wall proxy and the band of the lane with it; no sheet draws a jogged rail. The fallback named by t-director if the still reads as a broken rail: no east rail on that run, the face named as the guard there. Each stop and each omission is printed by name with what stands in the gap.
G14. THE TWO LISTS of t-artist2 (lists_for_table.txt, 120 lines, on the state as built): by kind, provisional: the 60 touching rows that are a landing solid lying on a joist, bearer, inner member or girt are the SAME bedding as the 46 red ones and take kind "bearing", not "butt"; the 46 bedding rows take kind "bearing" (all 46 lie one on another, none sideways, all under 0.005 m, by the statement of t-artist2); the 7 rows where a guard rail or post or a hand post grazes a mid girt or diagonal are NOT fixings: defects, cleared by the re-fit; the 4 proxy rows fall under the proxy criterion. FINAL acceptance line by line is made on the list from repo_b AFTER t-artist tags the unnamed landing solids (a table by drawing number is refused: numbers change with drawing order); then to t-qa; the list is closed before the next run of record.

## 81. Two readings of the staged climb code, moved to me from t-engineer (15:15Z, 2026-10-13; by reading only)

READING A, carried or live (for X4): staged Scripts Gameplay Climb. On a lane the controller is OFF (join, PlayerController.Climb.cs line 224) and StepLane writes the position from the lane each frame. The body becomes LIVE in one place only: StepLane about lines 256 to 267: ClimbRules.LeavesAt (ClimbRules.cs 406 to 411) is true only at the far point of a FREE end (u at or beyond length + headRun, or at or below minus footRun) with the target speed still pointing outward; then EndLane(FreeEnd) and controller.enabled = true (267). A LINKED end never hands over (258). AT A STOP CORNER (ClimbTravel.Advance, the stop branch): the remaining travel of the frame is discarded, speed zero, lane state HELD, controller still OFF; input along the next lane passes the body on (PassOnto, Continues), input back walks it back; strafe and jump do nothing. A body held at a stop corner is CARRIED, not live. Other endings (PlacementReason, ClearClimb lines 98 to 137; Respawn in PlayerController.cs 159 to 163) are placements and re-enable the controller they switched off. Whether condition (3) of t-qa keeps stop corners outside a stretch is still for t-qa to say.
FINDING while reading (not reachable on the tower in play; reported): a ClimbArea destroyed or unloaded while the player is on one of its lanes: PlacementReason returns None (line 101 tests not-in-an-area before area-disabled), ClimbMove returns false at line 151 with lane state still set, and the walk branch calls controller.Move on the controller the lane switched off: the error CharacterController.Move called on inactive controller every frame, body dead. A test teardown does this.
READING B, slot 22 (rig Stage A gave no result): from TestResults tower-eng head-edge playmode.xml and playmode.log: the arrival assertion reads rode True, released False, at (-0.34, 200.05, 0.00): the body at the far point of the head run at lane height; released in the rig means not on a lane AND supported, so the line does not tell still-on-the-lane from off-the-lane-with-controller-off. The inactive-controller error appears 10 times in all (7 in tests, 3 after the results were saved; test duration 2.98 s wall time), which fits the teardown hole above and not an every-frame state for 30 s. By reading the game code I find no path leaving a body off a lane with its controller off while the area exists, and no reason LeavesAt stays false at the far point with W held (TargetSpeed, ClimbRules.cs 55 to 64, is positive there). I CANNOT say rig fault or game fault from the evidence on disk. Asked: a diagnostic run of the UNCHANGED rig logging per frame the lane index, u against length + headRun, target speed, controller.enabled, grounded, last lane exit and the frame of each error. Not called a C2 stuck state and not called a rig fault until then.

THE FACT IT RESTS ON, one line from the code (staged Climb, by my reading and the table of t-engineer, which I CONFIRM as written, msg-carried-or-live.txt): at a stop corner the controller is OFF and the body is lane-held (ClimbTravel.Advance, the stop branch: travel discarded, speed zero, EndLane not called); the one place the body becomes live in play is the far point of a FREE end (StepLane, from LeavesAt to controller.enabled = true); a linked end never hands over.
X3 (as amended). THE STRETCH is exactly the part of the lane (the flight, and every run-out or path segment after it) over which the line is under wall_reach from the named wall; computed and printed per end with its limits. THE THREE BARS are carried ALONG the whole stretch, every 2 cm, not only at the lane end: (a) shortfall to the named wall at most 0.13 m; (b) overlap of the capsule as shaped with each named member at most 0.13 m and no rendered steel within 0.12 m of the axis, gusset plates included; (c) the eye at least 0.20 m from rendered steel. The search rejects any candidate whose line exceeds a bar anywhere in its stretch; so no first segment runs toward the face past the point where a bar would be exceeded, and that limit is printed per end. The line-and-band checks exclude exactly the named wall and members over exactly the stretch and print that they did.
X4 (as reworded). NO POINT WHERE THE BODY IS OR BECOMES LIVE lies inside a stretch: hand-over points, the crossing and free ends are outside every stretch; lane state cannot end in one; T-C1j lists each named end as named overlap, not released here. And (rule of t-engineer, from the fact that the final level stretch of a free end is also its join mouth and ordinary deck where a LIVE body can walk): a stretch lies wholly on the flight or on an INNER segment; it never reaches onto the final stretch of a free end. A STOP CORNER MAY LIE INSIDE A STRETCH on the four terms of t-qa: (i) the bars of X3 (a) and (b) hold AT the corner and all along the first segment to it, printed; (ii) the eye bar holds AT the corner at 0.20 m and the near rectangle is clear there through the FULL yaw and pitch sweep; (iii) on the tower the player held at that corner for 5 s with no input, and for 5 s with W and no turn, is shown still on the lane every frame (lane state asserted, no physics step taken), at the four frame steps; (iv) the limit of X3 on the first segment is carried by the search and printed per end. The table switch checks.named_overlap_stop_corner_allowed is therefore TRUE, with these four terms as rows; it remains a table value.
STILL OWED before X2 is called met on landing 6: the axis figure (0.226 m) confirmed with gusset plates in (the interference row has them; the print of t-artist did not).

Their class (carried at both ends; second choice a stop at the lower head and carried at the upper foot; never a stop at both ends below landing 6; at most one bend before release; live crossing between the two run-outs) and the cap (carry_bend_max_toward less checks.carried_bend_lever_room 4.0) were stated before any figure and are NOT changed by this.

Source: the distances t-artist printed on repo_b (scratchpad t-artist h builtin_run_vs_guard.json and shorter_run_l34.json): lane end to the near wall 0.370 N (flight 3 head) and 0.359 E (flight 4 foot) on landing 3, 0.376 S and 0.367 W on landing 4; the other face 0.78 m or more from each end. On a corner landing both mouths lie side by side on the inner edge and both flight lines run at 45 degrees to both faces, so each end is near one face and the other stair lies on the far face side. By that reading a bend toward the next flight turns the line AWAY from the near wall at all four ends, and after a bend of 30.91 degrees the line still closes on that wall at about 14 degrees.

Not yet printed. Asked of t-artist (geometry only, no search, no layout or region figure): per end the near and far wall and distances; the sign from the generator's own side test (side_next) stated as away from or toward the near wall; the angle to the near wall before and after bends of 30.91 and 34.91 degrees; and the two limits on the first segment toward the face (wall_reach on the carried line, since no named overlap exists on 3 and 4, and the 0.305 m of a hand-over point).

Recorded as stated by t-designer before the search: the carried class on these two landings is tight (a carried run-out of roughly 0.07 to 0.2 m on paper). If the print shows the sign the other way at any end, that end cannot be carried and the class goes back to t-designer and t-director before the search, not after.

Disclosure: ordered to read the rows independently; the readings of t-qa and t-engineer2 reached me before I opened the file, so this is my own recomputation, not an independent reading. Rows, case 1: on the lane from frame 42. Frame 888: laneU 3.22646928, printed equal to length + head run, uMinusEnd -8.94069672E-08, laneSpeed 0, target 1.60000002, W held, gate Infinity, blocked 0, no links, the replayed ClimbRules.LeavesAt None, controller off, grounded 1, laneExits 0, at (-0.34, 200.05, 0). Unchanged in those columns to frame 3645 (2758 frame rows). Frames 3646 to 3655: ten error rows, area gone, onLane 1.

Arithmetic (mine): length 2.88646936 and head run 0.340000004 as singles sum in double to 3.2264693677425385; rounded to single 3.2264692783355713, exactly the stored u; u minus the double sum is -8.940696716308594e-08, the logged figure. The body is stopped at the stored end (ClimbTravel.cs line 60 forms the limit through a float parameter, line 71 stores it) and the exit test (ClimbRules.cs line 409, u at or past length + headRun) never fires. By reading, the free foot is not exposed (line 408 compares against a negation, no sum) and no other end test is exposed (ClimbTravel 73, ClimbRules 389, 390 compare stored singles; 128, 129 carry BandEpsilon and are a join test; linked ends add zero); t-engineer to confirm by grep.

Fix form proposed, NOT staged until t-qa agrees: no tolerance; one function returning the head end as a stored single, used by ClimbTravel line 60 and by LeavesAt; an EditMode sweep of lengths shown red on the code of today first; rig case 1 as the PlayMode confirmation. FACT by reading (staged set of record, Editor/Lookout/LookoutGameplaySetup.cs lines 355 to 387; repo copy has the three asset paths at lines 22 to 24): CreateTuningAssets is LoadOrCreate: an existing tuning asset is loaded untouched; class defaults are written only when the asset is missing. RULING (to t-engineer2, t-designer, t-qa, t-producer): the same repo script gains a SYNC with two scripted entries, each with a batchmode form. REPORT: every serialized field of the three assets against a fresh instance of its class (SerializedObject iteration, no typed field list); each difference printed by name with both values; writes nothing. APPLY: class defaults copied into the existing asset in place, file and GUID kept; refuses unless REPORT ran on the same asset hashes just before. EditMode test: asset = class default for every serialized field of all three assets; red first on the asset of today. ORDER: REPORT first; its list to t-qa and me; every differing field decided by name before APPLY, because tests have already run against these assets. asset_tuning.py is used for nothing of record. A retune is a change of a class default and its Range in one diff, never an asset edit. ## 127. Shade case of the contrast row: what a shaded ring receives when the Sun is turned (16:32Z, 2026-10-13; not final)

Question of t-qa. FACTS by reading: the skybox is Assets/World/BellsBend/Sky/BellsBendSky.mat on the built-in Skybox/Procedural shader (sun disk on, atmosphere 1.1, exposure 1), RenderSettings.sun the Sun light; the DRAWN sky follows the Sun direction. The ambient probe and the default reflection are computed from the sky once, at load, by Assets/Scripts/Level/BellsBendSkyInit.cs line 10 (DynamicGI.UpdateEnvironment in Awake); nothing else in Assets/Scripts regenerates them. ANSWER: turning the Sun rotation alone changes nothing a shaded ring receives; the hours are then equal and cases 1 and 2 of the row are one measurement. To apply an hour honestly the test must call DynamicGI.UpdateEnvironment after setting the rotation and wait until RenderSettings.ambientProbe has changed, recording the probe per hour; that measures a world the game never shows (nothing moves the Sun). Advice to t-qa: grade in the game own state (authored Sun, ambient as at load), which by the sun print of t-artist2 is a shade case in the built cab; report a regenerated-hour sweep as information on robustness to a re-authored Sun.

Agreed with t-qa: if no direct light reaches the ring in the built cab, the "direct light excluded" reading of the light probe bears on both cases; forecast only until the cab-interior reading on the imported tower. t-director (amend49) now also recommends the shadowless hair-and-sill part is not built; the allowance stays on file; the word of team-lead awaited. Slot 31 (first reading of t-qa, not of record): gate green 864 of 864, gate-off control red 8 of 8, MG4 (a) to (d) as wanted; RV RED as predicted; RV-c RED, unpredicted: the lock-out is a distance from the hand-over point, not a distance walked, so a body that walks away and returns to the gate is locked again; no value of laneRejoinDistance cures it. By the code a floor join already requires the input inside the join cone into the lane and a handed-over body is heading out, so the only input the lock-out ever refuses is a deliberate reversal; my candidate (i) equals a lock-out that never fires. RULED: the lock-out is retired; the lane just left is joined like any lane; the cone is what keeps an arriving player from being taken back (RV-a, RV-b); dead parts removed by name (MayRejoin, the left-lane bookkeeping where unread, RejoinAllowed and its test, the field laneRejoinDistance through the sync); spec section 2.3 point 3 amended before grading; rig RV and RV-c green at head and foot at four steps with RV-a, RV-b and a stick-rebound case green; not layout-bound, outside the inputs hash, may land after the search. ## 173. The connections sheet read by me for the plates: what is legible (19:47Z, 2026-10-13; not final)

Image read: _bmad-output/research/domain-burns-lookout-tower-2026-10-10/images/mc39-usfs-1935-bracing-connections.png (USFS Dwg T-1007-A, "7 x 7 Lookout Tower, Typical Connections", sheet 7 of 10, as reproduced in Fickes 1935; a page scan at about 1700 x 2200). MY READING, with its limits. LEG JOINT (view "Diagonal and Strut Connections to Leg Angle"): ONE elevation, face on; no section through the joint. It draws a polygonal plate on the leg, lettered "1/4 Pl.", with the main strut and two diagonal braces bolted onto it, and the note "Omit bend if L is thicker than 1/8 in" pointing at a brace end. LEGIBLE: that there is a plate at the leg joint, its thickness, and that thin braces are BENT at their ends to seat on it (so the braces lap ONTO the plate and the plate lies against the leg). NOT LEGIBLE: which side of the tower face the plate is on (inside or outside): an elevation cannot say, and I cannot tell hidden lines from visible ones reliably at this scan; no outline dimension. So "the side of the sheet" is knowable only as an ORDER (leg, then plate, then brace ends on the plate), not as inward or outward. HANGER NODE (view "Typical Connection of Diagonals, Main Struts, Stiffeners and Hangers"): two diagonals crossing a stiffener strut with a hanger below; NO PLATE is drawn; "Use 1/4 Fillers" is lettered with leaders to the laps, and again on the small strut and hanger splice below. LEGIBLE with good confidence: no gusset plate at that node on this sheet; 1/4 in fillers between the lapped members. CONSEQUENCES, for t-director and team-lead, not ruled by me: (1) at leg joints the sheet-true build is the brace ends lapping onto a plate that lies on the leg; in the model the braces are not bent and no member may be moved, so no seat of the plate reproduces it exactly; (2) at the 20 hanger nodes the sheet draws fillers, not plates; the plates there came from the reading of the Burns photos by t-level (a plate with about six bolts where the diagonals cross at a mid-panel vertical; t-qa: cannot tell): the sheet and that photo reading differ, and the model is the Burns tower. t-director suspended the corner yes at 19:47Z: all 84 are held; t-artist told. t-director (amend7): my bar of lever plus 5 kept; their "wider bar on the layout" withdrawn; after the freeze a feel problem at landing 7 is cured in game code or carried to I5. t-qa: bars (h rev 3) H10 to H14 for stop ends, free ends, cab and hatch.

As 12 rise per 8 run: 6 in of going a step, 4 ft 6 in of run for nine goings (4 ft for eight). As 8 rise per 12 run: 13.5 in a step, 10 ft 1.5 in (9 ft for eight). The bay: 7 ft 0 in at the cab (lettered on T-1002-A); by the taper of the same sheet (22 ft 0 3/4 in at the bed plates, 7 ft at the top, over 99 ft 9 in) about 8 ft at the 1st landing: MY arithmetic from sheet dimensions. So the geometry of the sheet itself excludes 8 rise per 12 run and leaves 12 rise per 8 run; the arithmetic of team-lead is confirmed. t-level: the lettering alone does not say rise or run.

My "sections as the model's" of section 181 was about the sections; the MID RAIL is not on the sheet, so it is a model addition whose tag is t-artist to state. On flight 8 it does not change the branch: the posts are inside the face steel at every station whether or not a mid rail is drawn; t-artist says how many of the 11 pairs are mid-rail pairs. Whether mid rails stay anywhere is a look and sheet-against-photo matter for t-director and team-lead.
LANDING POST (team-lead rules: priced on scratch by t-artist2 at the sheet value with nothing else changed; lands only if every post-reading row stays green; else 40 mm stays as a named departure). Second reading on file (t-level, independent): "2 x 2 x 1/8 post" left and rear, "2 x 2 x 1/8 L" right. THE ROWS THAT READ THE POST, for t-artist2: (1) G2 face-edge clearance, ten runs; (2) short walls C-1 to C-5 (the inset is derived from the post half: 0.020 becomes 0.0254; fall-edge lists; the live rows at the ten short walls); (3) the newel rule NR2 to NR5 and NR6R (post face on the rail heel line; lane-side face 0.005 outside the rail plane; the butt of the hand rail); (4) K1 on its five runs (the outward move is the half section); (5) the wall proxies whose face is taken from the innermost surface of a guard (H7, G4): every such wall, with the standing regions, reach lists and lips they bound; (6) the live-body print, all landing rows; (7) the interference row; (8) the eye rows that read guards (eye to guard, the near rectangle, landing_eye_clearance); (9) the struck-rung check TS3 on landings 3 and 4; (10) the gates (MG3: no gap to neighbours).
